# The world

The farm, the weather and the day, the save, money and jobs, animals and visitors, and drawing it all cheaply.

## Where things stand

What is true now, with the sections below to read for the detail. Keep it current: an item that changes how something stands fixes the line here as well as adding its section.

- **The far landscape is on the new farm** (a ring of low-poly ground from the skirt's edge to 3.5 km with fields, hedges, woods, farms, the lane, the A-road and the river running on, and hills on the horizon; the clear day's fog thinned to show 1.5 km): "The far landscape (#708)".
- **A View distance setting** (`viewDistance` in Graphics, 300 to 3000 m, 1500 to begin with) sets how far a clear day shows on the new farm, and every weather's fog is its share of it: "View distance (#820)".
- **The yard is dressed on the new farm** (concrete with joints, drain and puddles, the barn wall's gear, heaps, steel gates, the stackyard's bales and the yard's sound bed): "The yard dressed (#817, part a)".
- **The yard at night, the barn's roofs, the bays and the bowsers** (floodlights on the gable and a pole, a weather vane that turns into the wind, gutters and downpipes, kerbed bays, bowsers on slabs with their nozzles in holsters): "The yard's lights, the vane, the barn's trim, the bays and the bowsers (#817, part b)".
- **The farm's tracks are on the new farm** (the stone track north to the pit and south over a farm bridge, a grass boundary track round the whole farm outside the pit's fence, and 6 m headlands in the six arable fields; gravel, ruts like the green lane, preferred by the girls): "The stone tracks, the boundary track and the headlands (#689)".
- **The barn is one building ** (26 by 18 m, one pair of doors in the long south wall, six bulbs, windows, no hayloft, the weather in one place, a desk, one pair of fonts): "The barn as one building (#818)".
- **Nether Thornby builds up as you drive in on the new farm** (30 signs and the village sign on the lane and the street, three houses facing the lane, a small farm with a walled yard, the street's houses in five designs, and the pavement, sign, street furniture and car park fixes folded in): "Nether Thornby's approach and the village fixes (#819)".
- **The inspection pit is a real trench in the barn floor, crossed at floor level** (slab and drawn ground cut, a plug fills the slab until it is bought, no collider because the ground is one heightfield): "The inspection pit as a real hole (#817, part c)".
- **The farm is the one on `docs/farm-plan.md`** (#692): a bigger farm (about 68 ha) with the farmstead in the middle, the only farm since the move (an old save opens on it with its vehicles, money, the girls and records and its positions laid again: `LAYOUT_VERSION` 2). `layoutNew.js` lays the plan over the base numbers in `layout.js` and has the new ground, outline and fields, the drive with its cattle grid and the straight lane at its foot ("The drive: from the lane to the yard (#568)"), the neighbour's yard across the lane with her track, her fields and her cows ("The neighbour across the lane (#569)"), the land's lie (falling east to the valley floor, rising west 100 m to the moor's ridge) the farmstead in the middle of the farm ("Move the farmstead to the middle of the farm (#682)") and Hollow Wood in the north-west corner with its woodpile at the gate ("Move Hollow Wood to the north-west corner (#683)"), the hill side (the green lane as its own field, the stream and ford, Hill Pasture, the Sheep Field and the Wet Meadow: "Move the hill side as one (#684)"), the Old Pit in the north-east corner by the lane, fenced in post and wire, with a shut lane gate and a farm gate ("Move the gravel pit to the lane (#685)"), the jobs on their own fields ("The jobs on the new fields (#691)"), Nether Thornby across the lane with its street through to the A-road, the garage on the lane and a stone bridge over the brook ("Nether Thornby across the lane (#688)"): "The new farm's outline and fields (#681)", "The land's lie, and stubble inside its field (#813)", "Field names, the strips and the places held for later items (#814)" (each farm's names dealt from its seed and kept in its save, the margins and strips, the footpaths, troughs and power line held), "A landmark in every field (#815)" (one thing in each field, its place written once in `layout.fieldLandmarks`, eight built). Back Lane, the old single-track road round the farm's north, west and south, is built on it ("Back Lane (#686)"), and the Moor beyond its west side up to the ridge wall ("The Moor beyond Back Lane (#713)"), which you ride (flushes that bog, both grids rattle, a summer flock, wind and birds; "Riding the Moor (#821)"). Nothing is at the old numbers (`NOT_MOVED_YET` is empty, with a test; "Opening the new farm (#692)"). Place anything new on the plan first. "The farm plan and milestones (#660)" in process.md. What belongs to a building is placed from its anchor, so a building moves as one: "Placing a building's contents from the building (#661)".
- **The farm's shape.** The farmstead is in the middle: the barn (one building, its doors in the south wall onto the yard), the yard, the stackyard with the Dutch barn, the orchard, the garden with the cars beside it, and Home Ground (the old grass field: the Home Loop, its bogs, the jump, the cones, the bonfire). The drive runs east 290 m to the cattle grid and the lane; across the lane are the neighbour's yard and Nether Thornby, and the lane bends north to the A-road. North-west is Hollow Wood with the Hollow Run and the Fern Trail; north-east the Old Pit, in a field of its own. South-west is the sunk green lane with the ford and stream, the Hill Pasture (banks up to 38 degrees, the climb course, the flask rock, an electric fence), the Sheep Field and the Wet Meadow, and Brook Meadow with the river beyond. Back Lane circles the north, west and south and leads up to the Moor. Six arable fields lie between them, each with hedges, gateways and a headland. Everything is in `LAYOUT`, with pure shape modules (`fields.js`, `hill.js`, `woods.js`, `quarry.js`, `neighbourFarm.js`, `villageShape` and the rest), and tests keep each piece from changing the farm's heights. "Opening the new farm (#692)" says what went; the sections from "The east fields and the farm road (TODO 2)" to "The barn gets a second span (TODO 52)" are the record of the old farm's pieces, which the plan's sections above replace.
- **Things in the wrong place are found by one test** (`farmPlacements` in `farmPlacement.js` lists what the builders place, from their own placements, with what is not listed yet named builder by builder; five rules: gateways, water, tracks, plots, reserved places; `tests/farmPlacement.test.js` runs them over the whole farm in under a second, with what is filed listed by issue): "One test for things in the wrong place (#1187)".
- **Getting about.** A hedge is solid 1.3 m either side of its line and its leaves are kept inside that and out of its gateways ("Hedges you can't walk into (#829)"). The girls walk by gateways (`fields.route`, Dijkstra over the gaps, and round the corners of a field with a notch in it). Fields are polygons with hedges along their sides ("Fields are shapes, not boxes (#662)"). A shut gate is skipped by routes and counts as hedge. When following you, a girl waits at the hill's foot and the wood's gate rather than going in. Gates are `Gate`s that swing and refuse to shut on someone; none is saved. "Traffic on the lane, visitors and gates that open (TODO 22)", "A gate on the cattle grid (TODO 32)".
- **Ground.** Grip comes per wheel from the surface (grass, track, puddle, tarmac, stubble, snow and wet leaves as factors) times tyre pressure. Driving wears the grass into soft ground, and in rain into new bogs, which fade back over minutes. Mud carries onto the road and smears (the farm road, the public lane and the village streets all count as tarmac, #363). Frost and snow are drawn in the terrain shader and thawed by tyre tracks; snow also lies on the world's roofs, hedges, trees, props and parked vehicles by a patch on their materials (`snowCoat.js`, #95). "Mud (2026-10-05)", "The ground changes (TODO 4)", "Better frost (TODO 20)", "Snow (TODO 11)". The green lane wears three times as fast as a field and recovers at a fifth of the pace, so a wet day ruts it towards a bog and it stays rough for days; the farm save keeps its ruts and half dry out overnight ("A muddy lane that changes (#42, TODO 147)"). The hay patch's long grass takes a pressed line from every wheel that lifts again in about seven minutes ("Tracks through long grass (#63, was TODO 168)"). The hay meadow is drawn in the ground shader with instanced long grass over it, in the grass field's south-east corner ("The hay patch looks like a meadow (#549)"). Worn ground is kept in tiles that exist only where something has worn it or thawed its frost, over all the ground, fine near the farm and coarse beyond ("Wear in tiles (#666)").
- **Roads (#664).** Every road is one entry in `layout.roads` (a centreline, width, surface, markings, verges, junctions), answered by `src/world/roads.js`, through a grid index per road so a long road costs no more a lookup (`tarmacAt`, `surfaceAt`, `levelAt`, `pointsAlong`, `lanePath`, passing places) and drawn by `roadMesh.js`: the lane, the A-road and the village street are laid over the ground, the farm road is painted into it. "One road network (#664)". On the new farm the lane bends round to the A-road, which is the one way off the map in the east, and nothing is walled: a junction's give-way line or the lane's southern line fades you out and puts you 25 m back up the road, facing home. "The A-road and being turned round (#687)".
- **Weather.** Each game deals a kind (mostly sunny), then a temperature band, a wind heading with gusts, and possibly one believable turn later in the day, which the forecast board predicts correctly 80% of the time. Storms always pass. Seasons come from the farm's own calendar (365 days, dealt start date) and shift the weather odds, daylight length, ground wetness and leaf tints. Read the weather as it is now, every frame; never once at the start. "Mostly sunny (2026-10-06)", "The day's temperature (TODO 8)", "Wind (TODO 20)", "A day that changes, and the forecast board (TODO 28)", "A thunderstorm (TODO 51)", "Seasons across games (#30, was TODO 135)".
- **The day.** It starts between 08:00 and 15:30 and runs at 12 times real time, through dusk into a night that stays. The temperature falls after 14:30 and frost forms. Vehicles throw real light through a fixed pool of six spotlights, and effects with their own colour are dimmed with the daylight. The moon's phase is dealt each game. You end the day at the calendar from dusk once both girls are at the barn: a recap card, a fade, then the next day. "The day running on into dusk (TODO 33): as built", "Stars and a moon (TODO 82)", "Calling it a day (TODO 189)", "The day's story, written (#55, was TODO 160)".
- **A farm that remembers.** Two seeds. `farmSeed` deals what the farm is (fleet, plates, quirks, fault luck, the girls' quads); `seed` deals today (parking, weather, time). Vehicles keep their condition but not their position; the girls keep half their favour, their skills and any lock-in; records, ticks and the logbook carry over. It saves every 30 s and on hide. `?seed=` never saves. An old save fills in what it lacks from the deal. A save from another map (`LAYOUT_VERSION`) keeps all of this but none of the kept positions: "A layout version in the save (#663)". "A farm that remembers (TODO 15)".
- **Money and jobs.** The chalkboard deals two jobs a day from `FARM_JOBS` (muck, silage, logs, hay, spraying), plus a service or feeding job on top when one is due. Each job pays £60 into the tin. The cork board deals second-hand ads (models not on the farm this game) and a sell page. Bought and sold vehicles change the next morning's fleet. Show day is the last Sunday of each month, and judges the quad you last rode. Each is a pure state machine fed threats, and each makes its own choices, so they don't enter the girls' chooser. Lane traffic, visitors (the post, a neighbour) and the neighbours' kids on mini motos are scenery, not physics; the kids stop at the gate to watch and make the girls keener to show off. ---

The sections below are the record, oldest first. A new decision goes at the end.

## Mud (2026-10-05)

- Mud had a `mudAt` hook but made little difference: Rapier's raycast wheels count only half the drive force against grip and four wheels shared it, so tyres never lost traction; mud only cut cornering grip and puddles were plain mud. Now grip goes from 2.2 on grass to 0.7 on the track and 0.42 in puddles (new `puddleAt`), multiplied by tyre pressure.
- `src/vehicle/traction.js` (pure, tested) limits drive force to what the tyres can take. Driven wheels are treated as locked together and spin up together when you ask for more, losing some drive and a little sideways grip (15%; 35% made the yellow loop off at 11 m/s). The engine load now uses the drive fraction that really reaches the ground, so the revs flare when the wheels really spin. The yellow is rear-wheel drive (`fourWheelDrive: 0`): on the track it spins 0.8 for the first 1.5 s of a launch, its tail steps out but it's controllable at 11 m/s and needs lifting off before bends at 14. Red and blue never spin on the track and barely in puddles. Cornering on the track is about 0.5 g against 1.8 g on grass.
- Spray is a capped point buffer per quad like the smoke (clumps off each tyre, spray from spinning wheels, a burst into puddles). Mud on the quads is a per-quad shader blend over cloned materials, driven by a coat amount that builds with speed and wheelspin through mud (tyres in about 10 s, bodywork half-coated after a lap), favouring tyres, arches and low parts, wet and glossy at first and drying lighter over 4 minutes. Ruts are ring-buffered ribbons per wheel (4,000 segments a quad), fading over 150 s in mud and 40 s on grass, laid on a new lookup of the terrain mesh's own height so they sit on the drawn ground. All of it costs about 0.16 ms of CPU a frame for three quads and 6 draw calls.
- Made much muddier later (TODO 1): body build rate 0.0011 to 0.004 (half-coated after about 20 s through mud at 9 m/s, not a lap), tyres 0.014 to 0.03, puddle splash and drying doubled or slowed, tyres shed less on grass. The shader's coat now reaches up to 1.3 m (was 0.95), tops of panels catch more, and the mask is 30% more generous. Judged by the numbers and one screenshot, not by feel.
- There's no way to wash the mud off in the game yet (the debug folder can). The puddle surface is still a grey disc.

## Inside the barn (2026-10-05)

Decided with you: build the barn procedurally rather than cut into the model; the big doors are always open (no door to work); same footprint; quads under cover stay clean and warmer; in the rain the girls sit on bales inside; engines boom and echo and rain drums on the tin; only the toolbox moves, to a workbench inside (the workshop item builds on it).

- `barn.glb` is gone (and its ASSETS.md line). `src/world/barnBuild.js` draws a hollow shed from boxes: board walls (canvas texture), gable ends, a tin roof on timber ties, the doors slid back along the outside of the east wall, a concrete slab, a workbench with a pegboard and a bare bulb. Footprint is the old barn's (about 10.4 by 9.2 m outside, door 4.6 m wide and 3.2 m high, eaves 4.2 m, ridge 6.3 m), door facing the yard on the +x side. The numbers are in `LAYOUT.barn`; `barnBaleSpots`, `bench` and `toolbox` (now on the bench, `lift` 0.92) are beside it.
- Pure logic in `src/world/barn.js` (tests in `tests/barn.test.js`; tunables under "Barn" in the tuning panel): `insideBarn`, `underRoof` (the roof overhangs 0.3 m), `indoorLevel` (0 in the yard, fading in over `doorFade` 1.5 m past the threshold, so rain and light change smoothly as you walk in), `wallBoxes` (five colliders; the east wall in two halves leaves the doorway), `floorBlend` (the terrain is levelled under the barn and eases off over 2.5 m, so the floor is flat; the slab is 1 cm proud of it and not a collider).
- Rain: no streaks fall under the roof (each drop is tested against the roof's footprint, so you see the rain stop at the door). The sound: `rainHeard` makes it louder under the roof (`rainTin` 3, up to about 1.7 times as loud) and a second looped layer of sparse short rings on tin fades in with `indoor`. Light: the hemisphere light drops by 55% indoors and a warm bulb comes up (30 cd, stronger on gloomy days); the sun's shadow from the roof does the rest.
- Echo and boom: the hub has a convolver with a generated 1.6 s impulse; every quad's `SoundPath` (so engine and ride sounds) has a low shelf and a send into it, set by whether you and the sound's source are inside (`reverbWet` 0.55 both in, 0.2 one in; bass up 6 dB for an engine inside heard from inside, 40% of that for you inside and it outside).
- Under cover: `EngineModel` takes `sheltered` (the quad is under the roof). Switched off, it cools 2.5 times slower (`barnCoolFactor`) and only down to 0.25 (`barnWarmFloor`); it never warms up on its own, so a quad that spawns cold in the barn stays cold, but one you've run and put away starts much better after a long wait (900 s time constant outside: after an hour outside 0.02, in the barn 0.31). It stays clean because the floor isn't mud, so none builds; I added nothing to dry it.
- The girls: the doorway spots are gone. Two `shelter` spots with a seat sit on the bales at the back, facing the doors; `restStandPoint` now treats a shelter spot with a seat like a bale (she climbs up from behind it), and a shelter spot without one still just stands. Two bales means two girls; a third would have no place and stay out. Bales in `barnBaleSpots` are built by `buildBales`.
- Checked once headless in rain: the game loads with no errors, a quad driven into the barn counts as sheltered, both girls ran in and sat on the bales, no streaks inside, and screenshots from outside, the doorway and the back looked right (doors, roof, ties, bench with the toolbox, light). Not checked: how the echo, boom and tin drumming sound (first guesses, all tunable), the bulb's look in the dark (the seed was rain at midday), driving a quad through the door and the bales' girls getting down in the running game, the real cooling times in play, whether a girl on the bale clips the quad parked beside it, or any fine-weather use of the barn.

## The hayloft (2026-10-05)

You answered "all as recommended" to six questions, so this is what was proposed.

- Layout: a plank floor over the west end of the barn, 2.6 m above the barn floor and out to x -64.6 (4.4 m deep, the full width), clear of the bay and the bench. The eye height on it is 4.2 m, just over the tie beams (their tops are at 4.16 m), so the camera doesn't pass through them. The two bales downstairs have 2.1 m of headroom under it, enough for a girl sat on one. Numbers are in `LAYOUT.loft`; the shape and rules are in `src/world/loft.js` (pure, tests in `tests/loft.test.js`; tunables under "Hayloft" in the tuning panel).
- Climbing: look at the ladder and press `E`; you're carried up (or down) over `climbSeconds` 2.6 with no ladder physics, and can't be stuck on it. You can't climb while your hands are full. The ladder foot is just west of the bay and its top lands at the gap in the rail.
- Edge: a rail (1 m, with a collider) along the open edge in two pieces with a 0.7 m gap at the ladder. The gap is the only drop, so rather than a separate hatch hole the ladder just comes up through the gap in the rail. A step off there is the ordinary landing; there's no damage in the game.
- View: a hay door cut in the east end wall above the big doors and in the gable above it (sill 3.5 m, 1.6 m wide, with a white frame and a hoist beam). It's a gap in the header and a notch in the gable, with no glass or leaves.
- Hiding: look at the hay stack while up there and press `E`. You move to a nook behind it and your eyes drop 1 m; any of `E`, `G`, `Esc`, movement keys or `Space` gets you out (you're put back where you were). While hidden, ride offers and favour requests are paused. After `hideCallAfter` 30 s a girl calls for you ("Where have you gone?"), again every 25 s, and after `hideFoundAfter` 110 s one finds you and you come out. A girl sat up in the loft finds you after 5 s. They don't walk to look for you: whoever calls or finds just says it, from wherever she is.
- Girls: `loft0` is a rest spot of its own kind (`loft`) at a bale at the edge. On a fine day, when she decides to sit out and the loft is free and within 45 m, she picks it `girlLoftChance` 0.3 of the time (else the usual bale or fence). She walks to the ladder foot, climbs along a path through the ladder top and across the planks (about 4 s) and sits, and climbs down the same way; it's the same rest as the bales, so talking to her or rain sends her down (rain then sends her to the barn bales). `restSpots` can have `stand` and `via` points for a spot like this.
- Straw: if Jess is sat in the loft and you stand below within 2.6 m of her, after 1.5 s each second has a 0.25 chance of her tipping a handful on you, then a 90 s cooldown. About three dozen stalks fall and stay on the floor (`src/effects/strawFall.js`, cosmetic). Jess is delighted, Amy tells her off. It doesn't happen while you're hidden.
- Lines: `loftLines.js`: `loftGo`, `loftSat`, `loftCall`, `loftFound` and `loftDrop` for each girl, both banks re-recorded.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless in rain that the game loads with no errors, the climb takes you up onto the planks, the hay door shows the yard through its frame, and a girl sent to the spot climbs up and sits. Not checked: how the climb or the girl's ladder walk look in motion, hiding and being found, the straw fall, the loft in daylight, dropping off the edge at the gap, or whether the camera clips the tie beams when you look up. The girl's walk up is a straight line along the ladder with the walking animation, which will look stiff.

## The hayloft moves to the front of the barn (2026-10-06)

Jamie's request: put the loft at the front so you can actually see out of the window. It was at the back (west end), which put the hay door 8 m away across the barn.

- A loft over the doorway has to clear the big doors (3.2 m, with a header to 3.5), so the planks are now 3.5 m above the floor (were 2.6) and run from the end wall back to x -62.5. Quads still drive in and park in the bay underneath, with the joists at 3.2 m.
- Headroom: the roof comes down at the sides. At 3.5 m up, standing eye height (1.62 m) plus a margin needs the roof above 5.4 m, which holds only within 1.9 m either side of the barn's centre line (`loft.band`). So hay is stacked down both sides with tall invisible colliders (2.2 m), and you walk the middle band. The north stack is the one you hide in; the south one is for looks. A test checks the band against the roof height.
- The hay door moved up with you: sill 4.1 m, top 5.5 m (was 3.5 to 4.8), so it's at eye height from the planks, and the gable notch is taller to match. The end wall above the big doors gets a solid collider up to the top of the opening, so you can't walk or fall out.
- The two tie beams that crossed the loft at head height are left out (their rafters stay); the edge beam under the open rail does the job.
- Ladder: foot at x -63.4, z 61.3, leaning to the gap in the rail at the open (west) edge. It's inside the walkable band and clear of the bay. The girl sits on a bale at the edge facing out over the barn (not out of the window), and Jess tips straw towards the edge (`loft.strawDx`).
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless: standing at the window shows the yard through the frame, the climb lands on the planks, the ladder and edge show from the doorway. Not checked: walking the band with the collisions, the girl's climb to the new seat, hiding in the new stack, how it looks with a quad in the bay below, and the straw landing on you at the edge.

## Mostly sunny (2026-10-06)

- Weather weights are now sunny 0.70, sunny intervals 0.15, overcast 0.07, drizzle 0.03, rain 0.03, fog 0.02 (they were 0.2, 0.2, 0.25, 0.13, 0.12, 0.1). Showers within a game are unchanged; `?weather=` still forces a kind.
- The yellow's lamp shell was floating above the bars; it now sits down on the bar clamp (`DASH_PLACE.blaster`, checked once headless).


## Bale jump (TODO 13)

Reworked with Jamie before building: the front loader, the bales and the bucket ride are out; the item is the jumping. He thought the ramp and the jumping were already in the game. They weren't, so both are new.

- **The ramp** (`world/jumpRamp.js`, `LAYOUT.jumpRamp`): a convex-hull collider and a plank wedge, 7 m long, 3.2 m wide, 1.2 m high at a square lip (about 9.7 degrees). Foot at (16, -8), facing the bottom right of the field. The spot was found by scanning for the flattest ground with 22 m of run-up behind and 24 m of landing ahead and clear of the track, bogs, puddles and bales (0.38 m of height difference); a test keeps it that way.
- **Her go** (`npc/jumps.js`, pure, tested; `HerDrive.driveJump`): `JumpRun` is a small state machine (approach, run, air, landing, settle, pause) that steers by pure pursuit up the ramp's centre line and gives a target speed; `herDriving.js` turns that into pedals, with the throttle flat out until she is at speed. The quads are slow off the mark (about 0.7 m/s squared in the headless run), which is why the run-up is 22 m and `runSpeed` is 10 m/s. Hands off the pedals in the air.
- **Rating** (`rateLanding`): bail if tipped; tame under 0.25 s airborne; rough if the quad lands under 0.92 level or over 6.5 m/s down; great at 0.7 s or more; otherwise good. All in the "The jump" tuning folder.
- **Watching** (`JumpWatch`): the other girl rates a landing 4 s later if she is within 40 m, and 70% of the time has her own turn 4 s after the first girl is done. Both only if she is stood about. Lines are in `jumpLines.js` and recorded.
- **Deciding**: `boredJumpChance` (Jess 0.12, Amy 0 by default, so only when asked or after Jess), only when the 22 m run-up to 24 m beyond the lip is clear of quads, you and the other girl.
- **Also**: the tractor and trailer now start at (-41, 70.5) and (-44.5, 77.9), 7.5 m towards the gate, because Jess kept getting stuck by the barn.
- **Checked:** unit tests; headless, Jess sent to the ramp with her quad placed 27 m back: she turned for it, rode it, left the ground for 0.4 s, landed rough (the nose came down, 0.83 level), pulled up and went back for another. **Not checked:** how it looks (no screenshot came out usable), Amy's turn, the lines in play, bails, how it feels with the other quads, the moved tractor in play. She also stalled or stalled-and-restarted once on the run-up in the headless run, which I haven't looked into, and jumps are short because the quads are slow.

## Wider yard spacing

The girls kept getting stuck on the parked quads. The yard slots in `LAYOUT.spawn.quads` are now two rows 6.7 m apart with 4.4 m between quads along each (before, 3.4 m, with a second row only 3.8 m away), listed alternately so a smaller fleet uses both rows, and the random shift (`quadJitter`) drops from 2.5 m to 1.4 m so neighbours can't be nudged into each other. Nothing else is placed near the new spots. Checked: unit tests only; not by eye or by watching the girls.

## The day's temperature (TODO 8)

Settled with Jamie: bands from frosty to a warm sunny day of up to 22 °C, fixed for the game (time changes later), the effects on engines, frost on the seats, no hats, a few girl lines.

- Pure logic in `src/world/weather.js` (`pickTemperature`, `chillOf`, `bandOf`; tests in `tests/temperature.test.js`). Picked after the weather kind, from `seed ^ 0x7e3b`, so the rest of a seed's weather doesn't move. Bands: frosty -5 to 0, cold 2 to 6, mild 8 to 14, warm 16 to 22, to the nearest half degree. Odds by kind: sunny 15/20/30/35%, sunny intervals 10/25/35/30%, overcast 10/35/55/0%, drizzle and rain 0/35/65/0%, fog 35/40/25/0%. `?temp=<degrees>` forces it (`Weather.temperature`).
- Chill: 0 at 12 °C and above up to 1 at -5 °C (`Weather.chill`, `World.chill`, passed to the engine as a control). At full chill: the choke an engine needs is 1.5 times (`chillChoke` 0.5), warm-up takes 1.6 times as long (`chillWarmUp` 0.6), the battery drains twice as fast on the starter and with the key on (`chillBattery` 1) and the glow plugs take 1.5 times as long to heat (`chillGlow` 0.5). All in the engine params, so in each quad's tuning panel. Engines already started every game stone cold, so there was nothing to change there.
- The sun: `Weather.warmth` (which heats the engines) gets 0.01 per degree away from 12, so a frosty sunny day takes the edge off the sun (down to 0.83 at -5) and a warm dull day adds to it. It is unchanged at 12 °C and on a sunny day above it (already capped at 1).
- Frost: `Weather.frost` is 0 above 0.5 °C and 0.4 at freezing up to 1 at -3.5 °C or colder. Each quad's seat (`seatFrost`, `src/vehicle/seatFrost.js`) is tinted a bluish white and matte by that much, until someone sits on it, which clears it over 4 seconds. Sitting on a frosted seat (over 0.3) gets a toast and a remark from the nearest girl within 10 m. The seat material is now its own per quad (it was shared on the quads with the plain seat), and the mud coat's copy of it is what gets tinted. The tractor's seat isn't frosted.
- The girls: an early remark in `watchWeather` (once per game, as for the sky) when the day is frosty, cold or warm and not raining; a mild day says nothing about it. `temperatureLines.js`, two lines each of `weatherFrost`, `weatherCold`, `weatherWarm` and `seatFrosty` for both girls.
- Console and debug: the `Weather:` line has the temperature, band, chill and frost; the debug panel's live list has `Day temperature`.
- Not checked in the running game: how the frosted seats look, whether the chill numbers (first guesses) make cold starts a pleasant struggle or a chore, and the girls' lines. Hats weren't built (the item's hats are dropped for now), and the temperature doesn't change through the game.

## Where things start (2026-10-07)

- All three jerry cans (petrol, pre-mix, diesel) start at different ones of the five `jerryCanSpots`, picked at random with the usual jitter (`randomJerryCanSpots`, tested). The petrol can's draw is the same as before for a given seed, so it stays where it was; the other two draw after it. The pre-mix and diesel cans no longer start beside the oil bottle and the bowser, so you may have to go looking for them: the bowser and the oil bottle still stay put.

## The ground changes (TODO 4)

- `src/world/groundWear.js` (pure, tests in `tests/groundWear.test.js`, tunables under "Ground wear"): a 2 m grid over the hedged field holding a wear value 0..1. Every wheel in contact on the move adds wear (`quad.js`, so the girls' quads and yours count alike), scaled by the quad's mass, tripled by wet ground and increased by wheelspin. Reading it back at the same spot gives what was added (the deposit is normalised against the bilinear weights, or a streak would read a quarter as strong between grid points). About 25 passes of a quad softens a strip of grass.
- Wear makes the ground soft (`mudAt`, so less grip, spray, ruts and mud coat, as on the track) from 0.3 to 0.65, and a bog from 0.72 on, judged on wear times a wetness factor (0.7 dry to 1.2 soaked). Dry, nothing worn ever bogs, because the factor tops out below the threshold; in rain a bog starts from about 0.7 wear and heavy rain makes it from less. That is the "rain swells them, dry spells shrink them" rule, and it needs no second mechanism: the weather's wetness already rises over about 40 s of rain and falls over ten minutes. Wear itself fades at 0.0008 per second dry (a soft patch is grass again in about ten minutes) and a quarter of that in the rain, so a bog made on a wet day outlasts the shower.
- It gets there by `World.mudAt` and `World.bogAt`, which now take the larger of the farm's fixed values and the wear's, so grip, sinking, the girls' waypoint checks and the ride sounds all see new bogs with no other change. The girls' race route also plans round them (`World.bogHazards`, up to 24 of the deepest cells, each 3 m radius).
- Drawn in the terrain shader from an 80 by 80 texel `DataTexture` (soft in red, bog in green, refreshed twice a second while anything is worn or recovering): churned brown for soft ground, dark and glossier for a bog, with the edge broken up by the detail noise. No canvas repaint.
- Not done: the ruts are not physically deeper. The Rapier heightfield and the mesh grid are built once and the ribbons are laid on that grid, so dipping the ground would need the collider rebuilt, which I judged not worth a hitch. A new bog is a dark patch with no dip or glossy skin like the three fixed ones. The three fixed bogs don't change size with the weather, only the existing 15% deeper when wet. The yard, the drag strip and the barn are exempt; the bigger field (TODO 9) will need `fieldHalf` only, as the grid is built from it.
- Checked: unit tests, and the game loads without errors (no shader compile errors). Not checked: how it looks in play, or how fast the girls' routes wear in practice; the numbers are picked, not tuned.

## The east fields and the farm road (TODO 2)

- Agreed with you: the farm grows east only, keeping the grass field, its hedges, the south gate, the track, bogs, bales and yard where they were; north (the tree line) and south (the hill, TODO 3) are left for later. The rest of the bigger-farm idea is split into TODO 3 to 8 (the hill, buildings and start spots, the bell, a map, a cattle grid, the girls on tarmac).
- Shape (`layout.farm`, `layout.road`, `src/world/fields.js`, pure, tests in `tests/fields.test.js`): the farm is x -80 to 200, z -80 to 80 (280 by 160 m, 1.75 times the grass field). The road leaves through a gateway in the grass field's east hedge at z = 2, 16 m past the track's far bend, runs to x = 184 (4.6 m of tarmac) and ends in a hardstanding 8 m in radius. Hedges run either side of it to x = 176 with a 2.8 m grass verge (1.6 m at first: the hedge blocks and bushes then nearly closed over the tarmac in a screenshot), two 6 m gaps each side into the fields, and open ends round the hardstanding. The fields are 120 by about 77 m. Hedge lines are now a list (`fields.hedges`, each with its gaps) that `props.js` builds from, colliders in pieces of at most 20 m so they follow the ground.
- Terrain: the detailed square goes from 200 to 420 m (420 segments, still 1 m; about 177,000 vertices, from 40,000), centred, so it already covers the ground through the south gate. The ground canvas goes from 2048 to 4096 to keep about 10 pixels a metre. The rise towards the distant hills is measured from the farm's edge rather than the grass field's, which leaves every height inside the old farm, and outside it to the west, north and south, as it was. The road is laid level across, following the ground along its middle, with a 3 m shoulder. The neighbouring patchwork now starts 6 m beyond the farm's hedges in the ground texture as well as the outer ring (`patchwork.js`, shared), so the band of plain grass outside the hedges is gone; the outer ring goes out to 1000 m. No distant tree clumps within 40 m of the farm or on the ground through the south gate.
- Surfaces (`src/world/surfaces.js`, "Surfaces" folder): factors on grass. Tarmac grips 1.3 (1.15 wet, before the usual wet loss), stubble 1 dry and 0.85 wet. "Rolls freer" is the drag: rolling resistance is only a 0.4 brake impulse, so the factor (stubble 0.75, tarmac 0.6) scales the body's linear damping too, which is most of what slows a quad. Grip goes per wheel through `surfaceGrip`'s new `firm` argument (it scales the grass part, not the mud); the drag is judged at the quad's middle. Stubble wears into soft ground and bogs like grass; the road never does.
- Mud on the road (`src/world/roadMud.js`, "Road mud" folder): each tyre carries 0..1 of mud, picked up on ground over 0.3 muddy (a few metres of the track loads it), 2% wiped off per metre on grass, and on tarmac shed at 1 - e^(-d/20) a metre, which leaves under 5% after 60 m. What it sheds lands on a 0.5 m grid over the road as a smear, three times the mud shed. A smear counts as 0.75 of mud for grip (so `World.mudAt` sees it, and the ride sounds hear dirt) and is drawn in the terrain shader as brown, darker where thick. Rain washes 0.004 a second at full rain; dry, it fades over hours.
- Tyre squeal (`src/vehicle/tyreSqueal.js`, "Tyre squeal" folder; volume `tyreSquealVolume` 0.5 in the audio settings): on tarmac only, the largest of a slide (slip angle 0.08 to 0.3 rad, faded in from 2 to 7 m/s), braking (lever 0.6 to 1, at 0.7 of a slide's loudness) and wheelspin (0.2 to 0.7). The sound is noise through two narrow resonances near 900 and 1900 Hz plus two slightly detuned tones, the pitch wavering. Tarmac also rumbles more quietly than the track and has 85% fewer small bumps.
- The girls: their drives may go anywhere inside the farm's hedges (6 m in); her loops are still checked by casting at the hedges, so a loop stays in the field she's in unless a gap lines up. Wandering on foot stays in her field. Walking anywhere goes by the gateways: `fields.route` is a Dijkstra over the dozen gateway points (one each side of a gap), and `walkToward` heads for the next one. A parking drive (bringing a quad home, putting one in the row) gets the same gateway points put in front of its route. The runaway tractor's distance to the hedge is the first hedge along its heading, through the gaps (`fields.hedgeAhead`). Not done: a race or drag race called while a girl is out in the east fields doesn't route her back, so she's likely to time out and the race is called off.
- A bug found on the way: the ground-wear texture was written x-major but sampled with u along x, so worn patches were drawn mirrored across the diagonal (wear at (20, -10) showed at (-10, 20)). Grip and bogs were right; only the drawing was wrong. The grid is now a rectangle over the whole farm (140 by 80 cells) laid out row by z, and a test checks the mirror point is clear.
- Straw stalks (`buildStubble`, 45,000 instanced tufts in the drill rows) draw each face twice, once each way round, front side only: on a double-sided material their up-pointing normals flip on the back faces, and half the field's stalks came out dark in a screenshot.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load with the overlay hidden (no errors or warnings, about 3.5 s to load): views down the road from the gateway, over the fields from above and across the stubble at eye height. Not checked: riding it (how the grip, the drag and the squeal feel and sound), mud smears building up in play, the girls actually walking or driving between fields, the frame rate with the bigger terrain on a slower machine, and the 4096 ground texture's memory (about 90 MB with mipmaps).

## Two different stubble fields, and hedges that don't show through (TODO 2, follow-up)

- Jamie: the two new fields were the same, and the hedges and bushes clashed and bled through all along them (and probably always had).
- The fields, agreed: both stay stubble (the dust, TODO 9, wants that) but differ in crop, drilling and lie (`layout.stubbleFields`). North is barley: ground #a7a07c, stalks 5 to 10 cm, less saturated, 1.6 times as thick, rows and tramlines along z, tramlines every 20 m. South is wheat as before: rows along x, every 24 m. The lie (`createFieldShape` in `fields.js`, used by `terrainHeight.js`): the north field has an elliptical hollow at (140, -44), 26 by 16 m and 1.3 m deep, painted darker and greener; the south field rises 4 m from 3 m off the road to its far hedge, faded in over its first 25 m from the grass field so the hedge between them has no step (a test checks there's under 0.3 m across each hedge a metre either side, and the road stays level across). Both are gentle enough to leave the girls' driving alone (the steepest is about 6%).
- The hollow holds the wet: `GroundWear` takes a `soak(x, z)` (0..1, 1 at the bottom of the hollow) and judges a bog there on wetness times 1 + `soakGain` (1) times soak, capped at 1, so half-wet ground in the hollow bogs as if soaked. In the dry it makes no difference.
- The hedges: the hedge, bush and oak leaf materials come out of their models as alpha-blended. Three.js draws those without writing depth, and each kind is one instanced mesh whose instances it never sorts, so a hedge block or bush further away could be drawn over a nearer one, all along every hedge. The leaf texture's alpha is cut-out (half the pixels 0, half 255, 2% in between), so `cutOutLeaves` (`assets.js`, tested) makes every blended, textured material a cut-out on load: no blending, depth written, `alphaTest` 0.5 with alpha-to-coverage for soft edges under the antialiasing. A plain cut-out made distant oaks look bare, because the smaller mipmaps average the alpha below 0.5; the alpha is scaled up by a quarter per mip level in the shader (Ben Golus's fix), which brought them back.
- This is probably what TODO 16 ("Fix the hedges and bushes around the side") was about; left open until Jamie has looked.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load with the overlay hidden: the hedge along the road from the north field and the grass field's north-east corner (no blocks showing through, trees in full leaf at distance), the farm from above, and each field at eye height. Not checked: the hollow turning to bog in rain in play, riding up the south field's rise, or the alpha-to-coverage edges on a display without antialiasing.

## The hill through the south gate (TODO 3)

- Agreed with Jamie before building: the south gate is swung open and tied back against the hedge for good, an ordinary gateway for quads, the girls' routes and the runaway tractor. Tipping comes from a rider-weight toppling torque that acts only through gravity (slopes), not from a higher centre of mass, so cornering on the flat is unchanged; a girl on the back adds to it. The girls' own drives stay on the farm; following you or in convoy they come down the lane and through the ford, then pull up at the foot of the pasture and wait, with a couple of lines each; on foot they walk the lane and the bottom of the pasture; on the back they go anywhere, with a couple of lines each on a steep slope.
- Shape (`layout.hill`, `src/world/hill.js`, pure, tests in `tests/hill.test.js`): the lane runs straight south from the gate (x = -30) to z = 134 between hedges 4.6 m either side of its middle, 3.3 m tall (the farm's are 2.4). Its bed falls steadily 2.5 m to the valley floor and runs 1 m deeper again along its middle, so the banks are about 1.7 to 2 m high halfway down. A first try sank it a fixed depth and eased that out at the end, which left a hump where it came out of the cutting; now the land beside it drops down the valley side to meet the bed instead. The stream wanders 2.5 m either side of z = 140, 3.2 m across, 0.75 m deep with 0.32 m of water; the ford is 6.8 m across, 0.42 m deep with 0.18 m of water and a gravel bed, muddy (0.8) for 6 m either side. The pasture is x -96 to 34, z 134 to 203 (the detailed terrain stops at 210). Beyond the stream it climbs in six banks measured from the stream's far edge, 18.2 m between them and 19.3 m from the ford to the top with the farm's own rolling; the two big ones rise 5 and 5.5 m over 11 and 12 m, so the steepest ground is 0.74 to 0.79 (36 to 38 degrees) over a metre, against 0.39 at most anywhere on the farm (a puddle's or the track's edge). Towards the east end every bank spreads out to 2.4 times as wide, the gentle way up at 0.47 at its steepest (25 degrees); 18 m in under 60 m can't be much gentler. A little tussocky rough on top (9 cm). The hill's shape fades into the neighbouring farmland over 40 m beyond its hedges, so it rolls away into the country on the outer ring. A test checks every height on the farm is exactly as it was.
- Fields: the lane and the pasture are areas (`lane`, `hill`) with their own hedges, so `route` goes grass, gate, lane, pasture. The south gate's gap is no longer `shut` (the road's big gate is), so the runaway tractor reads it as open and can roll on down the lane. Hedge colliders are now cut short wherever the ground under them climbs more than half a metre, so they follow the hill. The neighbours' patchwork (ground texture, outer ring, distant trees) is measured from the farm and the hill together (`fields.outside`).
- Props (`src/world/hillProps.js`): the stream's water is a ribbon along it, level across. Gorse (`hill.gorseAt`: on ground steeper than about 0.3 to 0.55, in clumps) is 260 instanced clumps of seven dark lumps with the odd yellow face, each with a short cylinder collider. Bracken (`hill.brackenAt`, patches over the rough above the foot) is 4,500 plants of curved fronds, a quarter turning russet, no collider. 16,000 tussocks over the pasture and the lane's banks and middle strip. The wood is 34 oaks from the farm's models with colliders and 60 bushes under them, plus five hedgerow oaks. The gate's leaf is swung back into the field and tied to a stake with orange baler twine, with its own collider; the jerry can spot "by the field gate" moved 2.4 m west to clear it. A first look showed the water as a grey band like concrete and the gorse as yellow-green boulders (yellow vertices bled across whole faces); the water is now darker and browner, the gorse coloured face by face, darker, with 7% of faces in flower.
- Tipping (`src/vehicle/topple.js`, tested; `riderTopple` 90 kg m and `backTopple` 0.8 in the vehicle params): a torque of `load` times g times the chassis's up vector crossed with down, applied each physics step while someone's at the controls or on the back. Static tip angles for a 300 kg quad with its centre of mass 0.28 m up: 60 degrees side-on with nobody aboard, 38 ridden, 31 two up; backwards, 46 ridden. Measured headless on the steepest bank (36.5 degrees), the red quad placed side-on and left 3.5 s: on its own it stayed up (leaning 38 degrees on its springs); ridden, it rolled onto its roof; nose-up and ridden, it stayed. The kids' quad has 40 (a grown-up on it would tip it at 17 degrees with 90). The mini moto and the ATC have 0: their tuning already counts the rider, and TODO 23 is their proper model. The tractor, the UTV and the Land Rover keep 90, which hardly moves them at their weights. Cornering on the flat is unchanged; the farm's slopes (6 degrees at most) give under a fifth of the tipping push.
- The girls (`followWay` in `companionDriving.js`, `followTarget` in `herDriving.js`, tests in `tests/follow.test.js`): following you, she now heads for the next gateway on the way to you (passing it within 3 m), which also helps following into the east fields. Once you're past the foot of the hill (7 m beyond the stream's far edge, `hill.pastFoot`) she goes to a spot just through the ford and waits, says so once (`hillWait`) and follows again when you come back down. Wandering on foot in the pasture keeps to a strip just past the water by the lane. Her own drives were already kept inside the farm. Walking to you, she will still walk up the hill: stopping her short would leave her never arriving for whatever she was walking over for.
- Lines (`src/npc/hillLines.js`, recorded): `hillWait`, three each, a shout with a 60 s cooldown; `hillSteep`, three each, on the back when the quad is more than about 28 degrees off upright and moving (`voiceSteepUpY` 0.88), 20 s cooldown.
- Ground wear stops at the farm's hedges (the wear grid covers the farm only), so the hill doesn't churn up. Not done: start spots or jerry cans on the hill.
- Checked: unit tests; one headless load with the overlay hidden (no errors or warnings) with views through the gate, down the lane, at the ford, up the hill and back over the farm from the top, and the toppling above; two views again after the colour fixes. Not checked: riding it with real controls (how the climb, the wheelspin and a roll feel), a girl actually following you down the lane and waiting at the ford in play, the lines by ear, the frame rate with the extra instances, and the girls walking up the hill on foot.

Not checked: a girl actually running for either building in the rain and getting in, a quad starting in the Dutch barn's bay, riding up the heap, the girls' own drives finding their way round the new buildings (they cast for obstacles, but nothing tells them the buildings are there).

## The bell by the barn doors (TODO 5)

- Where: on a timber post 0.7 m out from the barn's north-east corner, just past where the north door leaf is slid back (`layout.bell`, x -58.1, z 65.5), with its arm out towards the yard, so it's on the way out of the barn and in sight from the yard. The bell is about 0.32 m across, hung 2 m up on a yoke with a lever and a rope down to 1.1 m. Only the post is a collider.
- Ringing: look at it and press `E` (any time, hands full or not). It's a damped pendulum (`BellSwing` in `src/world/bell.js`, pure, tests in `tests/bell.test.js`): a pull adds `kick` 0.5 rad of swing in the direction it's going, up to `maxSwing` 0.75; it dies away over `swingDecay` 1.6 s; the clapper strikes at each end of the swing until it's under `strikeBelow` 0.1 rad. One pull gives five or six strikes, each softer, over about 2.5 s. The swing period is 0.9 s, so about two strikes a second.
- Sound: made in the game, no recording (`bellStrike`). Nine partials of a cast bell (hum an octave under the 560 Hz prime, the minor-third tierce, quint, nominal and the high clang), each a pair of sines a fraction of a hertz apart so it beats, high ones dying first, plus a 4 ms tick of the clapper. Made once and played per strike at 35 to 100 per cent by strength, through a panner at the bell (`refDistance` 8 m). No barn echo, since it's outside.
- The girls: ringing starts the same recall `N` did (`recallGirls`). They hear it anywhere on the farm, as the TODO said; nothing about distance. Rung again while they're on their way, it doesn't restart the recall (which would have them say `recalled` again); it gives them the full two minutes again, and a girl not there yet may say a `bellAgain` line (0.6 chance, with her usual cooldown). One bell line each added to `recalled`, three `bellAgain` each; both banks re-recorded.
- `N` is gone from the keys and the help. "Call the girls back to the barn" stays in the tuning panel's Girls folder, calling the recall directly with no bell. Tunables under "Bell".
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless: the bell shows on its post beside the door leaf, looking at it from 1.5 m gives "Ring the bell to call the girls back", `E` starts the recall and the bell swings (the rope swings out with it) and stops after about 3 s. Not checked: how it sounds (first guesses at pitch and loudness, all tunable), the girls arriving after a bell, and whether the bell is easy to spot from across the yard (it's small from 10 m).

## The cattle grid (TODO 1)

- The grid fills the grass field's east gateway (`layout.road.grid`, `src/world/cattleGrid.js`, pure, tests in `tests/cattleGrid.test.js`): 3.2 m along the road, tubes every 0.16 m (20 of them), 0.3 m of pit beyond the tarmac each side so it is 5.2 m across inside a 10.2 m gap. Post-and-rail wings with colliders close the rest of the gap, so the grid is the way through for a quad; the girls' routes and the hedge gap are unchanged and they cross it like any other ground. The ground under it stays solid and level (the wheels ride it flat, the tubes stand 6 cm proud), so there is no new physics: it is all feel and sound.
- Feel (`VibrationModel.gridJolts`, params `gridGain` 2.5, `gridFullSpeed` 8, `gridMaxRate` 28 in the Vibration folder): a run of small knocks into the same springs as the engine's shake, at the rate a wheel meets bars (speed over pitch) but never above 28 a second, since the springs can't tell faster ones apart; the size rises with speed and with the share of wheels on the bars. Unlike the ground jolts, which only show from outside, it shakes the camera and bars when you are riding. Every vehicle gets it.
- Sound (`RideAudio`, `gridVolume` 1.2 in the audio settings): a high short knock for each knock-rate tick, the odd rattle, and the road roll louder and brighter while any wheel is on the bars.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked: how it looks (the pit, kerbs and wings), or how the shake and the knocks feel and sound at different speeds; the numbers are guesses to tune by ear.

## Dust off the stubble (TODO 3)

- Pure logic in `src/world/dust.js` (tests in `tests/dust.test.js`), the cloud itself in `src/effects/dustCloud.js`, tunables in the Dust folder. A quad's `dust` (0..1) is the stubble under it, times how dry the ground is (full at wetness 0, none from `maxWet` 0.25 up), times its speed (nothing under 3 m/s, full at 10). Only stubble raises it, so grass, mud and the road do not. Rain stops it by itself, which is why there is no separate weather rule.
- The cloud (`inCloud`) is a strip straight behind the quad, 16 m long and 1.4 m wide at the quad, widening 0.12 m a metre. It reuses the smoke plume's geometry (`inPlume`) with no wind: dust is dragged along in the quad's wake, so it sits behind it whichever way the air blows. A quad's `dustIn` is the thickest cloud it is in from another quad; the game sets it each frame (`updateDustClouds`).
- Air filter: `dustRate` 0.012 a second at a full cloud, five times `mudRate`, so about 80 s from clean to choked, against seven minutes in full mud. A quad takes the larger of its own dust and the cloud it is in, so riding fast on dry stubble clogs it anyway and following closely is no worse than leading. The usual rules hold: engine running, over 1.5 m/s.
- The girls (`followTarget` in `herDriving.js`): behind a lead with dust over `followDustLevel` 0.25 her following gap is multiplied by `followDustGap`: Amy 2.2 (about 17.6 m, just outside the 16 m cloud), Jess 0.6 (4.8 m, deep in it). It stacks with the two-stroke smoke gap. Each says `followDust` once when she first notices (again for the next cloud), and inside `dustCoughWithin` 12 m she gets `dustCough` every frame she is in, which the voice's cooldown spaces out (30 s for Amy). Amy's cough line is there for when she is stuck close; she rarely is.
- Look: up to 90 tan puffs a second at a full cloud, 1.1 m across growing to 3.5 m over about 3 s while they thin, lifted off the ground behind the quad and carrying a quarter of its speed. 260 puffs a quad. Cosmetic only.
- Left out: the girls' own dust on their way to a field (they ride the same, so it clogs their filters, but nothing reads their filters), dust on the quad's paint, and dust in the camera's view while you follow a girl in a cloud.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game: how the cloud looks (colour, density, size against the stubble), whether 80 s to choke feels right, or how Amy's 17.6 m gap copes with a fast ride. The numbers are guesses to tune.

You answered "yes, propose others" and "procedural" to the two questions. All three are still procedural, with no new assets and no extra draw calls beyond a few small instanced meshes.

- Round bales (`roundBaleGeometry` in `props.js`, test in `tests/roundBale.test.js`): the plain cylinder is now a 32 by 14 segment body with a 2.5% belly and a rim that eases in over the last 0.2 m to 12% narrower at the ends. Length and radius are unchanged, so the cylinder colliders still match. Straw bales gain loops of baler twine round the middle; silage bales get a wrap texture with overlapping film bands instead of flat black. The Dutch barn's stack shares the same geometry, so it changed too.
- Workshop bench (`barnBuild.js`): the top is five planks in three tones with hairline gaps, on front, back and end aprons, with a diagonal brace at each end and a slatted shelf. The vice has a moving jaw and a handle. A red oil can, two screw jars and a rag sit on top, and the pegboard has a grid of holes (one instanced mesh). The collider is unchanged.
- Jump ramp (`jumpRamp.js`): eight separate boards in four tones over a dark body, a cheek board down each edge and a steel angle on the lip with six bolt heads. The collider hull is unchanged, so the jump behaves as before.
- Others I'd propose, not done: the fences, the water trough and the outbuilding props are still plain boxes.
- Checked: unit tests, and once headless that the game loads with no errors. Not checked: how any of it looks in the running game, so the bale rim depth (12%), board tones and the twine lines are first guesses.

## Better frost (TODO 20)

On a freezing day (`Weather.frost`, see "The day's temperature") the frost is now on the ground as well as the seats, and looks like frost.

- Ground: the terrain shader (`terrain.js`, `addDetailLayer`) whitens the grass by `uFrost`, patchy over the field, with a glitter of crystals from the detail texture and a little less gloss. It lies half as thick on the road. Churned ground (soft mud) has none.
- Thaw: `GroundWear` keeps a `thaw` grid next to wear, set from the same tyre passes (`thawAt` param, 0.006 of wear, so about two metres of one tyre melts a cell) and sent to the shader in the wear texture's blue. Unlike wear it never recovers: frost does not regrow in a day that does not move on. Footprints do not thaw it (the girls do not trample the grid).
- Seats: the tint is kept, with a speckled bump map (`frostTexture.js`, shared) and a faint cold glow so it reads in the shade. The tractor's seat now has its own material, so it frosts too (it was left out before). Sitting on any of them still clears it over 4 seconds.
- Not measured: the look in the running game, and what the extra shader work costs the frame rate (a few texture reads on the terrain). The sun does not melt the frost, as the day does not advance; that waits for item 33.

## Ice on the puddles (TODO 74)

- `world/puddleIce.js` (pure, tested) keeps a thickness (0..1) and a damage figure per puddle. Below `freezeBelow` (0 °C) it grows towards a target of 1 at 3 °C of frost, taking `freezeSeconds` (40) from open water; above `meltAbove` (1 °C) it melts over `meltSeconds` (90), faster in rain. A game starts with the puddles as a night at that temperature would leave them (`settle`), so a frosty morning starts frozen. Under `skin` (0.15) it is a film and doesn't count as ice.
- Grip: `coverAt` gives the share of the water that is frozen, the same shape as `puddleAt`. The wheel blends its grip towards `gripSlip` (0.14, against 0.3 to 0.42 for the puddles) by that share, and its puddle value is multiplied by one minus the cover, so a quad on ice takes no water up the intake, gets no wet brakes and throws no splash. Nothing else about the wheel changed.
- Breaking: each wheel on ice adds damage of `load * (1 + 0.12 * speed) / (strength * thickness)` a second (`strength` 1800 N). A cracked sound at 0.4 damage, broken at 1. By the arithmetic in the test a quad crossing at walking pace cracks it and a faster one goes through; full-thickness ice survives a gentle crossing for a second or two of wheel load. A broken puddle is open for `openSeconds` (90) before it may freeze again, so you see the hole you made.
- Looks: each puddle has its own material now (`PuddleIceView`): dark water to pale glossy ice with the thickness, and a white web of cracks (a canvas texture, made when the first one cracks) from the crack point. Sound: `crack` and `break` events come back from `Quad.takeIceHits()` into the ride sounds, using the existing crunch and splash clips pitched up, so no new recordings.
- Not done: the girls and you on foot don't crack it or slip on it, and the girls' own driving still gives the puddles a wide berth as before. Not checked: how the ice looks or how the numbers feel in play.

## The day running on into dusk (TODO 33): what Jamie settled

Asked before building, 11 October 2026:

- The game starts at a different time each game, morning or afternoon, and the day runs on from there through dusk into night, which then stays. A clock and a little weather station (a thermometer and a barometer) on the barn wall show the time and how it is.
- Night is dark enough that headlights matter, with the farm still faintly there under a blue night light. Stars and a moon came later (item 82, below).
- The vehicles throw real light, not only the faked beams.
- The temperature falls from the afternoon into the night by a random amount (about 6 degrees), and frost forms on the ground and the seats if it gets cold enough; a morning start is colder and thaws.
- As it gets dark the girls say they want to go in, and once it is dark the barn doorway and the cabs pull at them, through the chooser; you can still ask them out. Nothing ends the game.
- Item 121 (lights to work by), next: she brings a vehicle round to light what you are working at, and most of the time moves over to the passenger side once it's there. Ask her from her wheel, or Amy does it unasked, through the chooser like everything else she decides.

## The day running on into dusk (TODO 33): as built

- `src/world/daylight.js` (pure, tested): the time of day starts between 08:00 and 15:30 (from the seed; `?time=` sets it) and runs at 12 times real time, so a morning start reaches dusk in about 40 minutes and a late one in about 10. An October day: sunrise 07:15, sunset 17:15, the sun 29 degrees up at noon in the south (north is -z, so east +x), crossing from east to west, so the shadows move round. Dark (the sun 7 degrees under) at about 17:55. After that the sun stays down: no second morning, however long you play.
- The sky (`skyAt`, applied in world.js on top of the weather's): golden as the sun gets low (less on a cloudy day), then dark blue. At night the sun's light is replaced by a faint blue light from high up (0.35) and the sky's fill drops to 0.14 of the day's; the baked reflections dim with it. The first night looked too dark to see Amy at 0.07 and 0.12, so both went up.
- The temperature: the weather's picked temperature is now the day's warmest, at 14:30; it is coldest at dawn and falls by 4 to 8 degrees (from the seed) over the nine hours after the peak, most of it early. So morning starts are colder than before, and frosty days colder still at night. `?temp=` and the console set the temperature as it is now (the peak moves to match). The sun's warmth (`Weather.warmth`, which the engines' heat and the girls' cold read) goes with the sun, so the night feels cold to them as well.
- Frost both ways: the ground frost already followed `Weather.frost`; seats now creep towards it, forming over 240 s on a seat nobody is on and thawing over 150 s (`frostAfter` with the day's frost). Tyre tracks through ground frost still never refreeze.
- Real light: a fixed pool of six spotlights (`effects/vehicleLights.js`), given each frame to the lit vehicles that matter (the one you're on, then the one lighting the way for you, item 121, then the nearest), each from between that vehicle's lamps along its beam, as bright and coloured as its lamps (`headlightLevel`, so revs, flicker, a weak battery and the UTV's main beam all show). A fixed count, because changing the number of lights recompiles every shader; six because more costs every pixel. 12 per cent strength by day. No shadows from them. Loading still takes 1.67 s to Ready (measured).
- The barn's bulb stays on after dark, so the barn glows from outside.
- Smoke, steam, dust, mud spray, the yard haze and the rain are drawn with their own fixed light, so they're dimmed with the daylight; at night they glowed white.
- The trees sparkled at night: the leaves' soft cut-out edges let a little of what's behind the canvas through (Chrome on Metal, though the canvas is opaque), and behind it was the page's light grey. The canvas's background now follows the sky's horizon colour, which is what an edge should blend to anyway.
- The weather station (`world/weatherStation.js`): a board on the north wall past the door end of the bench, at eye height, with a clock, a thermometer (-20 to 40) and a barometer (Stormy to Very dry, from the weather, falling ahead of rain), drawn on canvases redrawn only when they move on. The dashes' clocks read the same time; their flashing and cycling still go by real seconds.
- The girls: `gettingDark` once as the light goes, `darkGoIn` now and then once it's dark and they're out in it (not in the barn or a cab), and going in is an offer (`goIn` in places.js: warmth 0.7, rest 0.5) to the barn doorway once it's 60 per cent dark; once in, they stay in while it's dark. The cold at night raises their warmth need on its own, so the Land Rover with the heater on (`warmCab`) competes. You can still ask them out.
- Debug: "time of day" slider and "the clock" on the Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm"; `weather.time`, `weather.clock` and `weather.darkness` in the console; "Time of day" tuning under world and "Vehicle lights" under driving.
- Checked headless: dusk at 17:12 (golden sky), night at 19:00 (dark, Amy just visible), a running red quad's real light pool on the grass at night, the weather station, and 25 s after dark with the girls free and no errors. Not checked: how it feels to ride the hill in the dark, the frame rate with all six lights lit, the morning frost thawing in the running game, or the girls actually going in (they chose a start and a drive in the 25 s).

## Stars and a moon (TODO 82)

- `src/world/night.js` (pure, tested): `moonAt(hour, phase)`, `starLevel`, `moonDisc`, `moonlightFactor`. The moon keeps the sun's arc, twelve hours up, rising in the east and setting in the west, but its rise slips by 24 hours times its phase: a full moon (0.5) rises at 18:00, is highest (0.75 rad, `moonHigh`) at midnight and sets at 06:00; a new one is up with the sun and never seen. The game runs a few hours past dusk, so a full moon is the one that matters, and it is also the one that comes up as the sun goes.
- Each game draws its own phase (`moonPhase` in "Time of day", the third draw from the daylight's random, after the start and the fall so those stay as they were). Not tied to the farm's day count: one night a game, and a phase that crept on by a twenty-ninth of a month a day would hardly be noticed. Settable in the Tune tab.
- Drawn in the sky shader (`sky.js`): a grid of star cells over the sphere, a tenth with a star, of varying brightness and a faint twinkle, fading near the horizon; the moon as a disc seen face on, shaded by phase (the terminator from the sun's side), with a halo. It is 0.035 rad across, about eight times the real moon, because the real size is a speck at this screen size. Both are drawn before the clouds, so cloud covers them. Stars vanish under thick cloud (`starLevel`) and a bright moon washes some out.
- The light: the night's blue light from above was a fixed 0.35 (`moonLight`); it is now 0.7 of that as a floor (starlight, sky glow) and up to 0.9 more (`moonBoost`) from a bright moon that is up, less under cloud. A full moon high in a clear sky gives 0.35 x 1.6 = 0.56. The light's direction stays high and fixed, so shadows don't swing about; only its strength follows the moon.
- Not done: the moon in the baked reflections (the environment map is built by day), and a moon-shadow direction. Not checked running: how the sky looks, the disc's size, or the star density on screen; unit tests only.

## Sheep on the hill pasture (TODO 9)

- Agreed with Jamie before building: only the flock this time. The gate that opens and shuts, escapes onto the lane and the road, the girls herding and the dog are three follow-up items (180 to 182). Sheep are kinematic, with no Rapier bodies: you can't hit or hurt one, they just move off. They look procedural but must look reasonable; Jamie may ask for better ones. Bleats are a CC0 recording, not synthesised.
- The flock (`src/world/flock.js`, pure, tests in `tests/flock.test.js`): nine sheep, each grazing (head down, still), walking to a patch 2 to 7 m off, or running. A graze lasts 5 to 20 s. A walk's goal is pulled back towards the flock's middle the further she is out (`cohesion`, `spread`), and sheep keep 1.3 m apart. Anything (vehicle, girl, the player) closer than `fleeRadius` 9 m plus 1.3 m per m/s of its speed, to at most 28 m, makes her run directly away (with a little pull towards the others) for 3 s after it stops mattering, and a sheep within 6 m of one that bolts bolts too half the time. A step onto ground she may not stand on is refused: a walker stops to graze, a runner turns about.
- Where they may stand (`setUpFlock` in game.js): in the pasture, 3 m in from the hedges, at least 9 m past the stream's far edge (the girls' "foot"), out of the wood, the water and thick gorse, and where the climb's slope is under 0.45 (the steepest banks are 0.74). `findFlockSpot` scores the grid by how much allowed ground is round it and picks one of the best by the game's seed, so each game starts them in a different roomy spot, and `?seed=` repeats it.
- Bodies (`src/world/flockView.js`): three instanced meshes (lumpy low-poly wool, a dark head hung from the neck that dips to graze, legs), laid on the drawn ground and tilted to the slope, rocking and bobbing as she steps. Shadows on. A first look had them too white with long thin legs; the wool is now creamier and greyer and the legs shorter and thicker. Still faceted, with no leg animation.
- Sound (`src/audio/sheepAudio.js`): `sheep-baa.wav` loads the first time a sheep within 130 m bleats, and plays from the sheep through a panner at each sheep's own rate (0.82 to 1.22), duller with distance, louder and a touch higher when frightened. A calm sheep calls every 12 to 40 s, a frightened one every few seconds. Credited in ASSETS.md. Not listened to.
- Debug: the "Sheep" tuning group (World, weather and sound). No actions or console paths beyond that.
- Checked: unit tests and two headless views of the flock on the pasture. Not checked: how it sounds, the frame rate with them, and riding into them.

Patches off the stubble are wasted, with a toast. Stood still with the tub right up, the whole load goes in one heap and the job has to be started again.

With muck in the tub `T` now tilts it to `spreadTipTo` (0.4, between the spreading threshold and the slide) and holds there (`UtvBed.cap`); `T` again lowers it and `Shift`+`T` tips it right up. Also the chalkboard hung inside the door's white battens, which stand 0.11 m proud: it now hangs 0.16 m out on a slate backing.

## Snow (TODO 11)

A weather kind, `snow` (weight 0.06, so about one game in sixteen; the temperature is rolled only from the frosty and cold bands, 60/40). Jamie settled the scope: the roll, falling snow, ground and seat cover, tracks that stay, grip and the cold; snowballs are left for a follow-up.

- **Weather** (`weather.js`): `snow` is how hard it falls now (flurries, eased towards a target re-rolled every spell, like rain) and `cover` is how far the ground is under snow. A snowy game starts at `SNOW_AT_START` 0.45; cover grows at `snow * 0.9 / 150` a second, so about three minutes of heavy snow covers it. It melts above 3 degrees (full cover goes in about 400 s at 8 degrees) and with rain (`rain / 300` a second). Nothing melts it on a frosty day, which is the point. `seatFrost` is the larger of the frost and 0.85 of the cover; the game feeds that to the quads' seats instead of `frost`, so the existing seat frost (sat on to clear, forming again) does the seats.
- **Ground** (`terrain.js`): one more block in the same shader as the frost, `uSnow`. Coverage is patchy at low cover and joins up as it grows. It is thinned by the wear map's blue (the tyre lines, the same as frost) and mostly removed where the ground is churned, so tracks show as the grass and mud beneath. Roofs, hedges, trees, props, the outer ring and parked vehicles came later, by a shader patch on their own materials ("Snow on everything that faces up (#95)").
- **Falling snow** (`effects/snow.js`): 1800 points in a cylinder round the camera, swaying and wind-slanted, none under roofs; tinted down at night in `updateWeather`.
- **Grip** (`surfaces.js`): `snowGrip` 0.62 and `snowRoll` 1.25 at full cover, scaled by cover, on top of tarmac and stubble (not a new surface type, since cover varies and sits on all of them). In the Tune tab's surface group.
- **Girls:** no new lines (they would need recording). A snowy day reads as frosty or cold to them, so they warm their hands and remark on the cold as before.
- **Debug:** console `weather.snow`, `weather.snowCover`, `weather.kind snow`; `?weather=snow`. Barometer reads 0.4 for it.
- **Not checked in the running game:** only unit tests. Not seen: how the white ground and the flakes look, whether 0.62 grip feels right, and the shader cost.
