## Hill climb and the records board (TODO 14)

Agreed with Jamie before building: you and the girls both climb; cones mark it like the drag strip; and the chalkboard grows to hold every record (lap, drag, climb and longest jump), not just the climb. The rest as recommended: scoring kept for the game only (it joins the farm that remembers, item 15), per-quad bests, the girls choosing to climb as an offer, falling out not fed from it, real traction and weather left to decide the times.

- **The course** (`layout.hill.climb`, `npc/hillClimb.js`, pure, tested): four gates at `points`, each `gateHalf` 7 m either side of its point and across the way to the next, marked by cones (`buildClimbCones` in `startLine.js`: two a side at the start and finish, one a side between). The points run up the gentler ground in the east of the pasture (steepest 0.44 measured with the terrain function, against 0.7 up the west banks), so the girls can drive it; a test checks the gates are in the pasture, out of the wood and the water, and no step is steeper than 0.6. The start is 4 m clear of the stream's far edge, so the girls' staging point is not in the water.
- **The run** (`ClimbRun`): the clock starts when a step crosses the start gate forward between its cones, then each later gate has to be crossed in order (a step that goes round outside the cones, backwards, or through a later gate first counts for nothing), and the last stops it. Out after `outAfter` 10 s off the quad or stalled (a stall cured sooner is forgiven), or `timeout` 150 s. You have to ask first (`Enter` within `nearStart` 25 m of the start, `armFor` 90 s), so riding past the cones is not a run; asking is in `askForRace` next to the lap and drag cases.
- **The girls** (`HillClimb.offers` and `take`, the chooser's `climb` offer): after `startAfter` 150 s, `cooldown` 360 s, not in the dark (`darkness` above 0.6), not while a race, a drag race or a recall is on, and not while the other is climbing. Jess `weightJess` 1.6, Amy `weightAmy` 0.7. She drives HerDrive's race mode: down the lane and across the ford (the fields' gateways, then a point 9 m past the stream on the ford's line, or she would cut the stream away from the ford), a staging point, a grid point `gridBack` 3 m behind the cones, a wait of `leaveAfter` 2.5 s, then through the gates and 8 m past the last. The same `ClimbRun` times her, from her quad's position, so her time and yours are comparable. She is out if her quad stalls, she stops doing anything about it for 4 s (as in the races), or she never reaches the start in `gatherTimeout` 120 s (called off, no event to the player).
- **What is said** (`hillClimbLines.js`, six lines each): `climbOff` as she sets off, `climbDone`, `climbRecord`, `climbOut`; and for your run `climbYou` and `climbYouRecord`, said by whichever girl is stood near the top (within 40 m, free to remark). When you take the record from the other girl she says `climbYouRecord` too, 2.5 s later. Lines carry no times (they are recorded ahead).
- **Records** (`world/records.js`, pure, tested): `Records.submit(kind, who, value, quad)` keeps the overall best and the best per quad for `lap`, `drag`, `climb` (lower) and `jump` (higher); a tie is not a record. Lap and drag times are the race clock from the go to the finish for any racer, `noteRecord` in game.js feeds them from the existing finish events. The longest jump is `JumpMeasure` (take-off to landing along the ground, at least `jumpMinAir` 0.45 s in the air and `jumpMinDistance` 4 m), for whoever is on any quad, you or a girl driving, so the ramp is not the only way to a record. You get a toast for a jump and for any record you set; the girls' records are only on the board.
- **The board.** The jobs board keeps its leaf; the records board hangs on the other leaf past the doorway (same backing and frame, `hangBoard` in `jobBoard.js`). Looking at either and pressing `E` opens the same card, which now lists the records under the jobs. The boards redraw when a record is set.
- **Bucket list:** King of the hill (a timed climb), On the board (set a record).
- **Debug panel and console:** "Hill climb" in the Tune tab under the girls' things, "Records" under items; each girl has a "% climbs the hill against the clock" action (`girl <name> climb`); the Girls tab can take you to the start and clear the records, and shows the climb's phase and the records; `farm.records` and `farm.climb` in the console.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and a headless run (past the overlay) of Jess sent up the hill: she went down the lane, over the ford, waited on the grid, set off and passed the first gates, the clock running only from the cones. She was slow on the banks (about 1.2 m/s over the first gates). The finish handling and the board card were driven by calling the game's own methods (a record on the board, the card's text), without errors. **Not checked:** a full girls' climb to the finish (the run was cut at about 35 s of climbing), your own run by hand, how the cones and the second board look, whether the girl's route needs help on the gorse in the east, and the new lines in the girls' voices (recorded in the background).

- **Follow-up (Jamie, after trying it):** the cones were hard to read as a course (gates 16 m across, small, and two a side close together at the ends). Now `gateHalf` is 4.5 m, the cones are 1.8 times the lap's size, a checkpoint is one cone either side, and the start and the finish are two such pairs 3 m apart along the course to ride through like a gateway. The crossing test is unchanged (the middle of the gate). And the two chalkboards moved inside the barn, side by side on the back wall (the jobs one 2 m south of the barn's middle, the records one 2 m north, 1.75 m up), and are 1.3 m tall instead of 1 m, because four records did not fit on the old slate. Not looked at in the running game: where they sit against the barn find quad parked at the back, and whether the cones now read.

## A farm that remembers (TODO 15)

Agreed with Jamie before building:

1. The same vehicles are on the farm every game. "New farm" deals a fresh pool. Vehicles arriving or being sold between games is left for a later item.
2. A vehicle keeps its condition and not where it was. Kept: hours, miles, engine wear, any fault left unfixed, the toolbox tuning, tyre pressures and wear, the sump, the fuel and its oil mix, the coolant, its quirks and the barn find's progress. Overnight the battery drains a little (flat if the key was left on), mud dries to about half and wet brakes dry. Dealt fresh each day: where everything is parked, the weather, the temperature, the time of day, and which faults start in play.
3. The girls: favour carries at half strength; a falling-out not made up carries as friction; what you taught them carries in full; romance carries once she is locked in, and so does a betrayal (trips round the back that hadn't got that far start again). Grudges, pranks and moods don't carry.
4. The bucket list keeps its ticks (still showing only what today's fleet makes possible). The records board keeps its bests, with a mark on those set today. The logbook keeps its last 40 entries, by day, and stops inventing "Yesterday" once there is a real yesterday.
5. Each load is the next day ("Day N" on the start screen). It saves to the browser every 30 seconds and when the tab is closed or hidden. A save from an older build fills in what it lacks from the deal rather than being thrown away.
6. `?seed=N` ignores the save and never writes it, so a seeded game repeats exactly. The console's `save` shows the save and `save load <json>` loads one (on the next reload); "Wipe the farm" is in the Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm".
7. The start screen has "Continue" and "New farm" when a save exists (New farm asks first, as it wipes the save); with no save, clicking to play starts a new farm, saved as Day 1.

How it was built:

- **Two seeds.** `this.seed` is today's (random each load, or `?seed=`); `this.farmSeed` is the farm's, saved with it, and is the first day's seed. What the farm *is* comes from the farm seed's streams: the fleet (or the saved list of ids, in order; if a saved id has gone from the game the fleet is dealt again from the farm seed), plates, top revs, the pods' first places, quirks, each quad's fault luck, the oil burner, sticky floats, the coolant leak, the worn gearbox, the girls' quads and their names for them, the barn find, and the tractor's, UTV's and Land Rovers' own streams. On a new farm the two seeds are the same, so a seed deals exactly what it did before. Everything else stays on today's seed. The quads' idles are on the main stream after the spawns, so a remembered quad's idle is still drawn (keeping the stream in step) and the saved one used instead.
- **What a vehicle keeps** (`src/farm/farmState.js`, pure, tested with the real pure parts as a stand-in unit): a named list of engine fields that are condition rather than running state (`ENGINE_KEPT`: wear, fouling, scuffing, seized, silencer, running-in, scar, coolant and its leak, boiled, diesel soak, big bore and its fit, battery), roughness (a service lowers it), hours and miles, the air filter, the fuel (litres, tap, oil, diesel, water, the sediment bowl), the faults' levels, started flags, caps and which tyre, the tyres' psi, brand and tread, the sump, the stiff tap, the mud and the toolbox settings. Restoring only writes fields the save has and that are numbers or flags, so an older save fills in what it lacks from the deal. The settings are put back the way the history's tinkering always was (`captureStock` then `applyTune`, kept as `unit.leftovers` for the toolbox), and `applyHistory` skips its faults, wear and tinkering for a remembered quad so nothing is applied twice; the history the girls know is then given the saved faults.
- **When it saves:** on the first unpaused frame (so loading and leaving without playing doesn't use up a day), every `saveEvery` 30 s, and on `pagehide` and the tab going hidden. Never on a seeded game, and never again after "Wipe the farm" or `save load` (so this game doesn't write over it). Restoring is in two steps: vehicles before the start report (`restoreVehicles`), and the girls, bucket list and records once everything is built (`restoreDay`); the logbook restores itself in `Notebook`.
- **Start screen:** the game behind the card is already the next day, so "Continue" is just a click on the card; "New farm" confirms, wipes and reloads the page. The buttons go once you've started (the card's `started` class).
- **The girls:** `favourKept` 0.5; friction carries at `frictionKept` 0.5, or at `frictionFallenOut` 6 if they were fallen out (8 falls out); skills in full; a lock-in carries (favour locked at `lockScore` again); a betrayal sets `nextInvite` to `betrayedCooldown` the next morning only (`Romance.betray` now marks `betrayed`).
- **Records and the logbook** carry the day they were set or written. The board adds "(today)" from day 2 on. The logbook shows today's entries by time, yesterday's as "Yesterday", older ones as "Day 3", and the new farm's dealt entries (from before day 1) as "Before"; it stops dealing those once there's a save. Entries now keep what they were (`what`), so Amy still knows a fault she wrote down that is still on the quad (the `wontRide` refusal).
- **Checked:** unit tests (`tests/farm.test.js`, the console's `save` in `tests/consoleShell.test.js`), and one headless run past the overlay: day 1 had no buttons; after playing, setting hours, wear, a belt fault, mud, Amy's favour, a climb record and a bucket-list tick and reloading, the card said "Continue: day 2" and every one came back as expected (mud and favour halved, the fleet the same), with no page errors. **Not checked:** the "New farm" confirm and reload by hand, the save on closing the tab, a remembered barn find part-way through, pods moved and then remembered, a long-running farm over many days, and how a saved game from this build reads into a later one.

## The barn calendar (2026-10-11)

- Asked for so you can tell which day you are on once loaded in (the start screen says it only before). It hangs on the barn's back wall at the middle of the boards' height, halfway between the jobs and records boards (`WallCalendar` in `world/wallCalendar.js`; the boards are 2.5 m apart edge to edge and it is 0.9 m wide). Nothing to press: you read it from where you stand.
- A page is `DAYS_PER_PAGE` 30 days, laid out seven across from a Monday, so it is the farm's own calendar rather than the real one. It starts in April and goes round twelve months, then says "year 2". Past days get a red cross, today a circle on a yellow highlight, and the rest are plain. `calendarPage(day)` is pure and tested; the canvas is drawn once at build, since the day cannot change while the game is loaded.
- The picture is "Miss Mudguard", a clothed 1950s garage-calendar pin-up (dungarees, headscarf, a wink and a spanner) in the cheeky style of the ones in every workshop, drawn on the canvas, so no asset to credit. The first version was a sheep in sunglasses; Jamie wanted something more garage. `PICTURES` holds one drawing per page, chosen by page, so more are a new function and a line in the list. April is arbitrary and fixed: the game has no seasons, so nothing ties the month to the weather, and every farm starts there.
- Not done: nothing is written on it (birthdays, a job due), and there is no close view. Not looked at in the game, only unit tested, so the sheep's drawing and the calendar's height on the wall want a glance.

## Drawing less (frame time) (2026-10-12)

Jamie: the game was working his M4 Max MacBook Pro hard, and more is coming. Measured first, headless at 1728 by 1117 points at Retina 2x (the 16" default), with `--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit` so the frame rate shows the real cost (`.scratch/pp/perf.mjs`, `tris.mjs`, `ab2.mjs`; headless fps drifts by a fifth over a run, so each change was alternated with the baseline three times).

- **What it was.** The game's own work was small: about 2 ms a frame for the girls, the vehicles and the physics, and 6.7 ms including handing the frame to the GPU. The GPU was the problem: 7.6 million triangles and 2,100 draw calls a frame at 3200 by 2000 pixels with 4x antialiasing. Uncapped it managed about 80 fps (12 ms), and a ProMotion screen asks for 120, so the laptop ran flat out and still missed. About 6.5 million of the triangles were ground cover and trees: tussocks 2.3M (16,000 of them, 144 triangles each), stubble 1.4M, grass 0.6M (90,000 clumps), bracken 0.3M, hedges and trees 1.2M. Each was one InstancedMesh spanning the farm, which three.js culls as a whole, so every stalk was drawn every frame, behind you or half a kilometre away. Each of these was worth about 2 ms: rendering at 1.5x instead of 2x (2.4), no ground cover (2.0), no instanced trees and hedges (2.0), no shadow pass (1.7). Small vehicle parts not casting shadows saved nothing. The engine sound worklets never sleep, but one renders 27 s of sound in 0.7 s, so a dozen cost little and were left.
- **Tiles.** Everything scattered in numbers is split into square tiles of ground, one InstancedMesh per tile with its own bounding sphere (`src/world/tiles.js`, pure and tested; `tiledInstances.js` builds them). The renderer leaves out tiles out of view, and out of the sun's view in the shadow pass. Ground cover uses 30 m tiles (`COVER_TILE`), hedges, trees, bushes and gorse 60 m (`MODEL_TILE`, through `instanceModel`, so every model scatter gets it). The layout is unchanged: the same random draws in the same order, so a seed lays out the same farm.
- **Draw distance.** Grass and stubble stop at 70 m (`coverDistance`), tussocks and bracken at 90 m (`roughDistance`): at eye height that is just under the horizon line, where the ground's own colour and the haze take over, and a side-by-side screenshot across the grass field with and without it couldn't be told apart. A tile goes when its nearest instance is beyond the distance (`DrawDistance`, updated by `World.update` from the camera).
- **60 fps cap.** `maxFps` 60: the physics already steps at 60 Hz, so 120 fps only added interpolated frames for twice the work. A schedule (`nextFrameAt`), not a gap since the last frame, so it holds 60 on a 120 Hz, 144 Hz or unthrottled screen (a gap with a jitter allowance let 80 through unthrottled). A skipped tick does nothing: no time passes and keys wait for the next frame. 0 lifts it.
- **Render scale 1.5.** `renderScale` caps the pixel ratio (it was the screen's, up to 2). With the antialiasing on, the difference is hard to see on a Retina screen.
- **Paused.** Behind the start screen and the pause card nothing moves, so the farm is drawn when the pause starts or the window changes, and twice a second in case something finished loading, rather than every tick.
- **Result.** Uncapped, about 175 fps (5.4 ms a frame) from about 80 (12 ms); 2.8 million triangles from 7.6 at the spawn view (draw calls about the same, 2,200: more tiles, fewer of them drawn). Capped, 60 frames drawn a second and the main thread about 30% busy, from 42%. "Graphics" in the Tune tab (World) and `tune.graphics` in the console set all four; the console's `perf` gives the frame rate, the game's own time a frame (`FrameStats`) and the draw calls and triangles.
- **Keeping it there.** Anything new scattered in numbers goes through `tiledInstances` or `instanceModel`, never one InstancedMesh for the lot, and small ground cover registers with the draw distance. After adding to the world, run `node .scratch/pp/perf.mjs` (dev server on 5180), which checks the spawn view against a budget. Not done, and measured after the tiles: the whole shadow pass is about 1.5 ms of the 5.3; the vehicles' shadows are 730 of its draw calls (about 110 casting parts each) and 0.5 ms; hiding every hedge saves 0.06 ms, so a simpler hedge model isn't worth it. If the budget gets tight, the vehicles' shadows are next: a few invisible boxes per vehicle that only the sun's shadow camera sees, standing in for the parts, rather than merging the parts (many move, change colour or get fitted and taken off).

## Wind (TODO 20)

- `src/world/wind.js` (pure, tests in `tests/wind.test.js`) keeps the day's wind. The weather kind's `wind` is still how windy the day is (1 on overcast, 0.3 foggy, 1.5 raining); the new part is a heading, dealt from `createRandom(seed ^ 0x817d)` so `?seed=` repeats it, which veers up to `veer` 0.25 rad round the day's over a minute or two, and gusts. A gust comes on over a fifth of `gustSeconds` 5 and dies away slowly, about every `gustEvery` 12 s; strength is the day's wind times `1 + gustiness * (1.5 * gust - 0.3)`, so between gusts it is a touch under the day's and at a peak about 1.7 times it. At strength 1 with no gust the smoke drifts at the old fixed breeze (`drift` 0.76 m/s), so nothing about calm days changed.
- Smoke, dust, rain and snow all read `wind.x`/`wind.z` now (the smoke's `windX`/`windZ` are set from it each frame, so editing them in the panel does nothing, as before). Dust puffs drift at 2.5 times the wind as they age. Haze and barn fumes still use the weather's scalar wind: gusts would only make their levels jitter.
- Hedges and trees lean in the vertex shader (`src/effects/windSway.js`): the shared materials are patched with `onBeforeCompile`, the lean is along the wind in world space whatever way the instance is turned (`inverse(mat3(instanceMatrix))`), grows with height squared and rocks out of step from place to place. Nothing on the CPU. `swayHedge` 0.14 m and `swayTree` 0.6 m at the top in an ordinary wind. Not swayed: the shadows (the depth pass uses its own material), grass, crops and the distant tree clumps.
- The hay door's shutter (`Shutter` in wind.js, mesh in `barnBuild.js`) is hooked back against the outside wall so the loft keeps its view. The wind blowing out of the door (east) past 0.5 lifts it; a spring pulls it back and it bangs on the wall hook (a damped spring with a bounce of 0.35, `bangSpeed` 0.8 rad/s to count). Wind into the door holds it against the wall. A bang is a synthesised slap (`audio/doorAudio.js`, positional at the door) and a `bang` notice at the door, so the girls within 20 m flinch through the existing rule; at most one every 3 s. Only the hay door has a shutter: the big doors slide and stay open.
- Loose hay: a gust of at least `hayWind` 1.25 blowing out of the hay door sends a second `StrawFall` of stalks out and downwind, at most once in `hayEvery` 90 s. Cosmetic.
- Debug: "Wind" in the Tune tab (World, weather and sound); console `weather.windHeading` (degrees, settable), `weather.windNow`, `weather.gust` (set it on for a gust now).
- The girls' minds: no new offer or need. The bang reuses the `bang` notice and its flinch. Not done: the girls remarking on the wind, hair and jumpers moving, the sound of wind in the trees, grass swaying.
- Checked: unit tests, and one headless load (no shader errors, the wind, smoke and shutter updating after a forced gust). Not checked: how the sway looks, whether it costs frame time, how the bang sounds, the shutter's look and swing by eye, or the hay.

## Wildlife (TODO 21)

- **Pure logic** (`src/world/wildlife.js`, tests in `tests/wildlife.test.js`): `Rabbits` and `Pheasants`, both fed the same threats each step ([{ x, z, speed, yaw, beam }]: every vehicle, you, the girls) and returning events for the game to sound and pass on. `hedgeSpots` finds the places along the hedges (1.5 m out on the field side, every 6 m, never in a gap) that are both the rabbits' burrows and the pheasants' hides.
- **Rabbits:** sit 3 to 14 s, hop 0.8 to 3 m, and bolt at 7.5 m/s for the nearest burrow when anything comes within 8 m plus 0.9 m per m/s of its speed (to 22 m). They go to ground for 40 to 110 s. Half the bolts thump (sound only). Grass field only for now: the hill and the sheep field are not wired.
- **Headlights:** after `beamDark` 0.4 of darkness, a rabbit within 32 m and 0.3 rad of a lit vehicle's bearing freezes (`caught`), upright, with two eyes drawn in a basic material that only shows while it is caught and dark. It bolts when the vehicle is within 3.5 m plus 0.3 m per m/s. The bearing is the quad's heading, not the exact lamp aim, which is close enough at that range.
- **Pheasants:** three lie hidden in the hedge feet. A vehicle moving over 0.8 m/s flushes one within 5 m plus 0.5 m per m/s (to 13 m); you, a girl or a crawling vehicle within 3.2 m. It flies away from the threat for 3.6 s, climbing to 5 m, then is lost; a replacement hides somewhere else, at least 30 m from you, after 50 to 140 s. Hidden ones also call now and then. The sound is synthesised (`src/audio/wildlifeAudio.js`): two rasping notes and a band of fluttering noise for the wings, a low knock for the rabbit. Not listened to.
- **The girls:** a `pheasant` notice (heard, 40 m, salience 1.6) with a `flinch` rule within 14 m, chanced by a new per-girl `reactStartleChance`: Amy 0.95, Jess 0.4, so Amy jumps. No new spoken lines (they would need recording); a line about the pheasant, and one about a rabbit in the lights, are an easy follow-up through `banter` or a reaction line.
- **Look** (`wildlifeView.js`): instanced and procedural, built to the sheep's level of detail (faceted, lumpy, vertex-coloured, roughly 700 triangles each). A first pass was smooth blobs and looked flat beside the sheep, so both got the sheep's lumpy noise and their markings. Rabbit: agouti-brown back, pale belly and cheeks, long ears with dark tips, eyes, nose, short thick forelegs with pale paws, long hind feet, white scut; sits up or stretches out on a hop; eyeshine is a pair of unlit spheres drawn only while it is caught and dark. Pheasant: copper speckled body and breast, green head with a white collar, red face patches, eye and beak, folded wings, a barred three-plate tail, legs with toes; the open wings are a fan of nine banded flight feathers over a pad of coverts and beat while it flies. Looked at in headless close-ups, not on the running farm at night. Nothing in `drawDistance`: 14 rabbits and 3 pheasants in four meshes.
- **Debug:** Tune "Wildlife"; Girls tab "Both girls and the farm" shows the counts and has "Flush the nearest pheasant"; console `wildlife.rabbits`, `.caught`, `.pheasants`, `.flush`.
- **Checked:** unit tests, and one headless load (no errors, 14 rabbits, 3 pheasants, a flush turning one to flying). Not checked: how the animals look, how the sound sounds, the headlight freeze at night in the running game, or the frame time (not measured with `perf.mjs`).


## Traffic on the lane, visitors and gates that open (TODO 22)

- Jamie, before building: use the farm's own vehicles for the traffic (the post van is the 200TDi in red); the neighbours are on the girls' model, all women; borrowing the trailer is left out (maybe never); the gates have to open and shut, the south one and the big one both; you pull in for a visitor by choice (they wait and hoot, nothing forces you); a quiet road.
- The lane (`layout.publicLane`, x 206.4, 4.6 m of tarmac, drawn as a ribbon over the ground in `buildPublicLane`) is outside the farm's east hedge and inside the 420 m detailed terrain, so it has real ground. It is scenery: no collider, and vehicles on it brake for anything ahead instead (`Drive.inTheWay`). They drive on the left, so the southbound lane is the east one.
- `src/world/traffic.js` is pure (tests in `tests/traffic.test.js`). `Drive` follows waypoints at a turning circle of 4 m. `Traffic` deals a vehicle every `passEvery` 160 s on average by day (three times rarer at full dark, `nightQuiet`), at most three at once; it picks from `LANE_KINDS` (van, Land Rover, tractor, UTV: the existing models, in a few paints each). A visitor sets out about every `visitEvery` 540 s from `firstVisit` 150 s in, never in the dark, one at a time; `postShare` 0.6 of them are the post. `Visit` is a list of steps from `planVisit`: in along the lane, turn across it, stop outside the gate, the driver gets out and opens it (only if it is shut), drive in along the farm road's left side, across the grass field to the barn, stop, the driver walks to the door and waits (`parcelSeconds` 5, or `chatSeconds` 30 for a neighbour), back in, a loop round in front of the barn to face east, out along the other side of the road, open the gate from inside if you shut it meanwhile, stop outside and shut it (or, `leftOpenChance` 0.25, drive on and leave it). Tests run whole visits through a stand-in gate, with the gate shut, open, shut by someone mid-visit and blocked.
- The view (`trafficView.js`): each model is built once per paint and pooled. The body has to be hung from `bodyRoot` at the ground offset and the tyres set to `radius + offset + sag` (as quad.js does), or it floats. A passing driver is a dark torso and head; the visitor's driver is the girls' rig with a plain outfit (a red shirt for the post, a green jumper for the neighbour), sat on the seat with `SitIdle` and walking with the girls' walk clip. At night the lamps glow and the beam cones show, with no real lights (the pool in `vehicleLights.js` is untouched). The parcel at the barn door is a box that stays until the next one. Nothing is shared with the quads' physics, so nothing on the lane or farm road collides.
- Sound (`audio/trafficAudio.js`): a sawtooth drone through a low-pass and a band of tyre hiss per vehicle, through a panner, its pitch shifted by `dopplerFactor`. Synthesised, no assets.
- Gates (`world/gates.js`, pure, `tests/gates.test.js`): a `Gate` swings over `swingSeconds` 1.8; it is solid (collider on, `blockBelow` 0.5) until half open, and its hedge gap's `shut` follows, so `hedgeAhead` (the runaway tractor) and the girls' `route` read it (a shut gateway is skipped, so a girl told to go through a shut south gate heads straight at it: she is not taught to open gates). Both gates are `Gate`s built in `props.js`; the south gate keeps its tied-open look (the stake and twine show only when it is right open) and has a second collider for the leaf swung into the field; the big gate's leaves swing inwards. Shutting is refused (and a toast says so) with a quad, a girl or you within `clearRadius` 3 m; opening always works. A visitor shutting it ignores her own vehicle. Gate state is not saved: each day starts with the south gate open and the big gate shut.
- Girls: a new `traffic` notice (a lane pass at strength 0.5, a visitor arriving or chatting at 1) and a new `wave` reaction (a hand up, swinging, `reactWaveChance`: Jess 0.85, Amy 0.25, 0.4 default), with a button in the Girls tab. The nearest girl who `canRemark` says one line a visitor comes (`postVan`, `neighbourCalls`), a parcel is left (`parcelLeft`), the big gate is left open (`gateLeftOpen`) or you shut it afterwards (`gateShutting`): `npc/trafficLines.js`. No new offer or need: they never go to the visitors themselves yet.
- Debug: "Traffic" and "Gates" in the Tune tab (World); the Girls tab's shared section shows the lane and has buttons for a vehicle, the post van, a neighbour and each gate; the console's `lane` path does the same and sets each gate.
- Not done: borrowing the trailer, the neighbour speaking, sheep getting out of an open gate (item 180), the girls opening or shutting gates themselves, the visitors' driver having hands on the wheel, the drivers of passing traffic being more than a silhouette, and road noise beyond a drone. ## A day that changes, and the forecast board (TODO 28)

Jamie settled: a scheduled turn rather than random drift (so it can be forecast), only believable turns, a board that is right most of the time, the girls reacting lightly, nothing for the tarp or the washing line.

- **The turn is dealt at the start** from `this.seed ^ 0x2c9d` (`src/world/forecast.js`, pure, tested): 40% of games turn, between 1 and 5 game hours after the start. The table `SHIFTS` is the believable ones only (fair weather clouds over, grey goes wet, wet clears, fog burns off); snow never turns, because its own cover and melt rules already deal with it, and nothing turns to fog or snow.
- **Easing across, not jumping.** `Weather.beginShift` blends the sky (`baseSky`) and the wind over `shiftHours` (0.75 game hours, about 3 real minutes at the usual clock rate). The rain retargets 40% of the way through so the sky darkens before it falls, and the barometer moves with the sky, a little ahead of the rain. The day's warmest moves by the difference in `KIND_WARMTH` between the kinds (sunny +1.5, rain -1.5 degrees), spread over the blend through `peak`, which `updateDaylight` already reads each frame.
- **The board is right 80% of the time about whether the day turns.** One roll per day: right means it shows the real turn; wrong means no turn when one comes, or a made-up turn when none does. It shows three slots, with the slot's middle hour deciding which kind it shows. It says nothing about wind or temperature. It never changes its mind during the day.
- **Forced or hand-set weather stays put.** `?weather=` makes the forecast `frozen`, and `WeatherHost.setKind` cancels any turn left, so the debug panel and console work as they did.
- **The girls.** Amy reads the board early (`forecastChat` 0.85, Jess 0.2) and says whether it is turning worse or better. As the sky turns (once the blend is 30% across) either may remark, more often when the board said so; when it did not, `forecastMissed` is the fallback. These are reactions to the weather in `watchWeather`, with their own cooldowns, not offers.
- **Not saved.** The day is dealt afresh each load like the weather it came from; the farm's save does not carry it.
- **Not checked in a browser:** the slate's drawing (symbols, text fit) and how the sky blend looks. Unit tests cover the dealing, the board, the blend, the rain timing and the temperature move.

## The stream after rain (TODO 43)

- `src/world/streamLevel.js` (pure, tested in `tests/streamLevel.test.js`): a level from 0 to 1. Rain over `rainFrom` 0.12 raises it by rain / `riseSeconds` 360 a second (steady heavy rain, about 0.85, takes about seven minutes); dry weather lowers it over `fallSeconds` 1100. It lags the shower on purpose, as the pasture's water takes a while to arrive. A game starts at wet ground x `startWet` 0.7, since weather isn't saved and a rainy start should look rainy. The level is not saved for the same reason.
- What it changes: `hill.setRise` lifts the water's surface by `level x floodRise` (0.22 m at most, under the ford channel's 0.42 m, so it never goes over the banks). `waterAt` and `puddleAt` read it live, so the water is wider across and wheels and the girls see it. The drawn ribbon (`hillProps.js`) is lifted by the same amount, browns with the level and drifts a streak texture faster (`flow`, 1 to 3.6 times). Engine drowning is multiplied by `gulp` (1 to 2.2) for a wheel in the stream (`World.gulpBoostAt`, used in `Quad.waterGulp`), because the ford's water already counted as full depth in its middle, so more depth alone changed little. There is no current pushing a quad downstream; flow is visual and the gulp is the cost.
- The girls: `streamUp` when it crosses `upAt` 0.5, `streamDown` when it falls back under `downAt` 0.2, said by a girl stood about within 40 m of you (`updateWeather`). Lines in `streamLines.js`.
- Debug: Tune "Stream", the Girls tab's shared section (level slider) and the console's `weather.stream`.
- Checked: unit tests only. Not checked in the running game: how the rise looks at the ford, the streak texture, how much the extra gulp changes a crossing by hand, or the lines by ear.

## A bonfire at dusk (TODO 45)

Settled with Jamie before building (all the recommendations): a fixed site on the grass field's south side; hedge cuttings as bundles carried like the cans (`E`, `G`) and put on a pile by hand, three to a fire; lit with `E`; a point light made at the start and only dimmed; the girls sit round it as an offer, with new lines; rain and wind put it out and a vehicle too near is warned.

- Site (`layout.bonfire` at -6, 71, `layout.brushwood`): 19 m from the track, 9 m from the south hedge, 10 m from the nearest "left here" spot; a keep-out circle of 6 m (`spawnKeepOut`) so nothing starts on it. Six bundles lie along the hedge at z 77, so one fire uses half of them and a second can be built the same day.
- `src/world/bonfire.js` (pure, tested): `Bonfire` with states none, built, lit and ash. Lighting needs the full pile (3) and rain under `rainOut` 0.4. It grows over `growSeconds` 25, burns `burnSeconds` 540 (counted from lighting), then dies over `dieSeconds` 70. Rain over `rainDamp` 0.06 makes it smoky and burns it `dampBurn` 2.5 times faster; rain over `rainOut` puts it out; wind over `windOut` 5 m/s blows out a fire that has not yet reached 0.7. `warms(distance)`: within 4.5 m of a fire at 0.4 or more.
- `src/items/brushwood.js`: `BrushwoodHeap`, a tied bundle of sticks with a few leaves, carried like the oil bottle (counts in `handsFull`, `G` puts it down, shown on the map). Used ones vanish.
- `src/world/bonfireView.js`: a ring of 12 stones, up to 18 sticks shown a bundle at a time (black once burnt, with an ash disc), three crossed additive flame planes that flicker and lean with the wind, a ground glow, 36 sparks, a woodsmoke emitter (its own `ExhaustSmoke`, dimmed with the daylight in `updateWeather` like the vehicles' smoke), the flickering `PointLight` (`lightCandela` 90, a fifth of that by day), and four logs lying round it. Flames and sparks are additive and fog-free, so they glow at night and wash out by day without needing dimming. No colliders: you can ride through it, and "Mind the fire!" shows within about 2.3 m of a lit fire.
- Girls (`src/npc/fireSit.js`, pure, tested): an offer `fire` in `OFFER_KINDS` (warmth 0.9, rest 0.6, company 0.5, purpose 0.3), weight `weight` 0.9 times 1 + `duskBoost` 1.2 times how dark it is, only while the fire is blazing, a log is free, rain is under 0.12 and she is within 90 m. Each girl has her own session and log; she holds herself with `hold('fire')`, goes by `goRest` to a rest spot of kind `log` (hips 0.46 m up), sits `sitSeconds` 110, and says `fireSit`, `fireWarm`, `fireRomance` (only if favoured and you are within 6 m), then `fireDone`, or `fireOut` if the fire dies. Cooldown 240 s a girl. Favour 1.5 (+1 if favoured) at the end. Sitting near a blaze counts as warming in `needsContext`, so her warmth need falls. E at the fire asks the nearest free girl, as the flask rock does.
- Lines: `src/npc/fireLines.js` (`fireLit` when you light it, said by the nearest girl stood about, `fireOffer`, `fireYes`, and the five above), recorded for both girls.
- Not kept overnight: the fire and the bundles are dealt fresh each load. Not done: sharing the flask at the fire (the flask's set is tied to the rock), anything on the fire but brushwood, the girls adding fuel, the fire spreading, marshmallows.
- Debug: Tune "Bonfire" and "Sitting by the bonfire"; the Girls tab's shared section shows the fire's state and builds and lights it, puts it out, clears it, and takes you there; each girl's "Make her do" has "fire"; the console has `farm.bonfire` and `farm.bonfireLit`.
- Checked: unit tests, and headless: the game loads with no errors, the prompts go from "Put the brushwood on the pile (1 of 3)" to "Light the bonfire", a lit fire reaches a full blaze with the flames shown and the light on, and a girl sent to it starts a session. Not checked: how any of it looks, the smoke and sparks in the wind, a girl actually sitting on a log (the seat height 0.46 m is a guess), or the frame-time cost of the extra light and smoke.

## Mud on the lens (TODO 47)

Asked and settled: `X` wipes (a ring item on the pad); open vehicles only; the sources are the quad ahead on the mud, puddles and the ford at speed, and hard rain; a screen overlay; goggles are item 48, so this has a `guard` value they can set; no new girl lines for now.

- `src/effects/lensMud.js` (pure, tested in `tests/lensMud.test.js`): a list of splats (x and y over the screen, size, thickness). `leaderAhead` finds the nearest moving, muddy vehicle within `followRange` and inside a cone ahead; `splatRates` turns that, a puddle under any wheel and the rain into splats a second, scaled by `1 - guard`; `updateLens` rolls them and dries the old ones (mud over `dryTime`, drops over `dropDryTime`); `wipeLens` makes the first wipe a wider, thinner smear (`smearGrow`, `wipeSmear`) and the second take it off (`wipeClear`).
- Open or not is by spec id (`isOpenVehicle`): the UTV, both Land Rovers, the Fiesta and the Corsa are closed. The rain only counts when the quad is not under a roof (`world.covered`).
- `src/effects/lensMudView.js` draws it as a 2D canvas under the HUD, about ten times a second and only while something is stuck, so a clean lens costs nothing. Its colours are dimmed with `daylight.darkness` so it does not glow at night.
- The lens is yours, not the vehicle's: it dries while you are off, and is hidden on foot or in a cab.
- Not saved between games. Not measured for frame time: a small canvas redrawn at 10 Hz.
- Not done: girl lines about it, and goggles (item 48).

## A thunderstorm (TODO 51)

Jamie settled: rarer than snow, flashes and thunder only (nothing is struck), the girls as recommended, nothing blocked, and the debug and console controls.

- **A weather kind** `storm` (`weather.js`): weight 0.03 (snow is 0.06), a near-black sky, rain 0.95, wind 1.9, cold to warm. `Weather.storm` is 1 in a storm and eases with the turn into or out of it. It is not a separate layer on top of rain, so the existing rain, wet ground, barn doorway shelter and cab shelter all apply as they do in heavy rain.
- **It always passes** (`forecast.js`): `SHIFTS.storm` goes to rain, drizzle or overcast, always, between `stormEarliest` (0.4) and `stormLatest` (1.5) game hours after the start, which is about two to eight minutes of play. A storm that lasted the whole game would keep the girls in the barn all day. The board shows it with the rain picture; the barometer reads 0.08.
- **Lightning and thunder** (`thunder.js`, pure, tested): a strike every 7 to 35 s at the height of a storm, 20% of them close (90 to 500 m). A strike is 1 to 3 flickers, the thunder comes `distance / 343` s later. Far strikes are dimmer and only rumble; near ones are bright and crack. The flash is the whole sky at once, so `updateStorm` in `game.js` adds to the hemisphere and sun light, the tone mapping exposure and the fog and sky colours for that frame (all reset by the sky's own update next frame), and less indoors. Tunables under "Thunderstorm".
- **Sound** (`audio/thunderAudio.js`): filtered brown noise with a rolling envelope, plus a bright burst for a crack. Procedural, so no asset to credit. Not heard by ear yet.
- **The girls**: thunder is a notice (`thunder`, heard, placed where you are since it comes from the whole sky, with the strike's nearness as its strength). A reaction rule makes her flinch only for close ones (strength 0.9 or more, `reactStartleChance`). One girl who is free to remark says a thunder line after each clap (`thunderClose` or `thunderFar`). Amy has `stormWont` 1, so `refusesStorm()` (`companionDriving.js`) stops her riding pillion, on the rack or driving, from her own choices and from the wheel, where she answers with `stormWont` instead. A seated Amy on a quad stood still gets off once (`stormGetOff`). Jess has no refusal, and her thunder lines are glad. Lines in `stormLines.js` (recorded with the rest).
- **Left out**: a girl already driving when the storm starts carries on (she isn't stopped mid-drive); Jess does not actively seek a ride out in it; no strike marks, no puddles gulping or fords rising faster, no rain on the camera. Not checked in the running game: how the flash looks (the amounts are first guesses), how the thunder sounds, and the lines in play.

## The barn gets a second span, and the bench takes any vehicle (TODO 52)

Jamie: the tractor, UTV, Land Rovers and cars can be bent like the quads but don't fit the bay. First plan was a repair pad in the yard; Jamie preferred a wider barn with a second bay inside, a second door, a second light, the same loft, and better jack and stand models.

- **Shape.** `layout.barn` is still the first span (loft, hay door, boards, bench all hang off it, so none of that moved). `barn.annex` adds a second span of the same size on its south side (z 64.6 to 73.8), so the barn is 18.4 m across. Each span has its own gable roof at the same height and pitch (a valley between, not one tall roof), and the wall between them is left out: three posts (`dividerPosts`, with colliders) carry a beam and a steel gutter. The second door is in the same +x wall, 4 m wide and 3.2 m high, centred on the span. `barnShape` gives `outerAll`/`innerAll` for both spans; `insideBarn`, `underRoof`, `indoorLevel`, `floorBlend` and `wallBoxes` use them (the east wall is cut in pieces round both doors), while the loft and the first door keep using the first span.
- **The valley.** The two roofs' lower edges at the valley end at the wall line with no overhang (with the overhang they crossed each other and flickered at the front). The loft's top rail is drawn only within 3.7 m of the first span's middle; the roof comes down below its height nearer the valley and it stuck through the tin once the valley could be seen from above (the collider keeps the whole rail).
- **Bays.** `layout.bay` is the quad bay, as it was. `layout.bay2` (6 by 3.8 m, long way to the door) is the big one, with a trolley jack and two axle stands (`workshop/jackModel.js`, about 30 primitives each: chassis channels, wheels and castors, pump housing, ram, lift arm with a rubber saddle and handle; stands with a three-legged base, a sleeve, a ratcheted column, a pawl and a saddle). `Bench.unitAtBench()` is the quad in the small bay (or the barn find), else anything in the big bay; the girls only use the small bay (`unitInBay`, unchanged), so none of her bench work changed.
- **Bench not moved.** I had said I would move it to the back wall between the bays. It turned out that nothing needs it: the bench acts on whichever vehicle is in a bay and the toolbox only has to be near the bench. Turning it would also have touched the rack, the notebook, the charger, the leads and the oil's places, the girls' work spot and the tests. If the 13 m between the bench and the big bay feels wrong in play, that is the next thing to change.
- **Jobs for everything.** Per Jamie, the jobs apply to any vehicle and grey out when they don't fit: `plug` needs a spark ignition (no diesel), `carb` and `boot` need neither a diesel nor `injected` (the Corsa). The rest already went by the engine's flags.
- **Moved to make room:** the generator (to z 75.7, outside the new south wall), the helmet hooks and the jumper rail (to the new south wall, with the girls' door waypoints now through the second door), the bell (north of the first door's north leaf), the silage (to the west wall, `layout.silage`), a jerry can's start spot, and one yard quad slot (-53, 72, gone: the door's keep-out circle took it and nothing else fitted). `spawnKeepOut` has circles for the extension and its doorway. The yard grass no longer grows under the roof of either span (it grew inside the second span's floor).
- **A second bulb** (`light2`, made at the start and dimmed with the first) hangs over the big bay.
- **Not done:** the fumes haze is still drawn only over the first span (an engine run in the second counts as indoors for coughing and the echo, but its fumes show less), the girls' pathing to the second door beyond the waypoints, and a second interior detail pass (shelves, tools) in the new span.
- **Checked** with unit tests and headless screenshots (outside, front roof from above, the valley close up, inside the big bay, the jack and stand at full size). The game logged `THREE.Object3D.add: object not an instance` twice from `instanceModel` in `assets.js` on load (an empty tile list); it comes from the hedges, bushes or trees, not this change, but I did not run it on the commit before to prove it. **Not checked:** driving the Land Rover or tractor through the new door and into the bay by hand, how it feels at night under the two bulbs, and the girls walking to the hooks and rail by the new route.

## Mist in the hollow and rainbows (TODO 61)

The item settled it; I chose the details without asking.

- `src/world/mist.js` (pure, tests in `tests/mist.test.js`; tunables "Mist" and "Rainbows"). A game has a dew drawn from its seed (`chance` 0.6 of games, 0.5 to 1 thick), so a ?seed=N repeats it and sunny days can still have mist (the weather's `wet` is zero for sunny, so dampness only adds to it, `dampWeight` 0.4). The target is the dew times how much of the dawn is left (full to `fullUntil` 1 h after sunrise, then gone over `burnHours` 2.5, down to half that at 20 degrees), how cool the air is (below 14 degrees, full at 4) and how still (gone at wind 1.2, so overcast clears it and fog does not). Rain at 0.3 or any snow takes it away. The level eases up over 90 s and down over 120 s. Games start between 8:00 and 15:30 and sunrise is 7:15, so only the earlier starts open in mist; the rest never see it.
- Look: the yard haze's `HazeLayer` again, with `count` and `spread` options: 26 big blobs 0.2 to 1.5 m up over the north field's hollow, stretched to its ellipse, opacity up to 0.34. It is dimmed with the daylight like the other haze.
- Rainbow: once rain has been at 0.25 or more, then eases to 0.12 or less within 300 s while the sun is up (sunshine 0.5 or more, taking the sky's sun into account) and below 0.6 rad, the bow comes up over 15 s and fades over 150 s (starting to go after 40% of the window). `src/effects/rainbowView.js` draws it as a ribbon of seven stripes, 40 to 43.5 degrees round the point opposite the sun, 900 m off and following the camera, added to the sky, with only the part above the horizon drawn. The ribbon is rebuilt when the sun has moved by about 0.01.
- Not done: the girls saying anything about either (a line each would need recording), the mist thickening the fog when you ride into it, mist over the stream or the meadow pond, and a double bow.
- Checked: unit tests and a production build. Not checked in the running game: how the mist or the bow looks, or that the bow sits right against the sky.

## The barn cat (TODO 68)

Settled with Jamie up front: one cat, called "the cat"; procedural but not basic; shooed with `E`; Jess talks to it and Amy pretends not to like it; a tabby; the sounds synthesised.

- **Pure logic** (`src/world/cat.js`, tests in `tests/cat.test.js`; tunables "Cat"). A small state machine fed a `ctx` each step: the threats (you and every vehicle, each with a `loud` of how hard its engine is run), the perches, the girls (`free` when stood about) and whether it should be sheltering. Modes: sit, walk, seek (a perch), jump, sleep, down, flee, hide, greetWalk, greeted, homeWalk, leave, curl. It decides after each sit: shelter, else (`perchChance`) a perch weighted by warmth, else a stroll. Events back to the game: mew, settled, woke, fled, greeted, fussed.
- **What counts as a perch.** An open vehicle only (its model's top under 1.35 m; a cab's roof is no place to sleep). Its warmth is the larger of the engine's held heat (`engine.soak`) and the sun on the seat (`weather.warmth`, nothing in rain or snow or inside the barn), `perchWarmth`. It wants one at 0.3 and gets up when it cools below 0.12. A running, occupied or moving vehicle is "taken": a sleeper on it is sent off (scared), and it seeks nothing taken. So a sunny midday makes every open quad a candidate, as the item says.
- **Shooing.** `E` at its invisible box (range 3 m) calls `shoo`: off a perch it hops down and runs, and keeps off that perch for `banSeconds` (240). Walking up to a sleeper does nothing; a vehicle revved at 0.55 or more within 9 m, or going fast near it on the ground, scares it. The girls are not threats (it likes them).
- **Sheltering.** Rain over 0.25, any snow, under 2 degrees, or darkness over 0.7: it walks to a point outside the barn's door and in, and curls up on the floor. The barn's wall is shut to it except at the door (`allowed` in `setUpCat`), and a perch is only jumped to from within 1.4 m (a blocked walk gives up rather than leaping a wall: tested).
- **The girls.** Five situations (`src/npc/catLines.js`, merged into both line sets): `catPerched`, `catShooed`, `catFled`, `catGreeted`, `catFussed`, two lines each for each girl. Amy: not touching it, tells you off for scaring it, "do not rub on me", then, caught half way through the greeting, a scratch behind the ears. Jess is soppy throughout. They are remarks (any free girl nearest you, or the one it went to), not offers: the cat goes to them, they do not decide anything. Its greeting is the cat's choice (`greetRate`) so it needs no entry in the chooser.
- **Look** (`src/world/catView.js`). Built in parts so it can be posed: body, a head with ears, eyes with pupils, nose and whiskers, a four-segment tail, four legs with white paws, brown tabby bands as thin shells round the back and sides (colour alone was too coarse to show a stripe). Poses: a diagonal-gait walk, trot and run (tail up, then streaming), a sit with the head turning and the tail round its feet, a curl with breathing, and a stretched jump. `fur` and `stalk` are now exported from `wildlifeView.js` for it.
- **Sound** (`src/audio/catAudio.js`): a mew (a sawtooth gliding up and down through a sliding band-pass, with vibrato; shorter and sharper as a cry), a hiss (high-passed noise) and a purr in 2.4 s bursts of chopped low noise while it sleeps or greets and you are within 5 m. Nothing recorded.
- **Kept between loads?** No: the cat starts in the yard each load, placed by a `farmSeed` stream. Nothing about it is a lasting condition.
- **Checked:** unit tests; the model's poses in a standalone render and the cat asleep on a quad, sitting and running in the game (headless, at full size). Not checked: the girls' lines by ear (recorded after), the cat sheltering in the barn in the running game, how often a real game sees it asleep on something, the greeting from a girl's point of view, and the purr and mew by ear.
- **Ideas left out:** stroking it (`E` when it is greeted), feeding it, kittens, the cat on the tractor or a car's bonnet, and it hearing the bell.

## The farm dog (TODO 70)

Settled with Jamie up front: item 70 only (she follows, rides the rack, chases quads and boots); herding and a better model stay with item 195. A border collie called Meg, a girl. Quads with a rear rack only. She just follows, with no call needed. The girls chase and nip with no harm done. Nothing saved between loads. Sounds synthesised. "Make it look good enough for now, not too crap."

- **Pure logic** (`src/world/dog.js`, tests in `tests/dog.test.js`; tunables "Dog"). A state machine fed a `ctx` each step: you (`owner`: where, how fast, riding or not), the rack of the quad you are on (`rack`, with `ok` false when a girl is on the back or it is tipped), every other vehicle (with its driver) and the girls on foot (with how fast each is walking). Modes: idle (stands, then sits), wander and sniff (a mooch near you while you stand), follow, seek (the rack), hop, ride, down, chase, nip, dodge. Events back to the game: bark, yelp, mounted, down, chase, nip.
- **Following.** Jamie found her glued to him, so she has her own business: she comes after you only beyond `followMax` (12 m), whether you stand or walk, as far as a spot 2.8 m from you (inside `followMin`, 4 m), trotting, running at `runSpeed` (7.5) when you move faster or she is well behind, and at `sprintSpeed` (13) beyond 22 m, so she can keep up with a quad for a while but not all the way. A long way back (120 m), or held up against a hedge with you more than 12 m off for 3 s, she is put back 5 to 11 m from you. `allowed` in `setUpDog` keeps her out of buildings (but not the barn, by its door), off the barn's walls and out of hedges except through a gap whose gate is open; she wades water, since nothing about the water was worth the code.
- **The rack.** A quad's `mugAnchor` (the middle of the rear rack, where a girl leaves her tea) is her seat: it is on the utility and farm styles only, so those are the quads she rides. She goes for it when you have been stopped on a quad for 1.2 s and she is within 30 m, runs to it, and hops (0.55 s, an arc) up. On it she follows the anchor's position and the quad's heading each frame. She hops down when you get off, when a girl sits on the back or the quad is tipped, and is then banned from the rack for 6 s so she does not bounce straight back. The seat is only the rack's height and heading: on a steep slope she does not tilt with the quad.
- **Chasing and heels.** A vehicle going over 3 m/s within 28 m (and not yours) is chased at 0.12 a second, for 9 s, then a 35 s cooldown; she runs 3 m to the side of it and a little behind, so she is never under the wheels, and barks about once a second. A girl walking within 14 m gets the same treatment at her heels (weaving behind her), for 8 s. Both are her own choice with their own dice rather than offers in the chooser, as the cat's greeting is: the girls decide nothing, the dog does. A vehicle coming straight at her (she is within `dodgeRadius` plus 0.9 m per m/s ahead of it and within 1.6 m of its line) sends her sideways with a yelp; that includes the quad you are on.
- **The girls.** Four remarks (`src/npc/dogLines.js`, merged into both line sets): `dogMounted`, `dogChasing` (said by the girl driving the quad she chases), `dogBoots` (by the girl she goes after) and `dogYelp`, two lines each. Amy tells her off and then softens; Jess is soppy. Like the cat's, they are remarks from whoever is free and near, not offers.
- **Look** (`src/world/dogView.js`). Built in parts like the cat: a barrel, haunches, shoulders, a white chest, ruff and belly, a neck, a head with a white blaze and chin, amber eyes, ears standing with the top folded forward, a tongue, a four-segment tail with a white tip, four legs with white socks. Poses: walk, trot (diagonal gait), gallop (pairs together, spine arching), stand with the head turning and the tail swinging, sit (computed from the body's tilt so the haunch rests on the ground and the forelegs reach it), sniff (nose down), ride (the sit, with the tongue out) and a hop. A bark tips the head back for a moment. Checked in a standalone render (`.scratch/dog/dog.html`) beside the cat's.
- **Sound** (`src/audio/dogAudio.js`): a bark (a falling sawtooth through two vowel bands with a puff of breath, doubled now and then when she is excited), a yelp, and panting in 1.2 s bursts of chopped noise while she runs and you are within 5 m. Nothing recorded.
- **Kept between loads?** No: she is put beside you when the game starts and has no lasting condition.
- **After the first look (Jamie):** the eyes were orange rings that bulged (now small, dark and flush); the head sank into the white ruff and the black body when stood or sat (the neck pivot is higher and further forward, the muzzle white so the face reads); the legs hardly seemed to move because their swing was a fixed rate and amplitude that slid over the ground (the gait phase now advances with her speed, each cycle covering about 0.8, 1.1 or 1.6 m, with a wider swing, and the legs are eased faster than the rest of the pose); the panting was loud and ran at a trot (now only at a run, a third of the volume, audible within 5 m).
- **Checked:** unit tests; the model's poses in a standalone render; the game loads and she stands beside you after the call button (headless). Not checked in the running game: her getting on a rack and riding, the chase, the heels and the dodge, how often a girl's quad is chased in practice, and the sounds and lines by ear.
- **Ideas left out:** herding (item 195), stroking her, a lead, her riding the tractor, a car or a trailer, her swimming, tilting on a slope, and a whistle to call her.

## North of the farm: the wood and the Hollow Run (TODO 69)

- Jamie: the wood goes north, laid out so it can grow east towards the stubble fields later, the way the hill grew south; entered by a gate; the track matters most and has to be fun to drive; the pond works like the south one; fallen branches are static; make the track very different from the one in the middle; name each circuit and leave a TODO for a race on the new one. Not asked for, so left out: the pond icing over (it was in the item's first wording; it is TODO 339).
- Where (`layout.woods`, `src/world/woods.js`, pure, tests in `tests/woods.test.js`): x -80 to 80, z -200 to -80, so the same width as the grass field and edge to edge with its north hedge, and the east side (x 80) is the line of the barley field's north hedge, which is what lets it grow east later (TODO 337). Hedged on its other three sides. The detailed terrain (420 m square) already reaches z -210, so no change there. The ground is the wood's own (`woods.profile`) fading out over 40 m beyond the hedges, like the hill's; it runs in over the first 14 m from the grass field's hedge so there is no step at the gate (a test holds this), and the farm itself is unchanged (a test compares it with the same layout without the wood).
- The gate (`layout.woods.gate`, in the grass field's north hedge at x -30, the same x as the south gate): the south gate's twin, built by the same `buildFiveBarGate` with the leaf swung back into the grass field (towards +z), tied open to start, with its own E prompt. Shutting it makes the girls' route to the wood stop at the hedge, like the south gate.
- The Hollow Run (`layout.woods.circuit`): a closed loop of 27 Catmull-Rom points, 589 m round, 3.4 m wide (the Home Loop is 4.6 m wide, 400 m, in an open field). Four long rows run east and west with a hairpin at each end, then a long run back along the north and down the west side past the pond. Checked by tests: inside the wood with 4 m to spare, never within 11 m of itself, no bend tighter than 4.5 m radius (the tightest are the two hairpins, about 5.3), no step steeper than 0.4 between metres, and a lap's height range over 2.5 m. It is fun by being different: narrow, with the trees close, a broad swell (`hump`, 36 m across and 3 m high) that the middle two rows climb over, banked bends (a berm up to 0.5 m on the outside of a bend tighter than 17 m, none on a gentle one), four whoops (`whoops`: 0.5 m high humps 11 m apart across the first straight), bogs in the shape the home track's are (they join `heights.bogs`, so the quads' bog rules, the wear and the map see them), and five logs across it in pairs that cover 2.2 m of its 3.4 m, leaving about 1.2 m, so you steer round them (`logs`, static boxes). Nothing in it is a jump: the whoops are small enough to ride through.
- The ground (`groundTexture.paintWoods`): leaf litter and moss, the loop and the gate's lead-in worn dark with two ruts and a grass strip, the same mottling as the home track. Only the ground texture has ruts: the terrain mesh is 1 m, too coarse for a rut.
- Trees (`woodsPlants`): 170 oaks (the farm's two models, with the 0.32 m cylinder collider the others have) at random spots, thinned into glades by a slow patchy pattern, no two within 6 m, none within the track's half-width plus 2.6 m, on the pond, in the bogs or by a log; 110 hazel and holly with no collider. All through `instanceModel`, so they tile and cull like the hill's. The north boundary's old tree line (`buildTrees`) skips the wood. The wood's trees are random each load from the props stream like the other trees.
- The pond: the meadow's pond again (an ellipse 8.5 by 6 m, 1 m cut with 0.55 m standing, so shin to knee deep), through the same `buildPond` (now exported from `hillProps.js` and given the hill or the wood as its source), in the dished ground of a gentle hollow. Its `waterAt` joins `heights.puddleAt`, which is what drowns an engine. Mud round its shore.
- The girls: a girl following you waits just inside the gate (`woods.waitSpot`, as the hill's foot) while you are more than 8 m in (`pastGate`), rather than driving among the trees. They don't go there on their own, and they don't drive the circuit (TODO 338).
- The map: the wood is on it (so the map is about 120 m taller than it was), with the Hollow Run, its bogs and its pond, and both circuits are named: `layout.trackName` 'The Home Loop' and `woods.circuit.name` 'The Hollow Run'. Naming matters for the race (TODO 336), which would keep a best lap for each.
- Console: `wood` and `hollowrun` are places you can send things to (`gameHost`).
- Measured: loading to "Ready" 2.44 s, against 2.50 s without it (the same run, within noise), under the 2.5 s budget. Not measured: frame time in the wood itself (170 oaks and 110 bushes in view at once; the perf script only looks at the spawn view).
- Checked: unit tests and the game loading without errors, with a look from the gate, inside, along the first straight, by the pond and from above. Not checked: riding it (the berms, whoops and log gaps are by the numbers, not by feel), the pond's drowning in the running game, the girls waiting at the gate, or the map's drawing.

## Autumn in the wood (TODO 83)

- Jamie settled the scope before building: autumn is about one day in six (not one in three); the trees turn orange and the leaves come down; the wet leaves are slippery; conkers lie under the oaks; Jess throws them at you and Amy minds. Making more of the farm seasonal is TODO 341.
- **Which days** (`world/autumn.js`, pure, `tests/autumn.test.js`): `autumnToday` deals it from the farm's seed and the day (`AUTUMN_CHANCE` 0.17, measured about 1 in 6 over 600 farm-days), so a day on a farm is always the same and the next may not be. `?season=autumn` or `?season=summer` forces it. It has to be known before the world is built (it turns the trees and paints the ground), and the farm is opened after the world, so `load()` peeks at the save for its seed and day first.
- **The trees** (`autumnView.applyAutumnTint`): the cut-out leaf materials of the oaks (two golds and oranges), the bush and the hedge get a colour multiplied into their green leaf texture (`AUTUMN_TINTS`, values above 1 in red because the texture is green). In place on the shared models, before anything is built from them, so every oak and hedge on the farm turns, not only the wood's. Not done: the leaves' own texture is still green underneath, so the tint is a guess at orange and may want tuning by eye.
- **The floor** (`groundTexture.paintWoods`): three times as many fallen-leaf blotches, in orange, gold and red. The track is painted over them, so the leaves show between the ruts and off it, not on the dark track.
- **Leaves in the air** (`autumnView.LeafFall`): 300 coloured points in a cylinder round the camera, like the snow but slower and swaying more; how many show is `leavesInAir` (`leafFall` 90 under the trees, a fifth of that in the fields, 0.4 times in heavy rain), dimmed with the daylight.
- **Grip** (`surfaces.surfaceFeel`, a `leaf` argument; params `leafGrip`, `leafDryShare`, `leafRoll` in "Surfaces"): the whole wood floor, not just the track (`leafCover`). Wet leaves give 0.72 of the grip and 1.12 of the rolling drag; a dry day has 35% of that slip. It scales with the ground's wetness the way tarmac and stubble do.
- **Conkers** (`autumnView.buildConkers`): seven round each oak (`CONKERS_PER_TREE`), 3 cm brown spheres through `tiledInstances` and the cover draw distance, kept off the track, logs and pond by `woods.treeOk`. About 1,200 of them, 30 tiles.
- **Jess throws one** (`npc/conkers.js`, pure): a chooser offer (`conker`, weighing `throwWeight`) for Jess only, when she is free in the wood, 4 to 14 m from you, and 150 s since the last. She turns to face you and winds her right arm back for 0.9 s (`companionThrowing.js`, a rig poser like the smoke-waving), the conker leaves her hand along an arc at 11 m/s to a point up to 0.9 m off your head, and it lands with a thock (`audio/conkerAudio.js`). It counts as a hit within 0.55 m of where you are when it lands, so keeping moving makes it a miss (a still target is hit about a third of the time). It never hurts and costs nothing. `throwing` is a soft job in `availability.js`, so asking her for something else gives way. The Girls tab's "Make her do" has a button and the console `girl jess conker`.
- **Amy**: tuts at a throw she can see (`conkerAmy`, 70%), and says once a game how lovely the wood is (`conkerWood`) and once, the first time you ride the wet leaves in it, to take care (`conkerSlippery`). Jess: `conkerGo`, `conkerHit`, `conkerMiss`. The lines are in `npc/conkerLines.js`.
- Tunables: "Autumn" in the Tune tab (the leaves, the throw) and the leaf grip in "Surfaces". The day's roll and the conker count are fixed when the farm loads, so they are constants, not tuning.
- Checked: unit tests, and a headless load with `?season=autumn` and `?season=summer` (no errors, 30 conker tiles against none, a forced throw going windup, release, land). Not checked: how any of it looks (the tint, the floor, the leaves), the arm's pose, the grip by feel, the sound, or frame time in the wood; loading time was not re-measured.

## Second-hand buying (#28, was TODO 133)

Jamie's answers: pay in pounds from a small starting purse, topped up by each farm job done; the pool is every vehicle in the game with different colours and variants, and any vehicle added later must join it (recorded here and in CLAUDE.md, and a test fails otherwise); the board is a bunch of classified ads that are mostly empty (semi-rare); selling is a separate item (#294), next.

- **The snag.** The tractor, UTV and each Land Rover have a single slot in `game.js` (`tractorUnit`, `utvUnit`, `landys.*`) and the Corsa and Fiesta are the girls' own cars, always here with an owner. A second of any of them would need a multi-instance refactor. So an ad is only for a model that **isn't on the farm this game** (the fleet is a subset of the pool, so there is usually something), and the cars and the loan quad are in `NOT_FOR_SALE` with the reason. A quad of a model already here would share its id. Jamie agreed to this scope; cars and duplicates are a separate item.
- **Pure logic** (`src/farm/classifieds.js`, tests in `tests/classifieds.test.js`). `FOR_SALE` has each model's going rate, kind and four colours (its own among them); `NOT_FOR_SALE` has the rest with reasons. `unlistedSpecs` and a test that finds every exported spec in `quadSpecs.js` fail for a vehicle in neither list, so **a new vehicle must go in one of the two** (CLAUDE.md says so). `dealAds` deals the day's board from a stream of the farm seed and the day: `adDayChance` 0.3 any ad, `secondAdChance` 0.3 a second and `thirdAdChance` 0.1 a third. `makeAd` picks a colour, hours, miles and wear leaning to the old end and a price from the going rate within `priceSpread` 12%, less up to `wearDiscount` 35% for wear. `Market` holds the purse (`startPurse` 250), the day's ads and what's bought (`maxBought` 2).
- **Buying** is not mid-game: the record (model, colour, hours, miles, wear, price) goes in the farm save (`market`, saved at once) and `withBought` forces the model into the next morning's fleet in place of the dealt one, as `usedSpec`: the model's spec, same id (so everything that knows the model still does), with the colour, hours and wear. The ranges are one hour wide because the tractor and UTV divide by them. `fleetIds` stays as dealt; the bought models are added after it, so `fleetFromSave` still finds every id. Old saves have no `market` and load unchanged, so `SAVE_VERSION` is not raised.
- **Paying.** `JobBoard.complete` calls `onComplete`, which `game.js` sets to pay `jobPay` (60): one place for the muck, silage and service jobs.
- **The board** (`src/world/classifiedsView.js`): cork on the back wall at z + 3.75 (photos at -3.65, jobs -2, calendar 0, records +2), slips pinned a little askew, a close view like the chalkboard's with the tin and `1` to `3` to buy. Bare cork with a few pins when there is nothing.
- **Debug:** Tune "Second-hand market"; the Girls tab's "Second-hand market" folder (the money, put ads up, take them down); the console's `farm.purse`, `farm.market`, `farm.ads`.
- **Not done:** the girls' lines about a bought vehicle (they would need recording), a gamepad route to buy (the close view is a menu, but the number keys are the only buy), selling (#294), and the cars. **Not checked by eye:** how the cork board and its slips look and sit on the wall, and how a bought vehicle looks in its colour (only the colour list is tested). The numbers (prices, £60 a job, a 30% ad day) are first guesses.

## Selling vehicles (#294)

Jamie's answers: anything in `FOR_SALE` can be sold, dealt vehicles as well as bought ones, but not the cars or the loan quad, and never the regular quads below two; the sale is a page on the cork board, with the buyer collecting the vehicle next morning; the price follows condition; job pay stays at £60 for now; the bought cap goes from 2 to 4; a sold vehicle's saved state is dropped. Also settled earlier: the starting purse is random, £200 to £600 in tens, from the farm seed; the money is shown with a toast when a job pays, on the end-of-day card and in the logbook.

- **Selling** (`classifieds.js`): `sellOffer(specId, { wear, bodyWear, faults })` is the going rate times `sellCeil` 0.7 down to `sellFloor` 0.4, by a badness of `0.5 * engine wear + 0.2 * body wear + 0.3 * min(1, faults / 3)`, with notes of what pulled it down. The first draft was 0.8 and 0.55 and was an exploit: a worn ad at the cheap end of its 12% spread, resold at once, made a profit. 0.7 and 0.4 are below the cheapest ad at every wear, and a test checks it for every model across 300 ads each.
- **The sale** (`Market.sell`): the money now (`today.sold`), then a bought vehicle is just removed from `bought`, and a dealt one goes on `sold`, which is saved straight away. Next morning `withoutSold` takes those models out of the fleet and `sold` is cleared; `fleetIds` is saved without them, and as `captureFarm` only walks the units that exist, the vehicle's saved state goes too. A sold model is advertised again (it is no longer on the farm), so you can buy a fresh one back. `sellWhyNot` refuses: not for sale, already sold, or below `keepRegulars` 2 of the five regular quads.
- **The page** (`classifiedsView.js`): `S` and `B` turn between the ads and the sell page; a number arms a row ("press it again to sell") and the same number sells, so a stray press doesn't sell a tractor. It reads `game.sellable()`, which takes each unit's engine wear, spec `bodyWear` and the faults that have started.
- **Money shown:** a toast on every job (`+£60 for the job: ... £310 in the tin`), `moneyFacts` on the recap card (the tin, and earned, spent and sold today, counted in `Market.today`, not saved), and logbook entries (`deal` kind: "Red, sold for £420"; a bought vehicle is written under its model's name).
- **Starting purse:** `dealPurse` (`startPurseMin` 200, `startPurseMax` 600) from `farmSeed ^ 0x7ea5`; a saved purse is put back over it, and a save from before buying existed has none, so it is dealt one.
- **Not done:** the girls' reactions and the tin in the barn (#299), the names of the models (#296, #297), several of a model (#298), and a gamepad route (still the number keys). Job pay and prices are unchanged apart from the sell rates. **Not checked in the running game:** the sell page, a sold vehicle leaving, and the toast timing.

## The money tin and the girls on the money (#299)

Jamie's answers: a detailed biscuit tin on a wall shelf under the cork board; `E` lifts the lid and a card shows the balance, today's money and the last five deals, with the contents growing with the balance; a procedural coin chink; remarks (not chooser offers) for a purchase, a sale, a vehicle arriving, an ad on the board, the tin low or high and, rarely, a job paying, Amy careful and Jess keen to spend; about 40 to 50 lines recorded (it came to 42), a debug button per remark and a console line.

- **The tin** (`src/world/moneyTin.js`, tests in `tests/moneyTin.test.js`; the view in `moneyTinView.js`): built from plain boxes and cylinders (no rounded boxes: a notch where two meet is the lesson of the vehicles), 0.26 m by 0.17 m by 0.07 m. A tartan lid printed on a canvas (a made-up maker, no real brand), hinged on the back top edge and opening 1.65 radians, a paper liner, rolled rim, base bead. Five additive layers of contents (`tinLevel` from `TIN_STEPS` 100, 400, 1,000, 2,500); the last piles notes over the rim, so **the contents are hidden while the lid is shut** (a first screenshot showed them poking through it). A test keeps every layer inside the walls and the lower four under the rim.
- **Place:** a plank and two brackets on the back wall at z + 3.75, 0.8 m up (the shelf's top), so the lid standing open clears the cork board's frame at 1.11 m and you look down into the tin. Seen headless: the closed tin reads from the board's distance, the lid text runs along the long side, and the open tin shows its contents at standing height. Not seen: it at night, or the bracket legs close up.
- **The card** reuses the cork board's close-view styles (`classifieds.css`). The ledger is `Market.ledger`, the last five buys and sells, kept in the save (old saves have none).
- **Remarks** (`marketTalk.js`, pure, tested): `moneyBought`, `moneySold` and `moneyIn` are called where they happen (`game.saySomethingAboutMoney`, the nearest free girl, with a chance); `moneyAd`, `moneyLow`, `moneyHigh` and `moneyArrived` come from `update`, from the state of things, each with its own cooldown, `moneyAd` once for each set of ads and `moneyArrived` once a day. A bought vehicle now records the day it was bought (`boughtDay`), so `Market.arrivals(day)` knows which turned up this morning. No line names a vehicle: they are recorded and cannot carry a name at runtime.
- **Not done:** a notice for the chink (the girls do not turn to the sound), the tin physically losing money in view when you pay, and the girls' view of whether an ad is worth buying (Jess is keen at every ad). The numbers (£60, £1,500, the gaps and chances) are first guesses.


## The neighbour's farm over the lane (#29, was TODO 134)

- Jamie, before building: put it along the lane (recommended); a small yard first, with the yard, gates, house and barn detailed enough; lending and borrowing split off as #315; she speaks, in a new third voice (a new Kokoro British reference, older and calmer than the girls); the vehicles are scenery, not farm vehicles.
- **Where it ended up, and why not where we said.** "Past the ford" on the hill lane has nothing there: the lane ends in the pasture, and the pasture's hedges close the world. The public lane (`layout.publicLane`, x 206.4) already has the tarmac, the traffic and the visitors, and the farm ends at the big gate, so the yard is on its west side, south of the farm's hedge (x 146 to 203, z 92 to 148, the lane at z 104), inside the 420 m detailed terrain so it has real ground and physics. The lane's near edge had an invisible wall along all of it, so nothing could reach it: `laneBarriers` (`neighbourFarm.js`, tested by flood-filling from the big gate) now leaves gaps in it at the big gate and along the neighbour's wall, adds a wall along its far side and ends it 62 m north of the big gate and 56 m south of the neighbour's gateway (`publicLane.open`, z -60 to 160). The ends are invisible walls like the edge was; passing traffic goes through them, as it went through the edge's. Beyond them the lane is as it was.
- **Ground.** The yard is levelled inside its wall to the lie of the land at its middle and eased out over 4 m (`createNeighbourFarm().floorAt`, folded into `createOutbuildings` so `builtAt`, `covered` and the terrain's levelling all count it; no new calls elsewhere). No distant trees are within 40 m of the farm's hedges, so none stand in it.
- **Drawing.** About 800 boxes would have been as many draw calls twice over, so `mergeStatic` bakes everything the group holds into one mesh per material; the three vehicles (`buildVehicleBody`, split out of `TrafficView.take`) stay separate. Measured with `perf.mjs` from the spawn view, which was already over budget before this (3,231 calls, 3.9M triangles, against 2,600 and 3.5M): the first version added 419 calls and 156,000 triangles; with the merge and the group hidden beyond 230 m from the camera it is 3,234 and 3.95M, the spawn view being 240 m off. Load time to "Ready" is 2.55 s, as without it (2.54 to 2.56). The yard itself is 833 meshes unmerged, 190,000 triangles, mostly the vehicles.
- **She** is `Neighbour` (pure, `neighbourFarm.js`), not a companion: no brain, no needs, no offers. She stands, turns to the nearest of you and the girls (within `lookRange`), greets (`greetRange`, then not again for `greetEvery`), chats while you are on foot close by (`chatRange`, `chatEvery`), says goodbye (`farewellRange`, after `nearForFarewell` s) and goes in at `indoorsDark`. The tunables are in "Neighbour" in the Tune tab's World group. Her rig is the girls' model with grey hair and a plum jumper, her wave a raised right arm from the rig's `offsets` (not checked by eye in motion).
- **Her voice.** A third bank, `companion-voice-neighbour` (24 lines, `NEIGHBOUR_VOICE` in `src/npc/neighbourLines.js`), recorded by `tools/generate_companion_voice.py --character neighbour` (it reads `NEIGHBOUR_VOICE` in place of a companion; the reference is `tools/voices/neighbour-ref.wav`, Kokoro bf_emma, Apache 2.0, exaggeration lowered by 0.1).
