She plays through `CompanionSounds` with that bank, so unrecorded lines would fall back to captions only; all 24 recorded without a miss. Captions come from `hud.caption`. The girls' two new keys (`neighbourFarm`, `neighbourWave`) are merged into Amy's and Jess's sets like the traffic lines and are recorded with the rest.
- **The girls.** On a greeting she raises a `traffic` notice (key `neighbour`, strength 1) so the existing look and wave rules apply; three seconds later the nearest free girl may say `neighbourWave`, and 2.5 s after you ride in, one says `neighbourFarm`. Nothing new in the notices, reactions or availability, and they do not walk over to her.
- **The vehicles** (Series, buggy, tractor, from the farm's own models) are scenery with one box collider each, not drivable and not farm vehicles, so not in `FOR_SALE` or `NOT_FOR_SALE` (those list the farm's own). Making them drivable or lendable is #315's.
- **Map:** the yard, its house and barn, and a name; the key moved west to x 38 to 140 (still over the 100 by 80 m a test asks for) to stay clear of the yard.
- **Checked:** unit tests (the yard's shape, the lane's walls by flood fill, her behaviour and her lines), the smoke test, the perf and load times above, and one headless pass from the lane, the gateway, the yard and the barn door, and beside her. Not checked: her wave, how she looks as she turns, the sound of her voice in the game, the yard at night (the windows' and the lamp's glow), riding the lane past passing traffic, and a girl driving onto the lane (nothing sends her there).
- **Not done:** lending and borrowing (#315), the girls going over to her, the neighbour's visits to the barn ending at her yard, and the vehicles being drivable.

### Second pass, after Jamie looked (#29 follow-up)

- **The gate** is a real `Gate` (`id: 'neighbour'`, pushed onto `props.gates`, so `TrafficHost` gives it the E prompt, the swing, the "in the way" refusal and the shared "Gates" tuning). Two five-bar leaves hung on pins at the gateposts' inner faces, meeting 6 cm short of the middle with a latch, shut at the start (a collider across the gateway until it is half open), swinging in to the yard. They were swung back, flat and fixed, before.
- **Concrete and the barn floor.** The slab stood 6 cm above the terrain (which is level at `y0` inside the wall), so every tyre was in it. Its top is now 1.5 cm above `y0` and the vehicles stand on it. The barn's own floor slab, whose top was at the yard slab's, is gone (the yard's concrete runs in under the door): that was the z-fighting.
- **The lane's camber.** `floorAt` eased the levelling out over 4 m on every side, which on the east side reached across the whole tarmac (its edge is 1.1 m from the wall); the lane at z 140 is 1.5 m higher than the yard, so it was dragged down into a cross-fall. On the east it now eases over 1 m, except within 6 to 20 m of the gateway, where the full 4 m ramps the lane up into the yard. A test checks the terrain under the tarmac is exactly what it is without the yard, for z 125 to 145.
- **Detail.** `neighbourTextures.js`: grained timber (porch posts, gate), weathered timber (bench, gate leaves), painted frames, a panelled green door with a letterbox, sash glazing bars on every window (the glow comes from an emissive map of just the panes, so the bars stay dark and the day glass is not lit), stone cills and lintels, ribbed wheelie bins and a barrel, bark with sawn rings on the logs, and a postbox face with a slot, hood and plate (a cipher, not a real maker). The porch is rebuilt so it joins: posts on stone bases, a beam across and a side beam each back to the wall, a lean-to slate roof whose underside is the beams' top and which meets the wall, with a bargeboard. Draw calls stay at baseline (3,237 against 3,231).
- **The tractor** has its bale spike hidden (`poseBaleSpike(object, 0, false)`).
- **Asked and not done yet:** where the stream goes (it ends at the pasture's east hedge, x 34, 110 m from the yard, and nothing carries it on), driving her vehicles (#315), and the tree: the plain low-poly trees round the yard are the world's distant tree clumps, not anything of hers.

### The brook, and the trees (#29 follow-up)

- Jamie: where does the stream flow to out of the farm, as it should be near the neighbour's? The land answers it: the pasture's stream is the lowest ground about (about -2.4 m at its east end), and the ground rises east to the lane (about 6 m), so the water has to come from the neighbour's side, not go to it. The stream is now the brook's west end: `brook.js` (pure, `layout.hill.brook`) carries it on from the hedge (the same line and depth at x 34, so no step; the hill's own channel stops there) east across the country, past the south of her yard (at least 5 m clear of the levelled ground), under the lane and on to a spring 20 m beyond. `createTerrainHeight` cuts it into the ground (`cutAt`) and counts it in `puddleAt` (so brakes, air filters and the engine's gulp see water as they do in the pasture); the ribbon is drawn with the stream's own material, so it rises, browns and flows with it. A test checks it never climbs more than a metre against the flow over 20 m, and starts 4 m or more higher at the spring than at the hedge.
- **The culvert.** There is no channel across the tarmac and its verges (`culvert.half`, 4.5 m either side of the lane's middle), easing back to full over 4 m, so the road is exactly as level as before (tested). A stone headwall with a concrete pipe mouth stands each side (`brookProps.js`, each with a collider). The west one is behind the invisible wall along the lane's west edge, so nothing can ride into it; the water stops 0.4 m short of each pipe because the ribbon pinches out as the channel ends.
- **The map** draws it from the hedge to the edge of the map; the key box covers part of it.
- **The tree.** The plain green low-poly trees near the lane were the world's distant clumps (`buildDistantTrees`), two of them 33 and 40 m east of the lane. None now stands within 45 m of her wall (`nearNeighbourYard`); the rest are unchanged.
- **Checked** by unit test and the smoke test, and headless stills of the hedge, the open country, the culvert from both sides and the lane. **Not checked:** the brook's colour in rain or at night, and riding the lane past the headwall.


## Seasons across games (#30, was TODO 135)

Jamie settled: the farm has its own real calendar (real month lengths, 365 days, no leap years), not today's date; a new farm starts on a date dealt from its farm seed and every game is the next day; everything in the proposal except spring blossom and new job kinds; a few seasonal girl lines; `?season=` takes all four; old saves land in the right place from their day number, so nothing breaks.

- **The date** (`world/seasons.js`, pure, `tests/seasons.test.js`): `startDayOfYear` deals a day of the year from `farmSeed ^ 0x5ea5`, and `farmDate({ start, day })` gives the month, day of the month, year, weekday and season of farm day `day`. Seasons are the meteorological ones (March to May spring, and so on). The farm date is separate from the game's day number, so a save needs nothing new: `day` and `farmSeed` already say where the farm is in its year. A year is 365 games, so most farms stay in one season for 90 games or so; `?season=` and the Girls tab's "Load it as..." buttons are how to see the others (`reloadAsSeason` loads again with the parameter, because the season shapes the world as it is built). A forced season is the middle of that season (15 January, 15 April, 15 July, 15 October).
- **The weather pool.** `SEASON_WEATHER` holds multipliers on each kind's weight (`pickWeatherKind`) and on each temperature band's odds (`pickTemperature`). Winter has snow (3 times its weight), fog, rain and overcast; summer has no snow, no frost, more thunder (2.5 times) and a new `hot` band (22 to 29 degrees) that only a summer sunny or fair day can draw; autumn is wetter and foggier; spring is showery. Seeded games use the same tables, so a seed still repeats, but a seed now deals a different day than before.
- **Daylight.** `daylightFor(dayOfYear)` runs sunrise and sunset along a cosine from the shortest day (21 December: 7.8 hours of daylight, sun 0.24 rad high) to the longest (21 June: 16.2 hours, 1.0 rad), 10.3 hours in mid-October as the old fixed day was. `daylightParams` copies the defaults with those, and keeps the start window at least 1.5 hours before sunset so a winter game does not begin in the dark. Calling it a day is allowed from the earlier of `endDayHour` and a quarter of an hour before sunset.
- **The ground.** `SEASON_GROUND`: `wet` is the least the ground dries to (`Weather.groundWet`: winter 0.4, spring 0.25, autumn 0.2, summer 0), so the mud and the bogs follow the existing wetness; `dust` multiplies the stubble dust (`dust.js` `seasonDust`: summer 1.4, winter 0.1).
- **The trees.** Autumn is now the autumn season, in place of the old one day in six (`autumnToday` and `AUTUMN_CHANCE` are gone); everything built on `game.autumn` is as it was. Winter dulls and darkens the leaves with `WINTER_TINTS` through the same tint pass. Not done: bare trees (the models have no bare version), spring blossom, and summer-only decoration.
- **The calendar** (`wallCalendar.js`): the page is the real month, the days under their weekdays (up to six rows, so the row height is 33 px), crossed off up to today. Every farm used to start in April with 30-day pages.
- **The girls** (`npc/seasonLines.js`): each says one remark a game about the time of year, 45 seconds after the weather remark (`companionVoice.watchWeather`, from `weather.season`). Recorded with the rest.
- **The panel and console.** `weather.season` (settable: loads again with the season), `weather.date`; the Girls tab's farm folder shows the date and has a button per season. The dust factor shows in the Dust tuning.
- **Left for later:** hay and baling are #31, which can key on `game.season`. Jobs on the board are unchanged.
- Checked: unit tests for the calendar, the seasons, the odds and the lines. See the summary for what was not looked at.

## Haymaking (#31, was TODO 136)

Settled with Jamie before building: one pull request; haymaking is a chalkboard job in stages (mow, bale, load, stack), turning the hay left out; the north stubble field; no separate mower or baler vehicles (the tractor working at pace does the job, a towed implement is a later issue, #143 the topper and #94 the harrow); loose bales are plain props, never Rapier bodies; the girls carry the bales to the trailer as a new chooser offer, Jess faster and clumsier; the farm remembers the cut state and the grass grows back over days; about ten lines each.

- **The patch** (`LAYOUT.hayMeadow`, 32 by 18 m at x 100 to 132, z -26 to -8) is the part of the barley field just inside its first gateway: all stubble with nothing built on it, clear of the field's wet hollow and short of the Dutch barn (tests check each). It is a grid of 2 m cells, each standing, cut or baled (`world/haymaking.js`, pure, `tests/haymaking.test.js`). The patch is drawn as one mesh of cells over the stubble (`world/hayView.js`), green, pale when cut and a shade darker when baled; 144 cells, so nothing to draw-distance.
- **Stages.** `Haymaking.phase` is derived, never stored: `cut` (60 cells mown), `bale` (8 bales left, one per 7 cells of cut hay driven over), `load` (8 bales on the trailer), `stack` (trailer stopped in the Dutch barn's open bay) and `done`. The tractor must be running and between 0.5 and 3.6 m/s, whoever is driving it (`updateHay` in game.js feeds it the quad's position, heading and speed, so a girl driving does the job as well). A swath is 1.6 m either side. The job needs the tractor and room for eight more bales in the barn (`STACK_CAPACITY` 16), so it drops off the board when the barn is full; a day's board is dealt as before, and the job is on it or not.
- **The girls** (`npc/hayLoading.js`, pure, tested): `hayLoad` is an offer in `OFFER_KINDS` (purpose 0.9), made while the load stage is on, a bale lies loose within 12 m of the trailer and the bed has room. `handleHayLoad` claims the nearest bale and the next free slot (so two girls never choose the same), `sneakTo`s to it, `startCarry('squareBale')` (a new `CARRY_ITEMS` kind, heavy, `balePace` 0.8), `carryTo`s the trailer's side opposite her slot and throws: the bale flies on an arc over `throwSeconds` 0.55 and sits in the slot, parented to the trailer's body so it rides with it. If she is called away mid-carry, the bale is set down where she stood and goes back to being loose; a claim not finished in 40 s lapses. Per-girl numbers are params, not rules: `hayLoadWeight` (Amy 1, Jess 1.3), `hayMissChance` (0.03, 0.22: a miss lands the bale at her feet and she picks it up again) and `hayCooldown` (5 s, 1.5 s). She is held (`c.hold('hay')`) while on it, so nothing else takes her.
- **You** can press `E` at a loose bale within 7 m of the trailer to heave it on yourself, so the job never waits on the girls.
- **The trailer** takes sixteen bales, rows from the back (`trailerSlots`, four rows of two across, a second layer above), clear of the front stack the model already has and of the winch. It is not a physics body, so the bales are children of its body and nothing else changes; the tow weight is not changed by them (not tuned: the tipping trailer's `towWeight` pattern is the place if it should).
- **Stacking.** Stopped in the Dutch barn's open bay (or 3 m outside it), the loaded bales leave the trailer and appear along the bay's back wall (`stackSpots`, four to a row, up to four high). `stacked` is kept.
- **Remembering.** `captureFarm` keeps `hay: { cut, cutDay, stacked }`: the cut cells as indexes, the farm day they were cut and the bales in the barn. On load, cut cells older than `regrowDays` 3 are standing again (the next load is the next day), younger ones stay cut, so a half-mown patch is still half mown tomorrow and the job starts from it. The stack never grows back to nothing. Not breaking: it is an extra key, and a save without it is read as a fresh patch.
- **The rest.** New lines in `npc/hayLines.js` (seven situations, two or three each, recorded: `hayCut`, `hayBaled`, `hayGo`, `hayThrown`, `hayMiss`, `hayLoaded`, `hayDone`). A bucket list entry, Hay maker. FAQ: how to make hay, why it will not cut, why the girls will not load, whether it grows back. Debug: "Haymaking" and "Loading the hay" in the Tune tab, the Girls tab's Jobs folder (jump to any stage, grow the patch back, empty the stack) and "loads a hay bale" under each girl's "Make her do"; the console's `farm.hay`, `farm.hayStacked` and `farm.hayDone`.
- **Checked:** unit tests for the rules, the slots, the saving and regrowing and the girls' offers; the smoke test; and once headless (the stage jumped to "load", the trailer beside the bales, both girls sent): both picked up and carried a bale, Amy's went up on the trailer and Jess's fell short, landed in front of her, was picked up again and then went up; jumping to "stack" with the trailer in the bay stacked the bales, ticked the job off and put the eight in the barn, with no error and nothing the frame guard caught. A bug that run found: the "room on the trailer" test treated slot 0 as full, so nobody was ever offered a bale. **Not checked:** the look of any of it (the patch's colours, the bales on the trailer, the carry and throw poses, the stack in the bay); mowing and baling with the real tractor on the real field (the stage logic was driven directly); the pace of the whole job by feel; and the new lines by ear.


## A show day (#34, was TODO 139)

Settled with Jamie: the show is on the last Sunday of a month, not a random day. The day ends at the show instead of the plain recap (no showground to ride to); the judged quad is the one last ridden; marks out of 10 for clean, tidy and running sweet; the girls' slow race is a result, not a live race; rosette kept and shown in the barn; a first ticks a bucket list item; a little favour for entering; no money.

- **When** (`src/farm/showDay.js`, pure, tested): `isShowDay(date)` is a Sunday (the farm date's weekday 6) whose day of the month plus 7 runs past the month's length; `isShowEve` is the Saturday before, in the same month. The farm's weeks start wherever its first day falls, so the calendar's Sundays are the farm's, and a test checks every year has twelve shows. The calendar draws a red dot under the date.
- **Going to it:** pressing E on the calendar on show day, with the usual rules (evening, both girls at the barn), judges the quad, saves, and opens the recap card with a show section on top (headline place, three marks and the total, who won the slow race). The recap and the fade carry on as before. A `showForced` debug flag, in the Girls tab's farm folder and as the console's `showday`, makes any day one.
- **Judging:** `judgeQuad`. Clean is 10 less ten times the mud coat (the worse of body and wheels) over `cleanMudAt` 0.5, so half a coat is nothing. Tidy loses `bodyWearCost` 6 for a fully worn body and `damageCost` 2 for each puncture, bent rod or bent rim. Running sweet loses `engineWearCost` 5 for full engine wear, `foulingCost` 2 for a fully fouled plug, `faultCost` 2 per started fault and 1 for a battery under `flatBatteryBelow` 0.25. These are guesses, not judged by feel. Only a quad entry counts (a tractor or car last ridden means no entry; the girls still race).
- **The others:** four entries, totals dealt between 14 and 27 out of 30 from the farm seed, the year and the month, so the same every time you load that month. A tie goes to you.
- **The slow race:** one roll from the farm seed and the day: Amy wins `amyPatience` 0.6 of the time. Each girl has a spoken line for winning and losing (`showSlowWon`, `showSlowLost`), Amy's at the card opening and Jess's three seconds later as the dayDone lines are. The evening before, a free girl says the show is tomorrow (`showEve`), and on the day itself `showToday`, once, after `showEveAfter` 25 s.
- **Kept:** `shows` in the farm save: the last 12 results (day, month, year, place, total, entries, quad, who won the race), one per day. Older saves lack it and load fine. Not a breaking change.
- **In the barn:** `src/world/trophyShelf.js`, a small shelf on the back wall under the calendar, with a rosette on a nail above it for the latest eight, coloured by place.
- **Fuller show (Jamie: yes to a reasons, a preview, the girls entering, a drawn rosette and a records line; the classes and a ridden showground are separate issues):**
  - **Reasons:** `judgeQuad` returns `notes` per mark (mud on the body, on the wheels, a worn body, punctures or bent parts, a worn engine, a fouled plug, faults, a flat battery) and the card prints them after each mark, or "spotless".
  - **Preview:** on show day the calendar's prompt names the entry and says what it would score now and which mark loses most (`previewText`), whether or not you can call it a day yet.
  - **The girls enter:** each girl's own quad (`homeQuad` or the one she is on) is judged by the same rules, unless it is the one you are entering. `runShow` ranks everyone with the rivals (a tie goes to you, then the girls, then the rivals); the card lists the ranking.
  - **Speech:** a queue (`showSpeech`): the slow race lines for Amy at once and Jess `recapJessDelay` later, then the best girl ahead of you says `showAheadOfYou`, or the worst girl behind you says `showBehindYou`, a gap after that.
  - **Rivals** gain `rivalYearly` 0.8 marks a farm year after the first, capped at 29.
  - **Records board:** a "Show rosettes" line ("2 firsts, 5 placed"), through `Records.extra` and `allLines()`; with six rows the board's rows are 52 px apart, not 58.
  - **Rosette:** an inline SVG on the card (`rosetteSvg`), and the card scrolls if it is taller than the screen.
- **Not checked:** the shelf, the rosettes, the calendar dot, the extra records row and the card by eye, a girl's quad being picked correctly when she is off riding another, and the new voice lines by ear.

## Spraying the field (#36, was TODO 141)

Settled with Jamie before building: a sprayer tank with a fold-out boom and a job spraying a field in straight overlapping lines, with your lines drawn on the farm map afterwards. Changed from the issue's wording on one point: it is a trailer hitched with `J` like the others, not a tank on the quad's racks. It also goes on the jobs board, but not every day, and once the field is sprayed it stays sprayed for a while and the job is not offered until it is needed again. Finite tank, refilled from the hose; the girls get a few lines and a notice reaction, nothing in the chooser.

- **Rules** (`src/world/spraying.js`, pure, `tests/spraying.test.js`). The field (`LAYOUT.sprayField`, 32 by 24 m of the south wheat stubble, clear of the muck heap, the hay patch and the gateways; a test checks) is a grid of 1 m cells, each carrying a dose. Boom out, sprayer hitched, moving between `minSpeed` 0.6 and `maxSpeed` 3.6 m/s: the 6 m bar hangs `boomBehind` 1.3 m behind the axle, and every half-cell of travel puts that much dose on each cell under it. One straight pass gives about 1 on a cell (sprayed from 0.6), two overlapping passes about 2 (doubled from 1.7, drawn darker). Forwards or backwards, it does not matter. A jump of more than 5 m between frames (the trailer being placed) is ignored rather than swept.
- **Done** at `coverNeeded` 88% of the cells sprayed, so a few missed edges are forgiven but a stripe down the middle is not. No score: how much overlap you had is only what the map shows. I considered scoring straightness and rewards and left them for a later issue.
- **The tank** lasts `tankMetres` 240 m of boom travel with the spray on, whether or not it is over the field, so spraying the hedge wastes it. A field of 768 m² at 6 m wide is about 128 m of perfect lines, so a tank has roughly 90% slack for turns and overlap and a sloppy go runs dry (one `empty` event, a toast and a line, then it stops until it is filled). The hose fills it in `fillSeconds` 14: hold `E` at the sprayer with the hose in your hand (an interaction on the sprayer's proxy, only while the hose is held). The hose reel is 25 m from where the sprayer stands (a test checks it is within reach with a metre to spare). I left out a separate water butt.
- **The trailer** (`vehicle/sprayerTrailer.js`, `sprayerModel.js`). A copy of the tipping trailer's hitching and motion (`TrailerMotion`, `TrailerCoat`, `TrailerObstacle`) without the tub, seats or cargo; any quad with a rack, the tractor or a Land Rover can pull it (`canTow`), not only the green quad. The model: galvanised frame and A-frame tongue as the others, a cream poly tank on two cradles with a green lid, a pump, a sight tube whose blue column shrinks as the tank empties, and a lattice boom with six nozzles a side. `poseSprayer` folds the arms from straight up (in) to flat (out) over `foldSeconds` 3.5; the arms and nozzles are `userData.keep` so they are not merged away. A full tank counts as `towWeight` 2.5 things in the tow load. `T` (from the seat or standing within 3 m) folds the boom; the status line says what to do next. It stands at `LAYOUT.sprayer` in the yard, east of the yard's spawn spots, with a 4.5 m spawn keep-out (the fleet test caught the first place, which sat on a yard spot).
- **The look** (`world/sprayView.js`). The field is one mesh of 768 cells over the stubble with RGBA vertex colours (clear, a damp dark green, a darker green), lit, so the daylight dims it. The mist is 220 points falling from the nozzles; being its own colour it is dimmed by `setLight(1 - darkness)` each frame, as CLAUDE.md asks for fixed-colour effects.
- **The board.** `FARM_JOBS.spray` (needs `sprayField`). The game deals the day's board from the jobs there are, and leaves `spray` out when `sprayWanted(save.spray, day)` says the field was done fewer than `lastsDays` 6 farm days ago, so it is dealt at random like the rest and is off for a week after a done field. Dealing a new job into the pool changes which jobs an existing seed gets. The job ticks off at `done`; spraying a field that is not on the board still counts, it just pays nothing.
- **Remembering.** `captureFarm` keeps `spray: { tank, since, doneDay, cells }`, the cells as a digit each in quarters of a dose (rounded up so a cell keeps its state). On load, spray older than `lastsDays` has worn off, whether it was a done field (from the day it was done) or a part-sprayed one (from the day it was started). The tank is kept either way. A save without it is read as a fresh field with a full tank, so it is not breaking.
- **The map.** `MapView.drawSprayed` draws the lines in green strips, darker where they overlap, over the ground and under the markers, and the field's outline (dashed) while the job is on the board and not done. The sprayer is a trailer marker.
- **The girls.** `npc/sprayLines.js`: `sprayStart` (the first cells sprayed), `sprayEmpty` (once per empty) and `sprayDone`, three lines each, Amy telling you to overlap and Jess joking about the smell. New notice kind `spray` (every 8 s while spraying, range 25 m, `heard: false`) with a `turnAway` reaction within 8 m at 50%, so a girl standing close turns her face away from the mist.
- **Debug.** Tune: "Spraying", "Sprayer trailer", "Sprayer trailer wear" and "Sprayer trailer mud" (in `tuneCategories.js`). Girls tab, Jobs folder: what the spraying is doing, and buttons for boom out and in, empty and fill the tank, spray half the field, job done and clear the field (all `Game.debugSpray`). Console: `farm.spray`, `farm.sprayTank`, `farm.sprayBoom`, `farm.sprayDone`.
- **Not done:** a spraying sound (the hose's hiss is not reused), a bucket list entry, the girls doing the spraying or helping fill the tank, a water butt as a second place to fill, and a score for straight lines. The tank is not a vehicle's lasting condition, so it is in the farm save rather than `captureVehicle`.

## Animals that need you (#41, was TODO 146)

Settled with Jamie: feeding is a chalkboard job on well under half of days (35%, not 70%); you carry the feed from the barn and hold `E` at the run and the sty; the pig is fenced and the hens are in a run; hunger is simple, with no penalty for never feeding; a running engine near the hens scatters them and the pig only grunts; the girls get lines; sounds are synthesised; hunger is not saved.

- **Logic** (`src/world/farmAnimals.js`, pure, tests in `tests/farmAnimals.test.js`). `FarmAnimals` holds four hens, a pig, a hunger for each lot (`startHunger` 0.35 at dawn, `hungerSeconds` 900 to starving, `hungryAt` 0.6) and the feeding. Hens peck, stroll and clamp to their run; a vehicle with its engine running (or moving faster than 1 m/s) within `scatterRadius` 9 m plus 1.2 m per m/s of its speed sends them running away from it for `panicSeconds` 3, and one `scatter` event goes out per `scatterCool` 6 s. A hungry lot with you within `crowdReach` 12 m crowds the fence nearest you; the pig squeals for you only when hungry and you are within 60 m, and grunts when fed. `feeding(which, dt, bucket)` takes `feedSeconds` 4 of holding and `feedUse` 0.45 of a bucket per lot, so one full bucket does both; letting go loses a half-done feed (progress ebbs at 0.3 a second when not fed that frame).
- **The job** (`FARM_JOBS.feed`, `feedWanted`, `feedChance` in the Job board tuning). Dealt from the farm seed and the day, so it differs by day and repeats on a reload, and added with `JobBoard.add` on top of the day's two (as the service job is), not drawn from the pool: drawn from the pool, a 35% chance would have shown on only about a seventh of days. `game.js` filters it out of `availableJobs`' pool for that reason. Pays `jobPay` like the rest.
- **Where.** `layout.farmAnimals`: the run (6.5 by 7 m) and sty (4.5 by 5 m) west of the barn between the paddock and the silage, clear of the west hedge; the bin against the barn's north wall and the bucket beside it. Both are in `spawnKeepOut`, so no vehicle starts in them, and a test keeps them off the barn, silage, paddock and hedge.
- **The item** (`src/items/feedBucket.js`). Carried like the oil and coolant bottles: `handsFull`, `G` to put down, status line, the map's item markers, the console's `feed-bucket`. The bin is a galvanised bin with a lid, with a proxy for `E`.
- **The look** (`src/world/farmAnimalsView.js`), all procedural with ordinary lit materials so the daylight dims them. The fences are static Rapier boxes (solid to you and to vehicles, not to the animals, which keep to their rectangles). Not tiled or drawn-distance registered: a handful of meshes in one spot.
- **Sound** (`src/audio/animalAudio.js`), synthesised on `WildlifeAudio`: cluck, scatter squawk with a clatter of wings, squeal, grunt.
- **The girls** (`farmJobLines.js`): `feedStart` (the first scoop of a game, only with the job on the board), `feedDone`, `hensScatter`, said by the girl nearest the place through `saySomethingAboutJob`, which now takes the place as a third argument. Amy tells you to switch the engine off; Jess thinks the chaos is wonderful. Recorded, banks committed.
- **Panel and console.** Tune: "Farm animals", "Feed bucket". Girls tab, "Animals, gates and the lane": hunger and what each is doing, and buttons to make them hungry, fill the bucket, scatter the hens, make the pig squeal, put the job on the board, feed them both and start a fresh day. Console: `farm.hensHunger`, `farm.pigHunger`, `farm.feedBucket`, `farm.feedJob`, `farm.hensScatter`, `to animals`.
- **Cost.** `smoke`: spawn 3.25M triangles, yard 3.80M, village 3.66M, wood 3.87M, hilltop 6.78M: no change from main.
- **Not done.** Saving hunger between loads (not asked for); the girls feeding them on their own (it would be an offer in the chooser); the hens laying, the pig getting out, the animals reacting to you walking up on foot.
- **Checked:** unit tests and the smoke test. **Not checked in the running game:** how the hens, pig, run, sty and coop look, whether the fence and the animals sit right on the ground, the sounds by ear, the pick-up and fill feel, whether the site is clear of everything by eye, or the frame time (`perf.mjs` not run).


## A muddy lane that changes (#42, TODO 147)

Decided with you: tyres wear the lane like a field but harder, a rut is shading, grip and bog rather than a dip in the ground, and the farm save keeps the lane's wear only.

- The wear grid (`groundWear.js`) used to stop at the grass field's south hedge, so the lane (z 80 to 134) never wore. Its bounds are now `fields.wearBounds`: the farm plus the lane, the two fields beside it and the pasture, 296 by 283 m, about 21k cells of 2 m against 11k before.
- `fields.laneAt` marks the lane: 1 inside its banks' 2.1 m half width, fading over 1.5 m. There `laneWear` (3) multiplies the wear, `laneRecovery` (0.2) the recovery, and `laneSoak` (0.6) makes it hold the wet like a hollow, so a rained-on lane bogs from far fewer passes than a field needs. Recovery is the rate that matters for "drying back over days": 0.0008 a second becomes 0.00016, about 100 minutes of dry weather from full wear to sound.
- Real dips would mean rebuilding the physics terrain while you play, so the ruts are drawn by the existing soft and bog shader and felt as grip and drag, and the girls plan round the worst of it as they do any bog.
- The save has `lane`: flat index and byte pairs for lane cells that are worn (a few hundred numbers). `laneKept` (0.5, "Farm that remembers") of it is back next morning, as mud is. Field and pasture wear is not kept. An older save has no `lane` and starts sound; no version bump.
- Panel and console: "Rut the green lane to a bog" and "Dry the green lane out" in the Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm", and `lane` in the console. The three lane tunables are in "Ground wear".

## The village (#43, was TODO 148)

Decided with you: the village is a short ride south down the public lane, with a side road that turns off to the west and bends (a straight street would have shown all of it from the turning); a 20 mph limit with signs; the names Nether Thornby, the Plough and Harrow, Pargeter's Stores (Post Office) and Hollis Motors; fuel from the pumps and treats from the shop paid out of the money tin; the girls asking for a run to the pub. Parts at the garage (#357, below) and pub banter and a drink (#358) are separate items.

- **Where.** `layout.village`: the road leaves the lane at z 171, 67 m past the neighbour's gateway, south of the brook (the culvert is at z 158; every footprint is checked to stand more than 4 m south of the brook's bank). `publicLane.open.maxZ` went from 160 to 200, so you ride on past the turning. Buildings face the road with `yaw` 0 or Math.PI only, so a footprint is a plain rectangle and `inRect` and the colliders stay simple. The road's centre line is a Catmull-Rom through 11 points; the village's area has drystone walls on its north, south and west and the lane's own barrier on the east (the barrier's west side has a gap at the turning, `laneGap`).
- **Ground.** The land rises about 0.1 m per metre going south here (the farm's rise term), which put the car park 2.3 m under the road at its mouth. Within the village's walls the ground is laid to a plane along the road (the natural level at the turning to the natural level at the road's end, so a slope of about 4% along it and none across), easing out over 9 m; `terrainHeight.js` splits `natural()` out so the plane has something to be laid against. On top of that, each building and the forecourt is levelled to the plane at its middle over 3.5 m, the car park to the plane at its mouth over 7 m. The road, pavements and kerbs are drawn over the ground on a 1 m step, 7.5 cm and 16 cm up, the road with a polygon offset over the lane's tarmac so the two do not fight where it meets it, widening by 3.4 m over its first 11 m.
- **Looks.** Each building is made from the kit in `villageKit.js` (a shell and eaved roof, barge boards, gutters, chimney stacks with pots, sash windows with frame, cill and lintel, bays, doors with a surround and fanlight, porches, dormers), built in its own frame and merged into one mesh per material (a few draw calls a building, not one a piece). Textures are drawn in code (`villageTextures.js`: brick, clay tiles, paving, the car park's bays, signwriting, the pub's painted sign, the road signs, the pumps' faces, the price board, the shop's stock, the awning, the roller door). Windows, lamps and lit signs glow after dark by raising an emissive with the darkness (`setNight`), never a light, as the neighbour's house does. The whole village is hidden beyond 260 m (`drawDistance`, "Village" in the Tune tab).
- **Buying.** Pumps: hold E with a can in your hands. The litres are billed in whole pounds as they go in (`PumpBill`, the fraction under a pound carrying to the next fill, so a fill never takes more than it was worth) and stop when the tin cannot pay the next pound. `Market.spend` is the small-money counterpart of `buy`. Shop: the sweets jar and the pasty warmer on the counter are looked at and E is pressed. Each treat raises both girls' favour, a pasty also takes 0.7 off their warmth need, and `Till` makes you wait 120 s (sweets) or 180 s (pasty) before the same thing counts again, so it cannot be farmed for favour. Prices and effects are in `DEFAULT_VILLAGE_PARAMS`.
- **The girls.** A new request kind, `pub`: a girl who is free, near you, with a free seat on a quad's back may ask (the chooser's offer, as the bales); it is done when she is on the back of a quad you ride, stopped within 9 m of `village.pub.stop`, and lapses like the others. The `askPub`, `thanksPub`, `remindPub` and `grudgePub` lines (three each for Amy and Jess) are in `requestLines.js` and recorded. The pub's picnic tables add rest spots of a new `bench` kind (a bench each side, 0.52 m up), which a free girl near them may sit on in fine weather, like the bales and the fence.
- **Cost.** `smoke`: spawn 3.25M triangles, yard 3.80M, village 3.66M, wood 3.87M, hilltop 6.78M: no change from main.
- **Not done.** The farm map does not show the village (its bounds are the farm's and the hill's), and the pub's door does not open: it is a painted door in a solid wall for now. #358 is the girls at the pub.
- **Debug and console.** The Girls tab's "Both girls and the farm" has the village's description and three buttons to put you on foot at the pub's car park, the shop or the pumps; `village` in the console has the same (`now`, `pub`, `shop`, `garage`); the Girls tab's "Make her do" has "asks to be taken to the village pub".

## Parts at the village garage (#357)

Decided without asking: the garage sells four parts over a rack on its front wall (between the roller door and the office) and the tyre stack by the door, each a click target like the shop's counter: spark plug £6, inner tube £12, drive belt £28 and a quad tyre £38. Those are the cheap, commonly used ones; jets, pistons and the other tyre sizes stay a post order. The prices sit well under the classifieds' rates (a quad is about £500) so a part is pocket money, and they are in the Village group in the Tune tab.

- **Straight to the shelf.** A part bought goes onto the barn's spares shelf at once (`Spares.give`), not into a carried bag. That is the point of buying rather than ordering: the post van takes about five minutes and the order costs nothing, so the garage trades money for time. Carrying a bag home was more machinery than the item is worth.
- **Refusals.** The tin must cover it, and the shelf must have room counting what is on order (`maxOnShelf`, as ordering does), so the garage cannot be used to overfill it. Both are in `villageShop.js` (`partWhyNot`, `buyPart`) with tests.
- **Oil is not sold.** The farm's oil bottle never runs out, so there is nothing to restock; a shop bottle would be a second source for no reason.
- **Debug and console.** `village parts` in the console and "Take you to the garage parts rack" in the Girls tab put you on foot at the rack. The prices are tuning.

## Tarmac on the lane and in the village (#363)

The public lane and Nether Thornby's streets rode like grass after #43, because `fields.tarmacAt` knew only the farm road. They now count as tarmac or concrete, so `surfaceFeel`, the tyre squeal, tread wear, the ground-wear exemption and `roadMud` treat them as the farm road.

- **What counts.** The lane from `minZ` to `maxZ` and `halfWidth` either side of its middle; the village road out to its pavements' outer edge (`curb`), the pub's car park and the garage forecourt. The same soft edge, 0.15 m each side. `villageShape.tarmacAt` is only worked out within a metre of the village's walls, so the farm's wheels do not pay for the road's 100-odd samples.
- **Road mud.** `RoadMudSet` holds the farm road's grid plus one over the lane you can ride (`open`) and one over the village's ground west of the lane's. A point of tarmac belongs to one grid; a tyre anywhere else uses the farm road's grid, which loses what it sheds. They share one set of params, so "Road mud" in the Tune tab sets them all.
- **Not drawn.** The terrain shader draws only the farm road's grid, and the lane and the village road are their own meshes, so mud shed there is slippery but not seen. Drawing it means a second smear texture on those meshes; left for later.

## Challenges on the chalkboard (#44, was TODO 149)

Agreed with Jamie before building (all as recommended): three challenges listed under the records in the chalkboards' close view, only you attempt them, the girls react with a line, bests saved with the farm (`challenges` key in the save, new keys only, so not breaking).

- **Why no new slate.** The records slate has no room (five lines plus the show's) and the wall is full (photos, jobs, calendar, records, small ads), so they are a section on the card. `src/world/challenges.js` holds the pure parts: `Challenges` (bests), `ColdStartRun`, `CleanWatch`, `judgeLineUp`.
- **Cold start.** "Cold" is a quad whose engine has not run since the day began (`ranToday` in `game.js`, one set per load, which is one day). `1` takes the cold ones, the clock runs `coldLimit` 300 s and each counts once seen running, so one that stalls again does not undo it. Under two cold quads it will not start. The girls starting one counts, because telling who started an engine is not worth the plumbing.
- **Line-up.** Agreed as "offset, heading and gap evenness"; with painted bays the gaps are fixed, so it is offset and heading only. `layout.lineUp` is four bays at the yard's front, nose east, checked clear of the spawn keep-out and the track by a test. Four perfect is 100, so a missing quad costs 25. Each bay takes the nearest eligible quad.
- **Clean climb.** `CleanWatch` fails the run on any frame the engine is stopped or `isTipped`; it resets when your run starts. A clean finish also submits to the `cleanClimb` best, on top of the climb record.
- **Lines.** `challengeDone` and `challengeBest`, said by the first free girl. A clean climb leaves the climb's own line alone.

## Garage fixes found testing (#367)

Jamie's first look at the garage (testing #367) found a run of faults, fixed together; the canopy and forecourt were filed separately.

- **Brick seam.** `brickTexture` had 4.6 bricks across its tile, so every metre of wall showed where it ended and started. It is now five, so it joins. The gable ends used a 1.5 m tile and started their courses at the eaves, against the wall's 1.2 m from the ground; they now match, so the brick runs unbroken up into the gable.
- **Signs are the shape of their texture.** The garage's name board was 12.6:1 on an 8:1 texture and the canopy's fascia 24:1: both stretched. The name board is 10.7:1 on a 2048 by 192 texture, and the canopy's name is a lit panel of 8:1 in the middle of a plain navy band. The roller door is 2.6 m high (was 3.2) so its shutter box clears the name board, which stays under the eaves.
- **Windows.** The side window was landscape on a portrait sash texture, so it stretched and its bars crossed the frame's own. It is 0.95 by 1.2 now, like the houses'.
- **Parts rack.** A "PARTS FOR SALE" board over it, packets with a printed front (name and a picture of the part) as a plane in front of each box, and the packets stand on the shelf rather than level with its top (they were z-fighting).
- **Pumps.** The face is a seven-segment display for the money and the litres (red) and the price in pence a litre (green, "152.9"), each with a small caption beside it. The nozzle hangs in a holster fixed to the pump's side and its hose runs from the nozzle's back into the pump's base.
- **Price board.** At the east end of the frontage, off the road, turned to face traffic coming in from the lane (it was in the road, square to the pavement).
- **Drums.** Plain painted steel with rolling hoops and a lid (the old ones used the shed barrel's ribs and read as coils). A mesh with a material array is dropped by the village's mesh merge, so a drum is separate meshes.
- **Prompts and sound at the village's counters.** A prompt that returns a string is an E action and the HUD puts the key on it ("E Buy a spark plug"); `{ text }` is plain text with no key, which is right for a refusal but was wrong for the buying prompts. Buying at the rack or the shop now chinks where you stand (`CoinAudio.chinkAt`; the tin's own chink is at the tin, a long way off) and the toast says what happened ("Bought a spark plug for £6. It is on the barn's rack now (4 there)"), while the prompt carries the count and the price.

## The river and its meadow (#49, was TODO 154)

- Jamie, before building: the river had to extend towards the village and must not vanish at the edge of the farm map. The land answers it: the detailed terrain stops at 210 m and the pasture and village fill it, so there is no room for a new river, and a river that leaves the map has to come from somewhere. So the river is the brook (#29) widened, not a second water: it already runs on east to the culvert under the lane and a spring, with nothing to hide at the edge, and the map already draws it.
- **Shape.** `layout.hill.river`: from the pasture's east hedge (x 34, as narrow as the stream so there is no step) it widens over 22 m to `halfWidth` 5.5 (11 m across), 1.3 m deep with 0.95 m of water, stays full to x 100 and narrows back to the brook by x 124. `brook.js` mixes the width, the cut and the water by `riverK`; the bed is raised in the crossing (`crossK`, x 66, 4.5 m either side, 3 m ramps) until 0.2 m stand over it, with the surface kept where it was. A wide river on ground that rises 1 m across it would have one bank under the water, so `terrainHeight.js` levels the ground across the valley to the ground at the river's middle (`levelK`, out 7 m beyond the banks).
- **Drowning.** `puddleAt` is relative to the water's own depth, so a 0.2 m wade read as full depth. `waterAt` now scales by real depth inside the river (`deepFrom` 0.45 m), so the crossing reads 0.3, under the engine's `waterDepthFrom` 0.55, and the deep reads 1. The existing rule needs speed (`waterSafeSpeed` 4 m/s), but 0.9 m of water is over any intake, so `deepWaterGulp` (new `waterDeepRate` 1.4 a second, a second to stall, a snorkel scales it) floods an engine in the river's deep at any speed. `world.deepWaterAt` feeds it, and only for a wheel with puddle above 0.8.
- **The meadow.** `layout.hill.riverside`, x 34 to 80, z 118 to 203, hedged round (its west side is the pasture's and the sheep field's east hedge), with a 4.8 m gateway in the pasture's, 14 m up from the stream, linked in `fields.js` so the girls route through it. At x 80 the east hedge has a gap spanning the water that a sheep hurdle shuts (a collider across, rails above the surface), so the hedge does not grow in the river, nothing rides out and the water runs on. The ground drops 12 m from the pasture's flank to the water, steep only in the south-west corner.
- **Looks.** Eight white posts with a red band (four a line, in the shallows and on the banks), gravel speckled over the crossing, willows (a leaning trunk, a ragged crown and 64 hanging strips, four of them), reeds along both banks, the farm's oaks in the corners, a muddy landing on each bank through `mudAt` (grip, not a drawn patch). The water is the stream's own ribbon, so it rises and browns with the rain. All in `riverside`, a set piece in `farDetail.js`.
- **Map.** The meadow, the river and a "River crossing" label. The key moved to the open ground north-east of the farm: the meadow took the strip it stood in, and the test that keeps it clear of every field still holds.
- **Girls.** `riverWatch.js` (pure, tested): `riverNear` when you come within 16 m of the crossing (not again for 400 s) and `riverStall` when the deep water has drowned the engine you are on (60 s), said by whoever is near and free, Jess 55% of the time when both are. Two lines each, both girls, recorded. Tune: "River remarks".
- Debug: `river` in the console (`now`), `tp me to river` to get there, and the same readout in the panel's "Both girls and the farm" folder.

## Sleepers mark the line-up spaces (#464)

- Jamie, testing #381: four white painted bays looked uneven, sat badly in the grass and are not what a barn would have. They are gone, with the lines and the `MeshBasicMaterial` they used.
- **Looks.** Four old railway sleepers (2.6 m by 25 cm by 15 cm, a little shorter, turned and tilted each), laid flat across the nose end of each space and half sunk, with a stepped worn top, grey creosote grain with splits, knots and lichen drawn in code, and a rusty chair plate and bolt. Two meshes (timber, iron) in a group named `lineUpSleepers`, which is in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`.
- **Place.** `layout.lineUp` is now at (-44, 75), nose south (yaw 0) to the south hedge, `spacing` 3 m (it was 2.6, so slightly uneven parking still scores well) and `length` 3 m. The sleeper is about 1.5 m past a space's middle, about 2.5 m from the hedge's face. It is between the barn's extension doorway (12 m west) and the field gate (about 6 m east of the last sleeper). The barn doors are clear. The scoring is unchanged: `bayPlaces` still gives each space's middle and heading, and four tidy quads are 100.
- **Moved to make room.** The bale trailer is at (-42, 37), up the yard past the sprayer: the old spot was 3 m from the row, and the yard's parking and away spots need 8 m clear of it, which leaves very little room that is not already taken. The jerry can's "by the field gate" spot moved a little west and north-east of the row's end, to (-33.4, 76.6).
- The girls' own tidy row in front of the barn doors (`npc/lineUp.js`) is a separate thing and stays where it was.

## The Fern Trail (#50, was TODO 155)

- Jamie: a separate single-track loop in the wood, clear of the Hollow Run and "big enough"; anything narrow enough may go on it (the trees do the policing, there is no rule for it); roots as bumps plus painted roots; one hoppable log; blue-banded way-marker posts and a named line on the map; the girls stay out of it.
- **Where** (`layout.woods.trail`, `woods.js`, tests in `tests/woodsTrail.test.js`): a closed loop of 27 control points, 390 m round, 2 m wide (`halfWidth` 1). The Hollow Run fills the wood with rows 25 m apart and never comes within 11 m of itself, so the trail runs in the strip along the north hedge and the gaps between rows, and crosses the Run in four places (a test counts them). It starts 3 m beside the lead-in, where the sign is.
- **Ground** (`profile`, `mudAt`): a bed 6 cm deep, two dips (14 m and 12 m long, 0.55 m and 0.45 m deep, muddy 0.55 against 0.12 on the rest of the path), no ruts. The old nearest-segment grid is now `nearestOf(loop, reach)`, used for the Run and the trail. Oaks keep 1.1 m off the path's edge (bushes 0.5 m), tighter than the Run's 2.6 m, so the turns have trunks to steer round; the sign and the log keep trees off too.
- **Roots** (`trail.roots`, `trailProps.js`): clusters of three every 23 m from 12 m round, none at a crossing, the log or a dip; 36 in all. The ground mesh is 1 m, too coarse for a root, so each is a tube mesh with a low cylinder collider (radius 0.07 to 0.09, top about 0.13 m up) that a wheel rolls over, and the ground texture paints them too, plus a few that run off the path.
- **The log** (`trail.hop`): 0.4 m thick and 2.6 m long, so it covers the path, a cylinder collider with its top about 0.3 m up (half sunk), so it is rolled over by a quad and hopped by a bike. A test holds it clear of the Run.
- **Posts and sign:** a post every 28 m (11 of them) on the right of the way on, facing back along it, with a blue band and an arrow disc; none at a crossing. The sign at the start has the name and "Follow the blue posts" (replaced by the trail marker, #846). The posts are merged into one mesh per material (the wood's draw calls went from 1,335 to 1,279 against a budget of 1,330 once they were). Group `trailMarkers` is in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`. Ferns (13 fronds, some russet, 75% in autumn) and brambles go through `tiledInstances` with the rough draw distance.
- **Map and console:** a dashed pale line and the label "The Fern Trail"; `tp me to fern` goes to the sign. Nothing is saved. No tunables, so no panel entries.
- Looks: one look in summer and autumn. The first round showed the roots as dark trenches (their tube wound inside out, and sunk too far) and the sign text cut off at the sides; both fixed.

## The gravel pit (#51, was TODO 156)

- Jamie, before building: put it in the unused ground north-east, past the wood's east side, through a gate in the barley field's north hedge; the haul road can be ridden in and out and the far wall is a real test; a `pit climb` on the records board and no chalkboard challenge yet; one line each from the girls on coming near and when your engine stops down in it; a shallow pond (it wets, it does not drown); a map label and a gate the girls route through. "Big enough, random enough and fun enough."
- **Shape.** `layout.quarry`: the field (the wood's east hedge is its west side, new hedges on the north and east, drawn coarse because nothing rides there) and its gate. `quarry.js` is pure: `dealPit` deals the pit from `farmSeed ^ 0x6a17` (`dealtLayout`, called in `game.js` before the world is built, so the farm's pit is the same every day; `this.seed` is now made before the date so a new farm's seed is the one it keeps): an ellipse 84 to 90 by 58 to 64 m with a ragged rim (three harmonics), turned up to 0.3 rad, 8 to 9.5 m deep. The ground is levelled to the lie at its middle over 14 m beyond the lip (and the field's distant rise is switched off, as the wood's is), the bowl cut as a lip, a middle at about 1.2 times the average slope and a scree toe (`bankShape`).
- **Walls.** The run from lip to floor is 14.5 to 16.5 m on the easy walls (about 30 degrees, 38 at their steepest), 10.4 to 11.8 on the steep wall (about 38 average, up to 47) with two chutes (15 to 17 m run, gullies about 30 to 35 degrees) and three buttresses (8 to 9 m, 45 and more) in it, none within 0.2 rad of another. The climb goes up between the chutes, its gates `gateHalf` (13 or so) wide to take both in.
- **Haul road.** From the gate straight to the lip on the side facing it, then a ledge down the wall at the depth the bank has at that distance (the inverse of `bankShape`), 5.4 m wide with a 6 m shoulder, a grade of 8 to 13% on the wall (measured 7.5 to 15), 135 m long. Its height is laid over the bowl's (`groundAt`), so it cuts into the wall and fills over the outside, and no point on it is steeper than 14 degrees (a test).
- **Surface.** `gravelAt` (0.7 floor, 1 walls, 0.75 road) feeds `surfaceFeel`: `gravelGrip` 0.72 and `gravelRoll` 1.2 at full looseness, so about 0.72 times grass on the walls and 0.8 on the floor. The pond (0.75 m cut, 0.35 m of water) is the wood's, so `puddleAt` and `mudAt` already work; no new drowning.
- **Pit climb.** `world/pitClimb.js` wraps `ClimbRun` (the hill climb's) for you alone: ask with Enter within 16 m of the start cones, ride through, one gate, the finish over the lip; out after 8 s stopped or 120 s. Kind `pit` on the records board (the board's rows shrink to 46 px at seven). Checked once in the running game: a Bruin 660 takes the first chute in about 3 s at 23 mph, an Ursa 450 to the lip, the Adder and the Asp roll on the way up, and the buttress stalls a Bruin at 1.7 m.
- **Cost.** `smoke` against the base commit: spawn 3.24 to 3.16M triangles, yard 3.55 to 3.59M (2,148 to 2,427 calls), village 3.71 to 3.96M, wood 3.87 to 4.09M (limit 4.1M, 1,278 to 1,255 calls), hilltop 6.81 to 6.78M. Everything in the field but the hedges is one set piece, `quarrySite`; the far hedges are coarse (2.2 m apart, 1.5 times as wide, no bush blobs: they were 190k triangles). Budgets unchanged.
- Girls: `quarryWatch.js` (pure, tested): `near` at the lip (not again for 400 s) and `stall` when your engine stops down in the pit (60 s), plus `pitYou` and `pitRecord` from whoever is near the top when you finish. Four lines each, both girls, recorded (appended last in the line sets so only the final banks changed). A follower waits inside the gate (`pastGate`, `waitSpot`).

## Kids on mini motos on the lane (#52, was TODO 157)

- Jamie, before building: the riders are children on the girls' rig scaled to 1.3 m (not a silhouette), on mini motos, because the model exists (the issue said pushbikes); about one group in twelve minutes, by day only; the girls' showing off is a weight on the chooser's offers, not a timer; four new lines.
- `world/kids.js` is pure (`tests/kids.test.js`). `KidsGroup` is one to three riders in single file on the lane's near side (`Drive` at 2 m turning circle, `kidsSpeed` 5 m/s). Within 18 m of the big gate it decides once: if you or a girl (anywhere, on foot or on a quad: `TrafficHost.watchers`, never a parked quad) is within `watchRange` 30 m of the gate, they stop level with it, each 2.2 m behind the last, turn to face in and watch up to `watchSeconds` 25, leaving `watchGrace` 6 s after the last watcher goes out of range. Otherwise they just ride by.
- `Kids` deals a group about every `kidsEvery` 720 s (the first no sooner than `firstKids` 240 s, plus half a wait), one at a time, not when `darkness` is over 0.3 or it rains or frosts. It lives in the host, not in `Traffic`, so the cars' tests are untouched.
- Showing off: `showOffBoost` multiplies the weight of `SHOWY_OFFERS` (showOff, jumps, doughnuts, race, raceDare, climb, cones, bigGo) by `kidsShowOff` 1.5 while they watch, in the same product `updateMinds` already builds. A girl who picks one says `kidsShowOff` (70%). Needs and the chooser are otherwise untouched.
- Girls: a `kids` notice (pass at 0.5, watching at 1) with the same `wave` reaction as the lane's traffic; lines `kidsPass`, `kidsWatch`, `kidsShowOff` and `kidsLeave` in `npc/trafficLines.js`.
- View: `moto` in `trafficView.js` is the TZ 65 upright on its wheels (the same `buildVehicleBody`), the rider sat on its anchor with `SitIdle`. Riders get a jumper from five colours and a bike from five paints. Not kept in the farm save.

## A shepherd's hut on the hill (#53, was TODO 158)

Settled with Jamie before building: a traditional wheeled hut you can walk into, a stove you light with `E` that burns a few game hours and goes out, tea from the kettle (hold `E`, the same mug as the rack's tea), and the girls using it for shelter, as a ride destination and a romance place, all in one item. I proposed a terrace low on the pasture and moved it, below.

- **Where.** On the flat top of the pasture at (-60, 196.5), door end facing north over the farm, 38 m west of the flask rock. The first plan was a terrace 20 m up from the foot, but the only ground flatter than slope 0.1 within 7 m is the top (slope 0.04 there, 0.2 to 0.5 on the lower banks): cutting a pad into a bank made the next bank's steepest slope 1.1 to 1.7 where `tests/hill.test.js` allows 0.85, whichever fade I gave it. The top needs a small level pad only (`level` 2.5 m beyond the walls, `fade` 5; `terrainHeight.js` takes the ground at its middle). Bracken, gorse, tussocks and sheep keep 8 m clear (`hill.nearHut`).
- **The hut** (`hutBuild.js`, procedural): 4 by 2.2 m, floor 0.56 m up on four iron wheels (door-end pair 0.5 m, front pair 0.4 m), sage-green boards with cream corner boards, a curved corrugated roof (rusty, over timber ribs, cream fascias, iron gutters), a half-stable door with the top leaf swung open, one four-pane window, two risers of 0.28 m (under the player's 0.35 autostep), a drawbar propped on a stone, a water butt, boots, and inside a bunk with a blanket and pillow, a cast-iron stove on slate with a flue through the roof, a kettle, a shelf with a mug, tea tin and candle, a stool, a log basket, a rug. Colliders: floor slab, walls with a 0.9 m doorway (the player's capsule is 0.6), steps, wheels, drawbar. Named `shepherdsHut` in `farDetail.js`'s set pieces. `world.covered` takes the roof, so rain stops under it.
- **Stove and kettle** (`hut.js`, `Stove`, pure, `tests/hut.test.js`): burns `burnSeconds` 900 (about three game hours at 12 times), a log adds `logSeconds` 450 up to `maxSeconds` 1800, heat rises over `warmUp` 45 s and falls over `coolDown` 300 s; warm from 0.35. The kettle boils in `boilSeconds` 25 on a warm stove and a mug takes `pourSeconds` 2.2 held (let go and it restarts). Warm inside the walls, and within `warmRadius` 2.5 m outside. The light is the pool one made at the start (`lightCandela` 14, a fifth by day, reach 7 m), the stove door and the window glow, the smoke is the bonfire's emitter. The crackle and the pour are procedural (`hutAudio.js`).
- **Girls** (`npc/hutStay.js`, pure, `tests/hutStay.test.js`): an offer `hut` in `OFFER_KINDS` (warmth 0.9, rest 0.7, company 0.4, purpose 0.3) weighed by rain, cold (under 12 degrees) and dark, and shy of a cold stove on a mild bright day. Quads stop at the foot, so she walks: `goRest` with a spot of kind `hut`, a `stand` at the foot of the steps and `via` points up the steps and in at the door, the loft ladder's mechanism. She sits on the bunk's edge, lights the stove if it is out and the weather wants it (or she was asked), puts a log on when it runs under 150 s, says `hutSit`, `hutWarm` or `hutRain`, `hutRomance` (favoured and you within 5 m) and `hutDone`, after 150 s. Favour 1.5, plus 1.2 if favoured, plus 0.5 if you made tea while she sat there. The hut's box is in `detourBuildings`, so she walks round it. Sat in the hut she counts as covered and, with the stove warm, warmed (`needsContext`). Three bucket-list entries. Lines in `hutLines.js`, recorded.
- **Ride destination.** She walks up from the foot where she waits when you ride up, and E at the doorway asks the nearest free girl. I did not add the hut to the sight-seeing outings: they are driven and the girls will not drive past the foot.
- **Debug.** Tune "Hut stove" and "Staying in the hut"; the Girls tab's jobs folder shows the stove and lights and puts it out; each girl's "Make her do" has `hut`; the console has `farm.hut`, `farm.hutLit` and `tp me to hut` (the foot of the steps).
- **Cost.** The model is about 6,300 triangles, merged by material into 39 meshes (a hundred and ten before: the hilltop spot's draw calls went from 1,512 to 1,564 against the limit of 1,650; triangles there did not move). It is left out by distance as a set piece, so no other spot sees it. One point light and one smoke emitter are kept all game. The hut draws from its own random stream: sharing the props' one moved every tree built after it and the wood spot's triangle count with it.

## The day's story, written (#55, was TODO 160)

Settled with Jamie: a short paragraph on the recap card above the logbook, written from templates by a third-person narrator who names the girls (text only, so nothing to record), and the last ten days' stories kept and readable on the wall calendar.

- `src/farm/dayStory.js` (pure, `tests/dayStory.test.js`): `DayEvents` is today's log, and `writeStory` takes the `storySentences` 4 most notable events (weighted: a falling out 6, a seizure or the show 5, someone stranded 4, a race 3, making up 2; the earliest wins a tie), tells them in the order they happened with a wording picked from a seeded stream (`farmSeed` and the day, so the same day reads the same), then fills spare room with the day's totals (jobs off the chalkboard, bogs, kilometres). A day with nothing says it was quiet.
- Where events come from: a race's result, you finishing a race, `handleFallingOut` (fell out, made up), `onStranded` on a girl's first call (bogged or tipped, naming the quad), and a per-frame edge on an engine's `seized` (primed on the first frame so a seizure carried over from yesterday is not today's news). The show's place is added when you call it a day. Events are not saved; the finished story is.
- Kept: `stories` in the farm save (`{ day, text }`, `storiesKept` 10, a day told twice replaces itself). An old save has none, which is fine, so the save version is unchanged.
- Reading back: `E` on the calendar opens `StoriesView` (the tin's card frame, `B` or `Esc` closes) whenever it isn't time to call it a day and there is a story to read; otherwise `E` calls it a day as before.
- Debug: `farm.story` in the console and "the day's story so far" in the Girls tab's farm folder; "Farm that remembers" tuning has `storySentences` and `storiesKept`.

## Tracks through long grass (#63, was TODO 168)

Settled without asking (the issue was clear): only the hay patch has long grass, so only it takes tracks; cut and baled cells show none.

- **Pure grid** (`src/world/grassTracks.js`, tests in `tests/grassTracks.test.js`, tunables under "Grass tracks"): a 0.25 m grid over the patch (128 by 72, 9,216 points) holding how flat each point is, 0 standing to 1 pressed. A wheel on the move (over `minSpeed` 0.3 m/s) presses every point within `width` 0.28 m, full in the middle of the tyre and easing off to the edge, so a line has a soft side and no gaps at 12 m/s whichever way it falls across the grid. It lifts at 1 / `springSeconds` (420) a second, so the oldest end goes first and a line is gone in seven minutes.
- **Where it is fed:** `quad.js`, next to the ground wear, for every wheel in contact, so the girls' quads, the tractor and the cars count as well as yours (`world.grassTracks`, set in `setUpHay`). A second pass over the same line does not deepen it.
- **How it is drawn** (`hayView.js`): a copy of the patch skin's own triangles lifted 1.5 cm, with a 128 by 72 texture whose colour is a pale yellow-green (176, 194, 118) and whose alpha is flatness times `lay` 0.65 (a quarter less strong than the first try, which read as white paint from above), only where the cell still stands. Redrawn five times a second while anything is pressed, never otherwise. One mesh, one draw call, nothing to draw-distance.
- **Not kept:** the lines are not in the farm save, as they are gone in minutes; a new load starts with the grass standing.
- **Debug:** the Girls tab's Jobs folder has "Hay: lift the tracks in the long grass"; the console has `farm.hayTracks` (what is pressed) and `farm.hayTracksLifted`.
- **Measured:** one look in the running game, the real red quad driven in a line and a turn: two soft tyre-wide lines, a curve through the turn, none off the patch.

## Wet tarmac that shines (#64, was TODO 169)

Settled without asking (the issue was clear): one shine for all the tarmac, not puddles, and the tyres leave no wet lines.

- **State** (`src/world/roadWet.js`, tests in `tests/roadWet.test.js`, tunables under "Wet road"): `RoadWet` holds 0..1 how wet the tarmac is, separate from the ground's `weather.wet`, which never falls below the season's damp. Rain soaks it in about 25 s; dry it loses it over 1500 s under cloud, faster by sun (up to 4 times) and wind (up to 2 times), a third as fast at freezing. A game that starts on a wet day starts with it wet.
- **Where the tarmac is:** `tarmacMask` bakes `fields.tarmacAt` over the whole 420 m terrain at 0.5 m a texel (a 840 by 840 one-channel texture, about 45 ms at load), because the road-mud grid covers only the farm road.
- **How it is drawn:** in the terrain shader, wet tarmac is 45% darker, roughness 0.08, and adds the horizon's colour by a Fresnel term (more when looking along it, `skyTint` fed from the sky each frame) on top of the environment map. Each spot has its own dry point from the detail texture's low frequencies, so it dries in patches. Frost, snow and mud smears reduce it.
- **Debug:** the console has `weather.roadWet`, the Girls tab's day folder a "wet tarmac (shine)" slider.
- **Looked at once** in the running game at 17:30: dry, half dry and fully wet, from eye height and low: dull dark grey, then pale patches, then a glossy sky-grey. The wet grip of tarmac was already in `surfaceFeel`; it is not changed.

## Steamed-up windows (#72, was TODO 177)

Asked Jamie about a key for wiping the glass; the answer assumed `T`, but `T` already works the tailgate or back door from the seat in the Tor, the Crag and the Griffin, and `X` already steps the wipers (#103). So there is no new key: the wipers clear the mist, and the sleeve and the finger are the girls'.

- **State** (`src/effects/cabMist.js`, pure, tests in `tests/cabMist.test.js`, tunables under "Mist inside the cab"): the windscreen is a grid of 1.5 cm cells over the rain glass's panes (`createCabMist`), each 0 clear to 1 fogged, off-glass cells (the Land Rover's centre post, a car's corners) never fogging. The side windows and the back are one number, `rest`.
- **Fogging:** people aboard times how cold the air is (0 at 16 °C, full at 3 °C) times how wet the day is, over `fogSeconds` (130 s for one person, wet and cold). The top and the edges fog up to 2.2 times as fast. Nobody aboard and no heat dries it over ten minutes.
- **Clearing:** the heater blows from the engine's warmth (from 0.3, full at 0.8) from three demister slots in a widening fan (`fanBase`, `fanSpread`), slower higher up; cells outside the fan go at 12% of the pace; the other glass at 25%, so it is last. A fully warm engine clears the screen in about two minutes, the back in about three. Running wipers wipe the sector they sweep (the same blade shapes as the rain's, `wiperGeometry`). Heavy mist (over 55%) runs drops down, each leaving a clear streak.
- **The girls:** a girl aboard says `screenMisty` after the screen has been over 50% for 3 s while someone drives, then 3 s later Amy wipes her side with her sleeve (a smeared patch, `wipeSwipe`) or Jess draws a smiley (`drawLine`), saying `screenWipe`. Once per spell: the screen has to drop under 20% first. Four new lines each (`temperatureLines.js`).
- **Drawn** (`cabMistView.js`): a plane 3 mm inside the glass on the model's `rainGlass` group, textured from the grid (RGBA `DataTexture`, redrawn at most ten times a second, only for cabs within 40 m), lit like the world so it dims at night. The side and back glass get a copy of the material per vehicle (marked with `markGlass`) that goes whiter and less see-through. The world outside is not blurred separately: the fog layer does it.
- **Debug:** the Vehicles tab has "Mist up the glass of its cab" and "Clear the mist off its cab" and a watched value; the console has `vehicle.screenMist` and the verbs `mist` and `demist`.

## The village road's seams (#535, from testing #388 and #390)

Decided without asking, from Jamie's list of faults on the road itself.

- **Junction.** The road's mesh began at the lane's middle (x 206.4), so its tarmac, with its own texture and a polygon offset, was drawn over half the lane and its dashed line. It now starts at the lane's west edge (`villageShape.laneEdge`, `mouth`): vertices east of the edge are clamped to it (`clampX` in `roadStrip`), so the flared mouth meets the lane in a bellmouth with the lane's own tarmac under it and nothing drawn over.
- **Markings.** The full-width line 13 m in is gone. The road is two-way: a broken centre line (2 m in 6) and one give-way line, a double broken line across the north half only (the left going east, the traffic leaving the village), just inside the mouth (`roadMarks` in `village.js`, painted by `paintMarks`). The pale edge lines stop at the car park's entry as well as the garage.
- **Car park.** `layout.village.pub.carPark.entry` (x 110 to 116) is a dropped kerb: the north pavement, its kerb and the edge line stop there, and the apron's far edge follows the road's edge, tucked 0.4 m under it, so tarmac meets tarmac.
- **Garage.** The forecourt slab was a flat box ending at z 184 while the road's south edge wanders between z 183.4 and 185.2, so the road overlapped it in places and left a gap in others. It is now a mesh from the garage's front to the road's edge (tucked under, at the road's height), so the two are flush.
- **Road end.** The "road closed" board stands on two legs in the road in front of the gate (it hung in the gate's plane, and its frame now stands clear of its face). The wall behind the gate is left out of the picture, but its collider and longer hedge colliders stay, so there is no way round. Past the gate `beyondRoad` draws the road on (`layout.village.end.beyond`, 17 m, rising 5 m) between hedges 2.4 m over the road, bending south and ending behind a hedge and two oaks. It is only seen: it stands inside the meadow's hedge at x 80, so it stops short of that.
- **Cost.** About six small meshes and two oaks; smoke's draw-call and triangle budgets hold.

## The village shells: eaves, doors, porches, bays and trees (#538, from testing #388, #389 and #390)

Decided without asking, from Jamie's list of faults on the houses, shop and pub (all in `villageKit.js` where they were shared).

- **The white strip on every roof** was the soffit: a flat board at the wall's top that stuck out 0.4 m past the roof's slope, which falls away below the wall's top, so it showed above the tiles. It is now flat at the fascia's lower edge, under the roof. The gutter was a half-pipe the wrong way up (a dome); it is now a half-round trough open at the top (`m.gutter`, double-sided), with a swan neck to each downpipe.
- **The stretched window above each door** was a half-cylinder fanlight wearing the window's sash texture, and it stood under a porch's beam. Doors default to no fanlight; a cottage without a porch gets a transom of three dark panes (`m.fanGlass`, lit after dark), and the pub's and the pub side door's are gone (the signboard and the canopy cover that height).
- **Porch gable** was built facing along x (a gable end seen from the side) and in the wrong material; it now faces out, in the wall's material.
- **Bay windows**: the side panes were turned by the angle from the wrong axis, so they stood across the bay, not along it, and the base and hood were plain rectangles. The panes now run from the wall's ends to the front pane's, facing out (`tests/village.test.js` checks the ends meet, both ways round), and the base, sill and hood are the bay's own trapezoid.
- **Shop**: the awning covers the door only, clear of the bow window's hood and the fascia board; it no longer uses `transparent` with `alphaTest`, which sorted against the clear glass. The open door hangs from its jamb at 75 degrees on a deeper step instead of standing through it.
- **Trees and hedges**: the village's oaks are `layout.village.trees` and each keeps its crown (5.2 m a unit of scale) off every building (tested). The green's oak moved to the middle of the green. A cottage's garden is no deeper than the wall leaves room for (`gardenDepth`), so the north cottages' back hedges no longer run out over the brook, and the back hedge is a little taller and wider than the side hedges so their tops and ends never share a plane.

## The hay patch looks like a meadow (#549)

Settled with Jamie: it moves out of the barley stubble into grass, same 32 by 18 m and 2 m cells; the drawing is fixed first, then the move.

- **Drawn in the terrain shader** (`terrain.js`, `hayMask.js`, `tests/hayMask.test.js`): the old lifted skin of vertex-coloured cell quads is gone. `HayView` fills a 0.5 m picture of the cells (red cut, green baled, alpha how far in the patch, with a 4 m margin) and a second one of the pressed lines; the ground shader reads them through a noise wobble and threshold, so the edge and each cut cell's border are ragged and the meadow is one surface with the field. Long grass, cut stubble (with 3.2 m swath stripes), baled tan and pressed lines are all colours of the ground, so the tyres stand on it (the 6 cm lift that showed wheels through the grass is gone) and frost, snow and mud lie over it as over any field.
- **Long grass** is instanced tufts (14 a square metre, nine single-triangle blades each, about 9,000 over the patch and its margin, thinning past the edge) in tiles on `coverDistance`. The vertex shader reads the same pictures: a tuft's height goes to nothing where the grass is cut or beyond the edge, drops to a fifth along a tyre's line, and the tips sway with the wind. No CPU work per tuft when the state changes. About 80,000 triangles and 2 to 4 tiles in view.
- **No other ground cover grows in it:** the field's grass tufts skip the meadow and a 4 m margin (`inHayMeadow` in `props.js`), so none shows through cut hay.
- **The new place** (`layout.hayMeadow`: x 44 to 76, z 52 to 70) is the grass field's south-east corner. The east end beside the road's gateway was the first idea but the Home Loop runs along it; a scan for a 32 by 18 m rectangle clear of the track (plus 3 m), bogs, puddles, bales and the ramp, within a metre and a half of level, found only this one. A test keeps it so. The tractor now crosses the grass and the track to reach it, then the road to the Dutch barn.
- **Measured:** one look in the running game from the saddle, the air and above (standing, half cut and baled, a pressed line): the edge fades into the field, nothing stands in the cut part, the tyre line shows in the grass and the blades. The smoke test passes with the frame budget as before.

## A deeper pit with two roads (#554)

- Jamie, before building: about 20 m deep (it was 8 to 9.5), with more roads up and down. I proposed a lap road and a second, steeper one; building showed a lap can't share the wall with a second road (two ledges at one angle stack on top of each other), so it became two roads forking at the lip.
- **Size.** Dealt 18 to 22 m deep (`depth`). The field can't grow (its hedges are 10 m from the map's edge), so the pit is turned to run gate to steep wall (`yaw` about 90 degrees) and takes about 90 by 70 m at the lip. Every run scales with the depth: easy walls about 40 degrees (1.15 to 1.25 times the depth in run), the steep wall about 47, chutes about 35, buttresses about 50 and a little over. A run is capped at 0.78 of the rim's radius so the floor never closes up at the middle (the floor is about 50 by 25 m).
- **Roads.** `descend` in `createQuarry` makes one road from the lip point facing the gate, each way round (`roadPoints` and `rampPoints`, each `sweep` 2.7 to 2.8 rad), a ledge at the depth the bank has at that distance, following the wall's run before the chutes and buttresses (`baseRun`) so the line is smooth across them. The depth grows with the metres actually driven (six passes), so the grade is even: about 12 to 15 degrees measured (a test allows 19 at the worst sample), not the 8 to 13% of the old 9 m pit: 20 m down in 85 m of wall can't be gentler. The steep wall sits between the two feet. Each road lays its ledge in turn in `groundAt` (so the ground never steps between them) and they are kept more than a road's width apart (a test).
- **Rest of the pit.** Heaps are 3.8 to 6.2 m and smaller as the floor fills; the pond moves to the gate's side of the floor; the excavator stands by the first road's foot on the side away from the climb; the plant is tried at up to 16 angles and kept on the floor, off the water and clear of both roads. The pit climb is unchanged from the floor, now 20 m of wall.
- **Cost.** `smoke`: spawn 3.25M triangles, yard 3.80M, village 3.66M, wood 3.87M, hilltop 6.78M: no change from main.
- **Not done.** A lap or a bench across the steep wall (it would have cut the climb); a spur to the plant (it stands on the flat floor, nothing to build).

## The pit's walls and rocks stop looking broken (#555)

- **Walls.** Two causes of the pixelated look. The painted ground (about 10 pixels a metre) had the pit's grit drawn as 0.48 m jittered squares, which showed as blocks up close; the 0.48 m painted cells stay (0.18 and 0.26 m ones added 7 s to the load, enough to time the CI smoke test out) but differ far less from one another (`m` 0.92 to 1.02, was 0.86 to 1.08), so no block shows, and the grain below gives the texture. And a steep wall is stretched on a ground picture projected from above, so the terrain shader (`addDetailLayer`) lays fine grit over the pit's field only, sampled from the detail map in all three planes weighted by the face's lean (triplanar, at 2.6 and 9 a metre), so a wall is as sharp as the floor.
- **Rocks.** `rockGeometry` is an icosphere (detail 3) with its vertices welded (`mergeVertices`), pushed about by a smooth lump of its direction, a few flat faces cut where a quarried stone has split, a flat base, smooth normals and a tint that drifts over it; a test checks every edge is shared by exactly two triangles. Four shapes for the stones and three for the big ones, instanced as before, each sunk into the ground (lower again on a slope) so the scree comes up round it.
- **Cost.** `smoke`: spawn 3.25M to 3.27M triangles, wood 3.88M (unchanged), hilltop 6.78M to 6.79M. Budgets unchanged.
