The 17 come to the outline's 68.00 ha exactly: Hill Pasture 10.6, Brook Meadow 6.6, Top Field 6.3, Home Ground 4.5, Long Piece 4.4, the farmstead 4.3, North Field 4.2, South Field 4.0, Lane Field 3.5, Low Ley 3.3, Far Field 3.2, the pit's 3.0, the wood 2.9, the sheep field 2.5, Wet Meadow 2.4, Hay Meadow 2.0, the drive 0.5.
- **Boundaries** are made from the clipped fields' own sides: a side two fields share is one line, one only a field has is the outline. 43 lines, 8.5 km, the 7 round the sheep field and the hill walls. Walls are on the map and stop the girls' routes, but nothing is built for them until #696.
- **Gateways.** 20, each put on the line between its two fields nearest the plan's point and kept a metre plus its width from a corner (the furthest moved is 6.7 m). Two of the issue's were corrected from the geometry: (130, 215) is the wood's line with Wet Meadow, and the farmstead meets the sheep field only at a corner, so its way in is through Hay Meadow from the stone track's gate. Added so every field has a way in from the yard without Back Lane: Home Ground to North Field and to Wet Meadow (#684's point), Brook Meadow to Hill Pasture, Low Ley and South Field, the drive into the yard, onto the lane and into Lane Field and Long Piece, and the plan map's (240, 440) into the sheep field. The green lane is one gap between Far Field and the sheep field until #684 lays it.
- **Ground by kind.** Every arable field is stubble (barley and wheat in turn); `stubbleAt` goes by kind. The 60,000 clumps are shared over the six, so they stand thinner (#813 sets a budget). North Field has the hollow; the south field's rise is today's landform, not the field's, so it waits for #813's. Stubble's paint still fills each field's box (#813).
- **Measured** with `perf.mjs`, layout 1 against `--query layout=2` at the five spots: 97 to 229 more draw calls (the new hedges), triangles -5% to +16%, ms a frame within the run's noise (7.7 to 7.0, 14.8 to 13.5, 13.4 to 11.9, 10.5 to 11.0, 11.6 to 13.0), and 3.1 to 4.3 s to Ready for the bigger ground. Layout 1 is unchanged; the budget spots for the new map come with each move.

## The barn's flicker, ladder, tarp and jack, fixed before the move (#808)

- **Alpha fighting.** `coplanarOverlaps` found 48 pairs on the booster pack (the foot, case, end caps, lid and handle shared planes) and, in the loft's box, the doorway beam's top on the planks' top (the beam is `floorY + door.height + 0.3`, the loft's top is `floorY + 3.5`), the joists' ends on the open edge's beam, the top rail on the posts, the hay door's sill, posts and head, and the shutter's battens on its boards. Each part now stands at least 4 mm off its neighbour's plane (`tests/boosterPack.test.js`, `tests/barnLoft.test.js`). The test looks only at the loft's box: the roof's ties meet each other at the ridge and the span join with the same fault (filed as #929), as does the jack's chassis.
- **The ladder.** You were moved on a straight line from the foot to the top, which ran under the planks' edge and up through them. `climbPoints` now goes up the ladder's own lean (`ladderXAt`), stood `CLIMB_STAND_OFF` (a capsule's radius) off it so your head clears the planks' edge, then over the edge to the top; down is the same points reversed. `Climb` takes the points between. `tests/loftClimb.test.js` puts the barn's colliders in Rapier and sweeps the player's capsule along both ways (and fails on the old line). Nothing pushes you out during the climb, so this is a path test, not a walk as the hut's is.
- **The tarp** was hidden when it came off, so it ended nowhere. It now slides out to the vehicle's right and squashes to a heap about a metre by a metre and a quarter and 15 cm high on the floor, and is moved out of the vehicle's frame into the scene so it stays when the vehicle is driven away. A farm that loads with it off puts it there at once. Putting the job back (a new farm) puts it back over the vehicle.
- **The jack** had its ram standing up through the arm and the arm hovering above the chassis on nothing. The housing and the ram now stand on plates between the channels, the arm turns on a pin through two ears, and the ram's rod ends 3 mm into the arm's underside (`tests/jackModel.test.js`). The arm is higher at the front than before, so the jack is 0.4 m tall.

## Two vehicles at the bench, the catalogue on the bench and the tin in the corner (#809)

- **The bench's order.** `unitAtBench` took the small bay's quad, then a standing barn find (the rusty quad or the old bike), and only then the big bay, so a find that stood there for the whole game kept the tractor, the Tor and the cars from the bench. `benchCandidates` (`benchJobs.js`, pure, `tests/benchCandidates.test.js`) now lists the small bay, then the big bay, then the finds nearest you first, and the bench opens on the first.
- **Why a row and not a second bay for the finds.** The finds are worked on where they stand, which is the whole point of them being under a tarp in the barn, and moving them to a bay would have moved the Plover for good. With a vehicle in a bay and a find standing, the bench menu's last row is "Work on the ... instead" (`W` `S` `E`, like every other row), and it swaps to the next one that is switched off. No new key, so nothing for `controls.js` beyond the line that says so. The girls' bench still works only on the small bay (`unitInBay`), unchanged.
- **The catalogue** lies on the workbench's front edge between the toolbox and the booster pack (`layout.shed.catalogue`, `tests/benchSpots.test.js` keeps it on the top and clear of every neighbour), where it hung on the west wall behind a bale. It is a booklet now (back cover, pages, front cover, spine, ribbon), lying flat with its cover up; the nail and string went, since nothing hangs it. Its box has `priority: 1` like the logbook's, as the bench's own box would be hit first.
- **The money tin** stands on the shelf it always had, moved from the west wall (z +3.75, behind the bales and the cork board) to the north wall's north-west corner (`layout.moneyTin`: x -3.9, y 1.05), between the photo board and the spares shelf, a few metres from the bench and out of the room's middle. The girls' snack tin keeps its plank on the west wall: Jamie's "biscuit tin" is the money tin, whose lid is printed as a biscuit tin's. Both places were chosen by eye from one shot each; the tin's is the more arguable.
- **Text.** The FAQ, the controls list, `docs/game.md` and the glossary said "under the cork board" for both; they say where they are now.

## The barn's boards: a records table, headings, slip shadows and plain tyre tags (#811)

- **Records.** Each record was two lines (the event, then the time under it) with a pitch that shrank as lines were added, so the two lines of one record ran into the next. It is a table now, one line each: the event on the left in 22 px chalk, the time and who set it on the right in 24 px, the event squeezed to fit what the time leaves. The baselines come from `recordRowYs`, evenly from under the heading to the foot of the board, never closer than 30 px for up to eleven lines (`tests/boardText.test.js`).
- **The classified board's shadow.** The only shadow on it was the faded patch a gone slip leaves on a bare board, which was a different size and place from the slips. The patch is now exactly the first slip's footprint (`slipRect`), with the pin where its pin goes, and a pinned slip casts a shadow like the photos' prints do. Jamie's note said the shadow "does not match what is pinned", so this is the reading taken; a different one is a new issue.
- **Headings.** SMALL ADS on the classified board (taller by 0.05 m to make room, 400 px a metre as before) and PHOTOS on the photo board, in the same marker lettering as the chalkboards' headings. New prints are dealt below the heading (`y` from 0.25 to 0.85 of the board, not 0.16 to 0.84); prints already pinned keep their place. Fonts are #807 and the boards' rebuild #503.
- **Tyre tags** say the size and, for an empty bay, NONE in red. The count went (the stack shows up to five, and the rack's panel gives the number), so the tag is only redrawn when the bay empties or fills.

## The land's lie, and stubble inside its field (#813)

- **The landform.** `LANDFORM_PLAN` (`layoutNew.js`) gives the height above the valley floor at seven plan x's: 0 from x 1000 east (the A-road is at about 1300, the village and the river on the floor), 7 m at the lane (885), 26 at the yard (520), 40 at 300, 56 at Back Lane (30), 84 at -150 and 100 at the moor's ridge wall (-330). `landform.js` joins them with a monotone cubic, flat at both ends, so the floor and the crest level out and #708 carries the far land on from either. It is a function of x alone: the land falls east, as the issue says, with no north-south tilt (the plan's rising land to the north is #708's). 2.5 to 3.8 degrees across the farm, 6 at Back Lane, up to 10 on the moor (a test holds the farm under 4.5 and the moor under 12, against the hill's banks at 38).
- **Read by** `natural()` in `terrainHeight.js` through `lie(x, z)`, the farm's rolling plus the landform, which is also what the yard, the drag strip's level, every levelled road and the Dutch barn's and the shed's floors are laid to, so they follow the slope. The strip's level is one height for its whole length, so it does not follow it (next). It is given as game metres relative to the yard (`landformPoints`), so the yard keeps today's height. Today's layout has no `landform` and keeps its own 22 m rise beyond the hedges, which layout 2 drops as the landform replaces it. The stream channel and the village's plane read `natural`, so they follow.
- **The drag strip** is still one flat level (the average of five lie heights along it), now on a slope of 3.5 per cent along it. It sits at 1.41 m against today's 1.24: its west end is in a cut of about 0.7 m (the ground there is 2.10 m, 14 m before the start), and its east end stands about 2.5 m above the ground past the run-off (-1.12 m at 6 m beyond it, a drop of about 23 degrees where today's falls 0.2 m). It is #616's to move onto the drive, so it was left, and the test of the levelled pieces leaves it out.
- **The wheat's rise** (today's south field's 4 m) was already gone from layout 2 (#681 gave `WHEAT` no `rise`, leaving the land's lie to this issue), and the landform is that lie.
- **Stubble in its polygon.** `scatterStubble` (`stubbleScatter.js`, pure) stands the clumps in the drill rows and keeps each inside its field's polygon, and `stubbleShares` shares them out by polygon area, not box area (the six fields are 25.5 ha in polygons and 27.5 in boxes). The old loop already kept clumps off non-arable fields through `stubbleAt`, but it counted a slanted field by its box, and Top Field and Lane Field, both arable, could take each other's clumps across their shared slanted side (the test fails on those two with the polygon check taken out). Today's two rectangles come out as before, clump for clump (`tests/stubbleScatter.test.js` has the old loop beside it).
- **A budget.** Today's two fields (1.8 ha) share 60,000 clumps; the six of the new farm (25.5 ha, fourteen times as much) share `layout.stubbleClumps`, 150,000: a sixth as thick per square metre, so the matrices and colours held while it builds are two and a half times today's, not fourteen.
- **The ground texture** (`paintStubble`, `groundTexture.js`) clips each field's straw colour, blotches, hollow, drill rows and tramlines to its polygon through `toPx`, where it clipped to the box. `tests/groundStubbleClip.test.js` runs it over a recording canvas on layout 2: one clip a field, equal to its polygon, and nothing filled outside one; today's two fields clip to their own rectangles.
- **Measured** with `perf.mjs --query layout=2`, origin/main against this, at the five spots: triangles +4% at the spawn (2.35M to 2.45M), +2% in the yard and the village, none at the wood, and -25% on the hilltop (5.75M to 4.29M, 1,827 to 1,481 draw calls: `frameBudget.mjs` stands at the highest point of the hill pasture, which the rising land moved from (-52, 202) at 16.4 m to its west corner (-96, 202) at 21.9 m, so it is a different view and not geometry lost); ms a frame within the run's noise elsewhere (7.0 to 7.1, 13.7 to 14.0, 12.0 to 12.4, 11.3 to 11.4) and 13.2 to 11.4 on the hilltop; 4.30 to 4.55 s to Ready. Layout 1 is not changed: its stubble is the same clump for clump and it has no landform.
- **By eye**, one look in two rounds (`scene landform` puts you at the west edge, about 20 m above the yard: 22.5 m): from 40 m up at the west edge the farm lies below you falling away east, each field's hedges straight along its polygon and its stubble filling to them. What the ridge and the moor look like waits for #713, which builds the ground out to it.

## Field names, the strips and the places held for later items (#814)

- **Names.** `fieldNames.js` has 64 plain British names, each for the kinds of field it suits and the traits a field needs for it (`north`, `far`, `long`, `high`, `low`, `water`, `pond`, `wood`, `pit`, `lane`, `near`, `south`). `layoutNew.js` works the shape-and-height traits out from each field and sets the ones only the plan knows by hand, so "Brook", "Water", "Pond" and "River" only go to a field beside water, "Hill", "Fell" and "Moor" to high ground, "Long" to a long one. A farm's names are dealt from its seed on their own stream (`farmSeed ^ 0xf1e1d5`), the field with the fewest choices first, and none is used twice. 300 seeds deal without running out, and no two of 300 farms but a handful share a set.
- **What is dealt.** Twelve fields: North, Top, Lane, Long Piece, Far, South (arable), Wet Meadow, the sheep field and Brook Meadow (pasture), Hay Meadow, Low Ley and Hill Pasture. Hollow Wood and the Old Pit keep the names the game already has for them, Home Ground is the girls' and your own, and the farmstead and the drive are the yard and the road (`FIXED_NAMES`). The issue says sixteen fields named; the plan has 17 (12 dealt, 5 fixed) and the map names all 17.
- **Kept by the save.** `captureFarm` writes `fieldNames` (the dealt ones); `namedLayout` reads them back when there is one for every dealt field, none blank or repeated, and otherwise deals from the farm's seed, so a farm saved before names, or a damaged one, gets what a new farm of its seed would. Layout 2 is never saved yet (`openFarm`), so this is first used when #692 opens the new farm. Both save fixtures gained the key.
- **Used by.** The map already writes `layout.fields[].label`. `fieldLabel(layout, key)` is what the chalkboard (#691) reads for "Spray Lane Field". The girls say `fieldPhrase(key, kind, crop)`, "the pit", "the barley field", "the hay meadow", "the meadow" (#951): the banks are recorded ahead of time, so a name dealt per farm can't be in a line (`voice-lines.md`). `tp me <key>` goes to each field by its plan key (top, north, lanefield, hay ...), at the place in it with the most room; the `layout` command and the panel list the names.
- **The strips** (`stewardshipPlan`): a 6 m margin inside every arable field (a side shorter than 12 m, where the outline cuts a corner, is folded to a point first so the inner line doesn't fold back on itself); a wildflower strip in Long Piece 6 m wide, 6.5 m in from the drive, 7 m shorter at each end (the headland and a metre) so it stays off the headland; a beetle bank 2 m wide and 40 cm high down the middle of Top Field, across its long way, headland to headland; a 4 m skylark plot at the roomiest place in Top Field (25 m or more from the bank) and in Far Field. The wildflower strip is inside the headland, not in it, so the grass margin stays a track round the field. Nothing is drawn: their look is #697.
- **Held.** `RESERVED_PLAN`: the footpath and the bridleway as the plan map's own curves, flattened (the footpath in two runs, broken at the stone track's bridge), their eight crossings, the moor gate (38, 700), five trough spots and the 11 kV line's seven poles (the last 52 m from the one before, the rest 61). The crossings are up to 9 m from the curve, as the map has them.
- **Seen from every gate.** `canSee` takes the land's lie alone (hedges and trees are not counted, which a tall thing clears from a gap): from each of the 20 gateways at least the church tower, the Dutch barn's roof and the last pole are in sight, and the trig pillar from most (the landmarks' places are #715's, #682's and #711's; the trig pillar's, plan (-330, 480), is a stand-in until #713 places it). Not checked by eye.

## Field names that keep, read well together, and are said by key (#951, from the recheck of #814)

- **A partly kept save keeps what it has.** `keptFieldNames` (`farmSave.js`) returns each good kept name and drops a blank, over-long or repeated one; only the fields without one are dealt, from the names left, so a field added or re-kinded later renames nothing (a test grows the layout by one field). Before, one missing name re-dealt all twelve.
- **No two names on a farm start with the same word.** Before it, 1,630 of 2,000 farms had a repeat (the recheck's count: "Long" on 64%, "Far" on 24%); now none of 2,000 does. A name whose first word is used is left out unless the field has nothing else (never reached on those 2,000). A kept name's first word counts too.
- **"Sheep Field" needs the new `sheep` trait,** which only the sheep field has; it went to another field on about 5% of farms (the recheck's count) and is now dealt only to the sheep field, on 1,162 of 2,000 farms. The other animal names (Horse Close, Cow Pasture, Calf Croft) can still go to the sheep field: not asked for.
- **`fieldPhrase(key, kind, crop)`** says the place by key where a field is one (the wood, the pit, the yard, the drive, the home field, the sheep field) and otherwise the kind, so the Old Pit is "the pit", not "the hill". #691's line about the girls now says so. A tp place for a field exists on the new farm only, which `debug-panel.md` says to state with a line.

## A landmark in every field (#815)

- **One list, one copy.** `FIELD_LANDMARKS_PLAN` (`layoutNew.js`) gives each of the twelve fields one thing of its own, in plan metres, and the layout carries it as `layout.fieldLandmarks` on the new farm only. #815 builds eight (`landmarkProps.js`, `BUILT_KINDS`). The other four are placed here and built by their own issues, which read the place from this list: the 11 kV line (#711, its poles are `RESERVED_PLAN.powerLine`'s, three in Lane Field), the field barn (#624, plan (378, 451)), the hut and the flask rock (#684, (95, 802) and (262, 814)) and the trig pillar and cairn (#713, `LANDMARKS_PLAN`'s (-330, 480), the Moor having no polygon of its own: `MOOR_PLAN`). Agreed with Jamie on 17 November 2026 because those issues depend on this one.
- **Where, and why there.** Each is 8 m or more inside its field and clear of the grass margin, the beetle bank, the skylark plots, the barley's hollow, the gateways, the troughs and the footpath's crossings (a test checks each). The dead elm stands in Long Piece's south hedge, 2 m in. Far Field's pond is at plan (160, 530), 14 m across with two willows, because the plan's only pond is the Wet Meadow's (its own, left alone): clear of the green lane's gap, the stream and the plot. Low Ley's alders are six, 4 m north of the plan map's brook curve, 20 to 40 m apart. Brook Meadow's are four oaks; the crack willow and the sight line to the bridge are #565's. The Wet Meadow's oak is #881, Hollow Wood's #683's and Home Ground's #682's.
- **Built from the kit.** `limb`, `grow`, `foliage`, `ivy`, `sticks` and `groundDisc` are exported from `landmarkProps.js` for the next trees (#683's veteran oak, #682's, #684's hawthorn). The farm's own oak model (4.3 m wide, 7.3 m tall, a bole 1 m across) cannot be stretched into a field oak (a bole 1.1 m across under a crown 20 m across), so `fieldOak` grows one from the kit: a short bole, five boughs sweeping out almost level, 40 or so clumps of leaves, a few bare dead tips, a burr, broken stubs, ivy laid to the bole's real radius, a worn ring and a fallen limb. The rest are procedural too. Textures reuse the kits where they exist: `barkTexture` (neighbourTextures.js, which gained standing-tree kinds), `rustTexture` (quarryTextures.js) for the cast iron, `boltsMesh` (the detail kit) for the roller's hub bolts; `landmarkTextures.js` holds only what was new (gritstone with lichen, worn earth). Colliders are cylinders and boxes as the other trees', and each trunk can be hooked by the winch.
- **The pond.** About 14 m across (rx 9, rz 7.5 and an outline that swells and shrinks a tenth round the bank). Its dish is cut in `terrainHeight.js` (0.9 m deep, flat to 30% of the way out, easing up to a bank 1.25 radii out) and the water stands 0.5 m deep at the middle, as the Wet Meadow's does. Like that pond it is not in the engine's water rule: only the river drowns an engine.
- **Far away.** A tree is a body (trunk, boughs, crown) and a group of detail. The body goes in the scene as it is, unnamed, so it is drawn like the farm's other trees and stays in sight from the gate; the group, named in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`, holds the detail (ivy, sticks, worn ground, reeds, duckweed, a fallen limb) and is left out beyond `setPieceDistance`. The gateposts and the roller are all detail. An earlier version gave the trees a far version (a box and an octahedron) and the review found `fullDistance` swaps it in at 45 m, which made each tree a green diamond from any gate; it is gone. `FarDetail.box` now takes an instanced mesh's own box (it took the shared shape's at the origin, so a landmark with instances reached back to the yard).
- **Looking.** Three rounds in all with `tools/shot.mjs`, which gained `--layout 2` and framing for instanced meshes. `tp me to loneoak` (and `oldgateposts`, `deadelm`, `farpond`, `castroller`, `loneash`, `brookalders`, `bigoaks`) goes to each, and `scene landmarks` sets up the ash in daylight. The Looks like list was trimmed to what the models have (the issue comment says what was left out).
- **Seasons.** The oaks' leaves take the autumn tint as the farm's do; the procedural leaves (ash, alders, willows) stay green all year, as ash leaves fall green. The snow coat covers them as it does the rest.

## Far Field's pond on the farm plan (#956)

- **The plan changed after the build, not before.** #815 built a small field pond with two willows in Far Field from a line in its own list ("Far Field: a corner pond with two willows"), but the plan (`docs/farm-plan.md`, the map) had only the Wet Meadow's pond, against its rule that a place is on the plan before it is built. Jamie agreed on 17 November 2026 to keep it ("OK, keep it"), so the plan caught up: the arable fields' row, the Wet Meadow's row (its pond is its own), the map page's ellipse and two willows at plan (160, 530) and Far Field's note, and `docs/farm-plan.svg` re-rendered.
- **Why a pond there.** A pond in a corner of an arable field is ordinary on a British farm and gives Far Field something to head for from its gates. It is about 14 m across on the field's low east side, clear of the green lane, the stream and the skylark plot (#815's section above has the numbers).
- **What the plan now promises.** An uncropped margin round it, so the stubble and tramlines stop short of the water: a separate issue builds it, depending on this one.

## The drive: from the lane to the yard, with the cattle grid at the lane (#568)

- **What.** On `?layout=2` the farm road is the drive: 6 m of old tarmac from plan (905, 345) west to the yard at (612, 345), 293 m, with 7 m of verge each side and no hedge (the three lines it shares with Lane Field, Long Piece and the farmstead are `none` in `boundariesOf`, so the crops come up to the verge). `driveLayout()` in `layoutNew.js` writes the drive and the lane once, from `DRIVE_PLAN` and `LANE_PLAN`, as `layout.road`, `layout.publicLane` and the two roads of `layout.roads` (the village street stays as it was). The drive keeps the road id `farm`, which a dozen readers ask for, until #692 retires today's farm road and can rename it.
- **The lane moved with it.** The drive has to meet a lane, and today's runs through the middle of the new farm, so the lane is straight at plan x 905 for the length of the ground (-70 to 970), with no invisible walls (`laneBarriers` is empty on the new farm; #687 bends it round to the A-road and puts in the junctions). Its `open` stretch now only says where the road mud's grid is kept (plan z 245 to 445). The village and the neighbour's farm still stand where they did, so they sit oddly (#688, #569); the village street's junction onto the lane misses it until then.
- **The grid and its gate.** The grid is 3.6 m long at plan x 880 to 883.5, across all of the tarmac, in a 9 m mouth in the lane boundary. Beside it, in the same boundary, is a shut five-bar field gate on its own posts for stock and horses (`bypass`: 3.4 m between posts, id `bypass`, the same steel gates as the big gate had). The big gate onto the lane and the gate across the grid are not built on the new farm (`layout.road.gate` and `grid.gateOffset` are absent), and everything that read them asks first: visitors, the girls' cars, the console (`lane.bypassGate`; `lane.bigGate` and `lane.gridGate` are false on the new farm), the panel's buttons, the traffic host and its prompts.
- **Routes.** `visitPlaces` says `open` when the road's grid has an `x`: `planVisit` then drives a visitor in over the grid, to the barn (still today's, #682) and back out, with no gate step, and `leaveRoute` takes a girl's car along the drive over the grid and down the lane; `goneBeyond` counts her gone past the drive's mouth and as far down the lane as today's is from the road. `tests/drive.test.js` runs both routes, and a whole visit.
- **The look.** The tarmac is painted into the ground like the farm road's (so mud, rain, frost and snow lie on it): 70 squares of repair in two tones, the edges broken into gravel in three tones, four water-filled potholes (#214 deepens them) and mud and straw at three gateways, all from `planWear` in `groundTexture.js`, which draws nothing and takes nothing from the random stream for a road that asks for none of them. The grid (`driveProps.js`, today's grid too) is galvanised bars on bearers over a dark pit, concrete sills the bars rest on, concrete kerbs with white-painted tops and ends, a "Cattle grid" sign on a post facing the lane and a post-and-rail wing each side; four white reflector posts stand at the lane end; the verge's grass is cut to 10 cm for the first 14 m in from the lane and grows to 55 cm by 150 m (`vergeGrassHeight`). The farm sign's place is `layout.road.signSpot`, on the north verge by the grid: #570 builds it there.
- **Kept as it was.** `layout.farm`, the old rectangle, stays at today's numbers, re-attributed in `NOT_MOVED_YET` to the wood, the hill and the quarry, which still place themselves from it. The achievements' "End of the road" is the drive's lane end on the new farm, as there is no hardstanding.
- **Measured** with `perf.mjs --query layout=2`, main against this branch at the five spots: draw calls 694 to 677, 2,918 to 2,872, 2,246 to 2,238, 1,640 to 1,601 and 1,494 to 1,451 (the big gate, the gate across the grid and the old grid's pieces are gone), ms a frame within the run's noise (7.6 to 7.7, 14.1 to 13.9, 12.6 to 12.2, 11.6 to 11.4, 12.1 to 11.1), 4.67 to 4.69 s to Ready. The spots are none of them on the drive, so its own cost (4,000 verge tufts in tiles, about 40 meshes in the grid) is not in these numbers; the over-budget flags are the new farm's as they were. No budget raised.
- **After the review (#961).** The grid's wings first stood at the grid's own x and ran out to the drive field's edges, which left a strip between them and the lane boundary to walk round the grid by, so the field gate had no purpose. They now stand in the boundary (`grid.wingX`, plan x 885) from the kerbs to the mouth's edges (`grid.mouth`, 4.5 m). Today's farm built the sign in the swing of the gate across its road, so it has none now. The Dutch barn, the cars' pad and the wash at the Lane Field gateway (plan x 700) are still today's, and the barn's back wall stands 1.5 m into the drive's tarmac until #682 moves it (a girl's car leaving from the pad is sent through it on layout 2; left to #682, which moves both).
- **Not here.** The drag races (#616) and the farm sign (#570) have their own issues; the potholes' depth is #214's.

## The neighbour across the lane (#569)

- **What.** On `?layout=2` her yard is across the lane at plan (950 to 1045, 250 to 335), about 95 by 85 m, with her gateway in its **west** wall at plan z 292 (our drive meets the lane at 345, so the two make no crossroads) and a short tarmac track out to the lane at x 905. Her farmhouse is at the south, her barn at the north-west on the map's footprint (26 by 17 m, its big door in the east wall facing the yard), and her Tor, UTV and old tractor stand about clear of the way in. `NEIGHBOUR_PLAN` and `neighbourLayout()` in `layoutNew.js` write all of it once, as offsets from her anchor (the yard's middle, plan (997.5, 292.5)) resolved by the anchors' own resolver, so moving the anchor moves the yard as one and a test checks every number follows (#972; each offset has its plan position beside it); `anchors.neighbour` and `neighbourFarm` came off `NOT_MOVED_YET`.
- **One yard, two ways round.** `neighbourFarmShape` takes `gateway.side` ('east' on today's farm, 'west' on the new one): the gateway's wall is the one in two pieces, and the gate's swing, the postbox's face, `neighbourGate` (`tp me neighbour`, the panel and `neighbour.go`) follow it. Anchors can't mirror and a half turn would put her door on the south side, so the yard was re-authored rather than turned (`turnable` stays off). `Neighbour`, `NeighbourHost`, the voice and the merge-by-material build are as they were.
- **Her ground.** The land falls 3 m across the yard and is flat from plan x 1000 east, so the yard is one level pad (the buildings are built from one `y0`): levelled to the lie at plan x 946, just before the gate, and eased out over 16 m (`neighbourFarm.floor`), so her track runs down from the lane at no more than 15% and no bank outside her wall is steeper than 25 degrees (`tests/neighbourMove.test.js`). Today's farm keeps its 4 m ease and the narrow one on the lane side, which only a lane right beside the wall needs.
- **Her fields.** The map's block from her hedge on the lane (plan x 912) to x 1290, z -60 to 560, in six fields (`NEIGHBOUR_LAND_PLAN`), at far-ground detail: each field's own grass tone and a dark line along each hedge in the ground and the outer ring (`landColor` in `patchwork.js`), her hedges as the farm's own leafy blocks within 150 m and plain strips beyond, with a collider a stretch and gaps for her track, her north field gate and a way between fields, and her 22 dairy cows (the map's own positions) as scenery in the two north fields, four instanced meshes through `tiledInstances` (`cattleModel.js`: a Holstein in three patterns, vertex-coloured). Her north field gate is in the lane's hedge at plan z 130, on the lane as the issue says (the map's dashed line there is the lane her herd walks, #714, not a track).
- **Seen from afar and hidden behind.** The far version (#400) and the girls' hiding behind her house and barn (#654) read the same shape, so both follow the move; the set piece `neighbourLand` is named in `farDetail.js`.
- **Her parked vehicles** were 93% of her yard's draw calls (200 to 270 meshes each): `mergeParkedVehicle` bakes each into one mesh a material (the Tor 204 meshes to 34, the UTV 268 to 51, the tractor 204 to 26). Measured on layout 2 by hiding her group and drawing again: at the lane 285 calls and 121k triangles became 165 and 177k, and just inside her gate 356 and 129k became 148 and 173k (they no longer drop their small parts far off, so the triangles rise). `.scratch/a569/cost.mjs`. Her land is 20 to 54 calls and 46k to 227k triangles (the leafy hedges, as the farm's own).
- **The dairy** (`dairyKit.js`, textures in `dairyTextures.js`, places in `NEIGHBOUR_PLAN`, as offsets from her anchor): the parlour at plan (1000 to 1030, 256 to 269), the collecting yard in front of it at (1000 to 1030, 271 to 289) (its slab, channel and rails run back to the parlour's wall, two metres north of the plan's rectangle), the bulk tank's room at (1036, 262) and the slurry store at (970, 318), each a builder that returns meshes in game metres for the yard to merge by material with the house and the barn (`dairyMaterials` is shared so the whole dairy adds about 20 draw calls and 4k triangles: 148 to 167 inside her gate). Jamie chose the tank room's place on 18 November 2026: the map has it beside the parlour, 90 m from the only gateway, and the issue and the review said "by the gate". The tanker (#714) comes in at the gateway, across the yard south of the collecting yard and up the strip beside it (`tests/dairy.test.js` finds a 3 m wide way); the gateway's landmark is the name board and churn stand of #911.
- **What is built.** Parlour: block to 2.2 m and fibre-cement above, nine high windows a side (none over the two cow gates), two railed cow gates with dark doorways, a green man's door, a roller shutter and vents in the west gable, the extractor fan in the east, a pressure washer and teat-dip drums by the door. Collecting yard: scraped concrete with a kerb, galvanised posts and rails with the herd's gap on the west, a grated channel along the parlour wall, a drain, a backing gate on overhead rails and a hose reel with its hose out on the concrete. Tank room: block, lean-to roof, a steel door open on the tanker's side, a stainless tank on legs seen through it (you can walk in as far as the tank), a pipe in from the parlour, the pipe stub with a camlock coupling and a collection-point board. Slurry store: 18 m of precast panels 3.6 m high with a lip, a crust inside, a ladder with a safety hoop, a danger board and a loading pipe. The floor and step of the tank room are 4 cm colliders so the capsule walks in (`tests/dairyEntry.test.js`).
- **Held to the standard.** The bare-block test (`parlour`, `collectingYard`, `tankRoom`, `slurryStore` are built in `tests/helpers/worldModels.js`) and the coplanar test pass with nothing in a `KNOWN_` list: the roofs are one-sided sheets (a double-sided box counted six times its surface against the triangle floor), and each overlap the test found was a part moved a few millimetres, never ignored. The look was two rounds with `tools/shot.mjs --eye`.
- **The barn's door is framed here**, as agreed with Jamie on 18 November 2026 (the standard says a missing frame round an opening isn't done): a steel jamb each side, a header, a concrete ramp over the threshold and the top rail the sliding leaves will hang from, with brackets and an end stop (`neighbourFarmProps.js`, `tests/neighbourMove.test.js`). The leaves, the windows and the inside are #963.
- **Not here.** The review's sound, routine, barn and night items are #965, #964, #963 and #966. Her gateway is a `budgetSpots` entry in layout 2 (`neighbour`); `BUDGETS` in `tools/frameBudget.mjs` has no entry for it yet, so nothing measures it.

## Move the farmstead to the middle of the farm (#682)

- **What.** On `?layout=2` the barn, yard, stackyard, orchard, garden, cars, muck heap and Home Ground's play things stand where `docs/farm-plan.md` puts them (`FARMSTEAD_PLAN`, `farmsteadLayout` in `layoutNew.js`, plan metres). The barn is today's barn as it is (Jamie, 13 November: #613 rebuilds it in place), turned a quarter left so its doors face south onto the yard. The yard's anchor sits 10 m out of the doors and 2 m along, as today's does from today's barn, and turns with it, so the bell, tanks, generator, hooks, rail and everything else keep their place beside the barn (the tanks land against its east wall, the generator along its west one).
- **The barn turns.** The barn code is written in the barn's own frame (where it stands at yaw 0 about its anchor) and a frame turns a point into the world's: `barnFrame` (`barn.js`: `toWorld`, `toLocal`, `vecToWorld`, `headingToWorld`), `placeInBarn` for what hangs on a wall, and `layout.barnLocal` (`layoutBarn.js`) holding the barn's own things (`bench`, `loft`, `bay`, `jumperRail` and the rest) as they are in that frame. `insideBarn`, `underRoof`, `indoorLevel`, `floorBlend` and `wallBoxes` take a world point and turn it themselves. `buildBarnShed` draws everything in a group at the anchor, turned, with the colliders turned the same way. The barn's `at(...)` has `yaw: 0` so its half-sizes stay its own. Quarter turns only. Turning the barn in the data alone left its spares rack in the field (#661); this is the work that issue said to do first. `tests/barnTurn.test.js` turns it and checks each piece against the resolver and the unturned barn's answers; `tests/farmstead.test.js` does it again on the real layout 2.
- **What had to follow.** The loft (`loftRestSpot`, `worldClimbPoints`, `strawTip`), the wall boards, calendar, ads, trophy shelf, snack tin, results sheet, weather station, spares and tyre racks, the bench's proxy and notebook, the charger and batteries on the floor, the girls' work spot, the hay door's wind (`headingToWorld`), the cat's and dog's idea of the doorway, the girls' recall spots and the fumes' way out, the map's barn, the visitors' stop, door and parcel places, the spawn keep-outs and the detour code, which took a door on any side (`door.out`). `tests/gameSize.test.js` held `game.js` to its ceiling, so the loft's places went into `loft.js`.
- **A visitor's loop was too tight.** `planVisit` turned round in a loop of 3.5 m, tighter than the vehicle's own turning circle (4 m), so on some geometries it orbited a waypoint it could not reach: it now turns in 4.5 m (`visitPlaces` `turn`).
- **Home Ground.** One anchor, `home`, at plan (332, 360): where today's grass field's middle falls, so the Home Loop, its bogs, the cones, the jump, the line-up (by the south hedge), the brushwood and today's field's straw bales keep today's shape, in `at('home', ...)`. The bonfire is its own anchor at plan (400, 424), its south-east corner. The drag strip is not moved (the review; #616 removes it): it stays at today's numbers, which on layout 2 is in the yard. The paddock goes: Home Ground's yard side is a boundary of kind `rail` (`RAILED`), drawn as post and rail by `buildHomeRails` for the stretch the yard fronts (plan z 317 to 415, with the gateway's gap), and the four fence seats sit on it facing out over the field.
- **The yard** is concrete over plan (425 to 615, 317 to 415), painted in the ground (`paintSlab`: 6 m bays, joints, mottling) with no grass through it. Grip on it is the ground's, as before: it is a look for now. The garden's lawn is behind a drystone wall with a gate on the yard side (`buildGardenWall`, merged into two meshes, through-stones for the rough face). The hens' house and the pig's ark (`orchardKit.js`) stand in fenced runs in the orchard with the feed bin; both are built from the kits' wood and tin with trim, and in the bare-block and coplanar tests.
- **Kept clear, not built:** the machinery shed, grain store, cattle shed and clamp and sheep pens are rectangles in `layout.farmstead.keptClear` and a test checks nothing of ours stands in them. The Dutch barn is at plan (446, 268) at the stackyard's north (the landmark moved with it from (446, 281)).
- **Still today's numbers:** `fieldHalf` and `gate` (the hill and the wood place themselves from them: #684), the drag strip (#616), the field shed (#624), the trough (#710), the hay, spray and harrow fields (#691). The field shed stands inside the garden plot's east end on layout 2 until #624 moves it.
- **Not measured, and not unchanged.** The hen house and pig ark are built on today's farm too (the hens' run and the pig's sty are in its yard, the frame budget's spot), so #976 added about 80 unmerged meshes there where the coop and shelter were about 13; #978 merges each per material (a handful of draw calls, the same triangles). Layout 2's numbers and the yard spot's change from main are not measured here.

## What the recheck of the farmstead's move found (#978)

- **A visitor drove into the barn on today's farm.** #976 widened the visitors' turning loop to r 4.5, dx 5, which put its far side 0.7 m inside the second span's wall. It is r 4.5, dx 3 now: wide enough for the vehicle's 4 m turning circle (the reason it was widened: on some geometries a loop tighter than that orbits a waypoint it cannot reach), with its far side 1.3 m off the wall. A simulated visit on both farms must stay a metre outside the barn (`tests/farmsteadVisits.test.js`).
- **The yard's trailers were on the drive's line.** The yard's turn put the tipping trailer at plan (542, 345), where the visitors drive, and nothing blocks a visiting vehicle. `trailer`, `tipTrailer` and `sprayer` are placed in `FARMSTEAD_PLAN` south of the lane, outside the kept-clear places, and the test keeps a visit 3 m off them and the tanks.
- **Missed in the turn:** the money tin's shelf (`moneyTinView.js`, now built in the barn's frame and put in the world with `placeInBarn`) and the pit's half-sizes (`shed.pit` has a `yaw: 0`, so a turn doesn't swap them). The map's barn label and the hay door's offsets are turned too. Tests for the turned `workSpot`, `strawTip`, hay-door heading and `placeInBarn` compare them with the unturned answers turned by `barnFrame`.
- **Home Ground's rail** is drawn and solid along the whole `rail` boundary (plan z 235 to 440 less the gateway), not just the yard's frontage: a boundary of kind `rail` has no hedge, so what the rail doesn't draw has nothing.

## Move Hollow Wood to the north-west corner (#683)

- **What.** On `?layout=2` `woodsLayout` (`layoutNew.js`) carries today's wood to the plan's corner by one shift: the Run, the Trail, their markers, the pond, the logs, bogs, whoops and deadwood keep their shapes (Jamie's walk: "keep its shape"), so `woods.js` times the same 589 m and 390 m (`tests/woodMove.test.js` pins both). The wood is the shape of its field (`woods.outline`, seven sides, 28,455 m2): trees, mud, the ground's weight and "in the wood" read the polygon, and the rectangle is only its bounds. The review's 1.8 times today's area and 300 oaks were wrong: it is 1.48 times, and 252 oaks and 163 bushes at today's density.
- **Centred, 5 m east.** The outline cuts the north-west corner, which left the Run's west tip 1.6 m from the hedge; 5 m east leaves 6.4 m (a test holds every point of both loops and the pond 6 m clear).
- **The gate, and a longer lead-in.** The plan's gateway onto the Wet Meadow is at x 130, 38 m east of where the Run's junction falls once it is centred, so a straight 22 m lead-in cannot keep the shape, the place and the gate at once. Jamie chose (18 November 2026, "1. a") to keep the plan and bend the lead-in: 12 m straight in from the gate (the frame budget's spot), then west inside the south hedge to the junction, about 70 m in all. The second gateway onto Back Lane is in the wood's west side (`GATEWAYS_PLAN`, `woods.backLane`); neither is built as a gate (#696), and the old north gate is not built on the new farm.
- **The woodpile at the gate.** Its anchor (`anchors.logYard`) is in the Wet Meadow east of the gate with the hedge at its back; the tractor's rounds, splitter and stack move with it. Every farm has a lean-to with an old stack under it, and a chopping block with an axe, chips and split logs (`logYardProps.js`, `layout.logYard.scenery`). The working stack got stakes, bearers and bark, and the rounds and logs bark and sawn ends from the kits (#738's logs and woodStack, off `KNOWN_PLAIN`), as did the Run's logs and deadwood.
- **One great oak.** `buildVeteranOak` (the field oak of #815's kit, with a hollow) stands in the glade north of the pond, 19 m from any track, with nothing else within 11 m: a 20 m crown against the wood's 4 to 8 m.
- **Edge and plants.** A spot every 2 m along each hedge, 1 to 3.4 m in, of thorn (the farm's bush, larger) and bramble, none within 5.5 m of a gate. #561 (folded in): ferns and brambles are textured, alpha-tested cards (`woodPlants.js`) whose vertex colours vary leaf to leaf and, in autumn, from the tips in (green, yellow, orange, brown on one plant), two kinds of each; 104 triangles a fern and 100 a bramble.
- **Oaks far away.** See `performance.md`, "Oaks far away (#683)".
- **Not here.** The wood's sound, its night lamp and bands and the bogs' water are #980, #981 and #982; new kinds of tree and the autumn colours of trees #848; timing the Run and the Trail #230 and #560 (there are no records for them; Jamie, 18 November: leave them to those). #231 (grow the wood east) closes with this: the new wood is big enough and has its second gate.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: the lean-to, stack, block and axe (two rounds), the wood from the gate, the great oak across the pond, and the Fern Trail's ferns in autumn. Not looked at closely: the thorn and bramble edge, the wood at dusk.


## What the recheck of Hollow Wood's move found (#985)

- **The floor was still a box.** `paintWoods` painted the leaf litter and the fallen-leaf drifts over `woods.rect`, which on the new farm is only the bounds of the seven-sided outline: about 5,500 m2 of litter lay outside the wood, up the slanted east hedge into North Field and in a sliver of the Wet Meadow. A wood with an `outline` now paints a floor cell and a drift only where `woods.inWoods` says it is in the wood (no canvas clip: the painter yields part way, and a clip held across a yield would catch another tile's drawing). Today's rectangular wood is painted as before.
- **Two names on the map.** The wood's own label and the plan's field label both said "Hollow Wood"; on a plan that names its fields only the plan's is written.
- **A tint that went the wrong way.** `barkPale` was the bark texture multiplied by a colour, which can only darken, so it was darker than `bark`. The dark kind carries the tint now and the pale one is the texture as painted. The FAQ's "nearly 2.8 hectares" is "about 2.8".
- **Left to #847:** the map's tree dots are still a lattice over the wood's bounding box.


## Move the hill side as one (#684)

- **One landscape, behind `?layout=2`.** `hillLayout` (`layoutNew.js`, from `HILL_PLAN`) gives `layout.hill` on the new farm: today's hill, read by `hill.js` as before, set on the plan's fields. The pasture, the sheep field and the meadow are their fields' own outlines (`rect.points`, with the bounding rectangle kept for the ground's weight); `inPasture`, `inEast`, `inWest` and `outside` follow the outline, as do the ground paint and the tussocks. A rectangle without `points` behaves as it did, so today's farm is unchanged (`tests/hill.test.js`). `hill`, `gate` and `anchors.hut` came off `NOT_MOVED_YET`; the river, its riverside meadow and the brook are #565's, so the stream ends 8 m past the pasture's hedges.
- **The lane is a field.** #681 had one gap between Far Field and the Sheep Field; the lane runs between them, so `lane` is a 9.2 m strip (corners `pe` and `re` added, `r1` moved 5 m west to x 195 so the lane is straight), kind `lane`, kept by name, with hedges either side and an open end at the ford. It is 125 m, 2.3 times today's. Its head is the south gate (`layout.gate` now says where, `gate.z`), and Far Field, the Sheep Field and Home Ground each have a gateway onto it. Jamie's choice of 13 November stands: the banks are stretched, not redesigned.
- **Banks.** Each of today's six keeps its rise and width (so 38 degrees at the steepest, 18.2 m in all) and its distance from the stream is multiplied by 3.3, so the last ends about 175 m up where the plan's map draws it and the treads lengthen. A second, slower wander (`slowWander`, a swing every 140 m) keeps a bank from repeating. The easy way up is the east 100 m, as today's was the east 27%. The sheep field keeps its two terraces (valley 2.5 m as today, so the banks are 1 m high), moved to plan z 491 and 527 with the wall across at 468.
- **The hut is seen.** On the plan's landform alone the last bank hides the hut's roof by 2 m from the ford; `crown` (3.5 m over 55 m, under the hut, so the pad is cut at its level) lifts it clear. The sight line from the ford and from Home Ground's gate is a test. The review's "flatter than 0.1 within 7 m" is not true of today's hut either (0.21 at 7 m); the pad is level to 2.5 m and eased over 5 m, tested at 2.5, 4 and 7 m.
- **The fence is a line.** `createElectricFence` takes `from`, `to` and a gap in metres along, or today's `z`, `fromX`, `toX`, `gap.at` (read as the same line). It runs north to south at plan x 232 from the foot to the south hedge, its gap the first 22 m from the stream, the whole of the foot, where quads cross it on the way to the climb (a test drives the line from the ford's waiting place to the first cones). `onHalf` is the rule the flock follows: `setUpFlock` keeps the hill's flock to one half and 1.5 m off the wire, gap included. `hillHalf` is -1 by default: on the new farm the halves swap each farm day (`hillHalfFor`), so the other rests; today's farm keeps the climb's side, 1. The numbers of sheep and the lambing and summer routines are #707's.
- **Numbers.** The pasture is 390 by 305 m; the wood asks for 85 oaks and 150 bushes (today's 34 and 60) in 44 by 80 m, and the loop's spacing places 64 to 78; the meadow's pond is today's 22 by 16 m at the plan's (112, 292) with a 65 m way in from the gateway at (197, 296). Gorse, bracken and tussocks are today's counts over thirteen times the ground: sparser, on purpose, to hold the frame (the hilltop spot on the new farm is 929 calls and 2.54M triangles against 1,750 and 7.0M); scaling them by area is in the reviews' list.
- **Hilltop** (`tools/frameBudget.mjs`) looks for the highest ground on the pasture's outline, not its rectangle.



## What the recheck of the hill side's move found (#994)

- **Today's oaks.** The hill wood's loop got `trees * 60` tries, 2,040 where it had 2,000, so today's farm drew 40 more on the shared props stream and every random draw after it moved. `woodTries` keeps 2,000 where a layout asks for no number of oaks.
- **The sign and the work light.** The fence's warning sign is a plane facing +z, edge-on to a wire running north to south: `signYaw` turns it across any line. The work light's "stood at the gate" point read `fieldHalf` for the gate's z; it reads `gate.z` first, as the gate and the ground paint do.
- **The flock rule is one function.** `hillFlockAllowed` (`flock.js`) is what `setUpFlock` and the `flock` scene both use, and a test removes the fence clause and fails. The scene looks for ground on the flock's own half when the cones are not on it.
- **Still open.** The plan's map page needs the lane's field and corners (#995). Until #692 saves the farm's day on the new farm, `hillHalfFor` always gives half 1 there.

## Move the river and its meadow into Brook Meadow (#565)

- **Settled with Jamie (19 November 2026): the river keeps #813's landform and falls with the land.** The issue asked for a fall of about 0.3% and flow reversed to run east. The landform is a height by x alone, so a river that runs along x falls 12 m in 230 m (5%) through the meadow, and a 0.3% one would stand 9 m proud of Low Ley at its east end or sit in a cutting 9 m deep at the west. A second opinion found no valley term or contour in the code or the plan that settles it. So the river runs east down the fall, not as a still river; a valley cut or a gentler landform through the meadow is not done. Pools and riffles were tried as a deeper cut with deeper water (`poolExtra`) and changed nothing you could see, as the two cancel (the surface is the ground less the cut plus the water standing), so they were taken out; the recheck found it (#565).
- **A surface that only falls.** The land rolls up under the line in four places, which would run the water uphill. `brook.useGround` (given the ground along the line by `terrainHeight.js`, for a river with `eastward`) cuts the channel deeper there by how far the surface would have risen above the lowest it has been, so it never rises downstream (`tests/riverMove.test.js`, seen failing without it). The water standing on the bed is unchanged.
- **Shape.** `riverLayout` (`layoutNew.js`, from `RIVER_PLAN`) puts `hill.brook`, `hill.river` and `hill.riverside` on the new farm: waypoints along the alders' curve, 8 m bends (`river.bends`), a width that varies by a quarter (`river.vary`, 8 to 13 m across), full from plan x 460 to 650 and narrowing to the brook by 710. The brook ends 8 m short of the lane's verge, open and shallowing, with no culvert (`brook.noCulvert`) and no spring: #688 takes it on there. The farm bridge is #689's, so the river has its place (`river.bridge`, plan (420, 572)) and the crossing stands 55 m from it.
- **The joint.** `cutAt` is 0 at `minX` itself: the pasture's stream and the brook both cut there, leaving a 0.9 m pit at that one x (the issue's pit, and today's farm has it too). `groundTexture.js` carries the silt along the river from the stream's end so the bank's dirt does not stop square; `tests/riverMove.test.js` paints the joint and checks every column.
- **River gates.** Each fills its boundary's own shut gap (`river.hurdles(fields.gateways)`: the gap's middle and half, not the river's width, which left open strips either side). The three places the river crosses a boundary (x 420 and 640 in plan metres, and the farm's east side at 885) are shut gaps in `GATEWAYS_PLAN` (`shut`, no `gate`), each with a gate of two stout posts, a rail and hung boards; the fence either side is #696's. `placeGateways` and the gateways list now keep `shut` for a gap that is not a gate.
- **The meadow.** No hedge ring; Brook Meadow's own outline is `riverside`. Fourteen willows in groups with a pollard in every fifth, a crack willow by the bridge's place, six drinking places and the gateways' worn ground in `mudAt`, reeds in three heights with seed heads and dead stalks (`reedGeometry`), the oaks are #815's `bigOaks`. The five-bar gateway from Hay Meadow is two posts and a leaf hung open (`gateLeaf`).

## Move the gravel pit to the lane (#685)

- **One move, five follow-ups.** #685 held about 25 pieces of work (the review as well as the move). Jamie settled the split on 19 November 2026 ("GO as proposed"): #685 is the move; the lane entrance's dressing is #1002, the relics made disused #1003, the walls, field ground, scrub, tree line and rebuilt small things with the area caps and lighter tyres #1004, the top-of-pit props #1006 and the pit's sound #1005. The numbers measured before the move stay in the issue.
- **The field and the pit.** `quarryLayout` (`layoutNew.js`, `PIT_PLAN`) gives `layout.quarry` its own shape (`outline`, the field's corners cut to the farm's outline, with `minX` to `maxZ` its bounds) and the pit's centre (`centre`, plan (805, 108)); `dealPit` deals the pit about it exactly as before (88 by 69 m at the lip, 13 to 16 m deep). The 7 m rim squeeze (`RIM_MARGIN`) only runs for a field with no `outline`, so today's pit is dealt as it was and the new field, 165 by 185 m, never needs it. `tests/pitMove.test.js` deals 200 farm seeds on it: every one has a plant, 3 to 5 heaps and a rim inside the field with 3 m to spare.
- **A list of gates.** `gatesOf` gives `createQuarry` its gates, each `{ id, x, z, width, open, inward }` (`inward` is the unit vector into the field): `haul` is the one the road starts at (the lead-in runs from its position, bending across its own direction), `way` the open one you come in by (`pastGate`, `waitSpot` and the container's place read it, so the girls wait 3.5 m inside the farm gate), and `plantOk` keeps every gate 9 m clear. Today's single south gate is the same list with one entry, and its numbers are unchanged (`tests/pitMove.test.js`, "the same pit on today's farm").
- **The lane gate is shut for good** (Jamie, 15 November 2026): `GATEWAYS_PLAN` has it `shut: true`, so `fieldMap.js` leaves it out of every route and counts it solid (`hedgedAt`, `hedgeAhead`), and `buildPitLaneGate` makes the steel gate, chain and posts with a collider and no `Gate`, so nothing can open it. The farm gate is a five-bar field gate tied open in the west fence, a `Gate` like the south gate (`buildFiveBarGate` takes a `yaw` for a fence along z). The Done-when said both gates open until it was corrected on the issue to match this.
- **Post and wire on all four sides** (#558 closes): a boundary of kind `fence` (`FENCED` in `layoutNew.js`: every side of `pit`) is drawn by `buildPitBoundary` from the kit in `wireFence.js` and is solid because `fieldMap.js` already counts every kind but `none`. Line posts every 3 m, a strainer and strut at each corner (shared where two sides meet) and each side of a gap, three strands of barbed wire as a ribbon with the barbs drawn on it (alpha-tested, rust in the texture), 4 in 100 posts down with the wire slack, and a collider along every span that a down post does not break. The lip fence is made from the same posts and wire, with barbs and a low collider (0.9 m) along each span that has its wire, open where the road comes in and the climb goes over the lip (a test checks the road and the climb line against the boxes).
- **Flags for the climb** (`buildClimbFlags`, `startLine.js`): a pole planted 0.4 m in the ground, plumb on any slope, with a lettered cloth reaching in across the gate (green START, blue FINISH, orange between, poles 2.1 m and 2.7 m at the ends). The hill climb keeps its cones.
- **`farm` and the fence's gates.** `layout.farm` stays in `NOT_MOVED_YET`, now under #692: nothing on the new farm's pit reads it, only today's farm road and the terrain's rise beyond a farm with no landform do. The old pit's gap in the plan became the farm gate (`id: 'quarry'`) and the lane's was added (`id: 'pitlane'`).
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: see the PR. Not looked at: the lip fence from the floor, the flags at dusk.

## Back Lane: a winding single-track loop round the farm (#686)

- **The road.** `BACK_LANE_PLAN` (`layoutNew.js`) is the issue's line, smoothed by the road network: 2.63 km, not the "about 3 km" the issue says (kept as given; a test accepts 2.5 to 3.4 km). It joins the lane at both ends, so a road can now have a list of two `junction`s (`roads.js`). 3.2 m of tarmac, 14 passing places (`spacing` 190 m, 10 m long, 1.7 m wide, alternating sides), worn tarmac with tar bands and crumbling edges (`tarmacPixels` `bands`, `crumble`), a grass strip down the middle on the west and south stretches only (Jamie, 13 November), give-way lines at both ends. Outside the outline by more than 8 m everywhere (`tests/backLane.test.js`).
- **Levelled to the hill, not just the lie.** The farm road is laid to the land's lie alone. Back Lane runs over the hill pasture's flanks, where the hill adds up to 7 m of its own shape (a first try left a 4.4 m step across the road), so `level.natural` lays it to `natural` ground (`levelAt(x, z, lie, natural)`; `terrainHeight` exposes `naturalAt`). A test walks the whole centreline at 2 cm and finds no step over 3 cm. Where it climbs the hill's shoulder it runs up to 2.6 m into a bank, as a sunken old road does; two stretches are declared `cuttings`.
- **Hedges, trees and gates** (`laneHedges.js` pure, `backLaneProps.js`): a tall hedge each side at the edge of a 2.6 m verge, pushed back round a passing place, open at the lane (9 m), the ford (6 m), the stile and each gate, with a collider every 6 m and far strips by distance (`hedgeFar.js`). An oak or ash every 35 to 75 m in the hedge. A gate across from each of the farm's gateways onto the strip between (North Field, the wood, Far Field, the hill: two new gateways in `GATEWAYS_PLAN`), with gravel and mud washed out across it (`washPixels`). The gates are posts and a leaf standing open: not `Gate`s.
- **Signs** (`roadSigns.js` kit, `laneSigns.js` places): single track and the national limit at each end, a passing-place diamond each way at every bay, the bends triangle before the hairpins and three chevrons on the outside of each, a ford triangle each side, a depth gauge, a fingerpost and a stile, and a drain grate about every 330 m. No speed is enforced (Jamie, 13 November).
- **The ford.** The hill's stream used to end at the pasture's hedge. It now runs on west to the road (`stream.west`, `hill.westCut`, which fills in what the hill's profile fades out), where the same ford section dips the road, so the water over it is 0.18 m and rises with `streamLevel.js` like the lane's own ford; deep water drowns an engine there too. A concrete bed and a dressed stone kerb are laid over the road for 11 m. A ditch along the low (east) side of the west stretch is cut by `roads.ditchAt`.
- **Traffic.** `backLaneTraffic.js`: a `LaneDrive` has `Drive`'s face but moves by how far along the road it is. Two that meet head on: the one with a passing place on its left, nearer, before they meet, pulls in and waits until the other has gone by (`shouldYield`); with none either way the later-dealt one creeps. A test runs two vehicles at each other and finds one waiting in a bay, a metre and a half apart at the closest only there. `Traffic.spawnBackLane` deals one every 300 s on average (`backEvery`); they start at one end of the lane (not on the public lane first).
- **The girls drive home** by it some days (`homeVia`, `backLaneJess` 0.45, `backLaneAmy` 0.15; Jess's car is already the faster).
- **Upper Thornby** is a far version only (`upperThornby.js`, `farVersion.js`): a church with a tower and eleven houses on the plateau beyond the Moor's ridge wall, seen from the ridge (it stood at plan (-215, 745) until #1037, which was inside the Moor once #713 built it: a quad could ride through it; it is now at (-460, 745)). It is in `NOT_MODELS` of the bare-block test, as it has none of the surface detail a near piece is held to.
- **The budget.** `hairpins` in `BUDGETS`: 1,136 calls and 3.07M triangles at its `layout.budgetSpots` place on `?layout=2` (`node tools/budget.mjs --query seed=3&weather=sunny&time=15.5&layout=2`), set 8% over. Today's farm notes it and skips it.

## The A-road, the lane's bend and being turned round (#687)

- **Two lanes, not single track (Jamie, 19 November 2026).** The review suggested single track with passing places; Jamie chose two lanes so the lane reads as a real road and differs from Back Lane: 5.2 m of tarmac (`LANE_PLAN.halfWidth` 2.6, was 4.6), a dashed centre line, no passing places (a `[~]` line of the issue, agreed with Jamie at the end). The A-road is wider again, 7.3 m (`A_ROAD_PLAN.halfWidth` 3.65, the width of a rural single carriageway), with a white dashed centre line (4 m on, 8 off), edge lines broken at each side road's mouth, cat's eyes every 18 m and a 5 cm crown. Back Lane stays 3.2 m.
- **The roads.** `A_ROAD_PLAN` (the issue's line plus control points at the junctions) runs plan z -70 to 970, 1,042 m. `LANE_PLAN.north` takes the lane from the A-road at (1291, -30) west along z -31, round the bend through (936, -26) and (905, -2) (Back Lane's start, a control point of both) and straight on south at plan x 905, with control points 80 m apart so the curve does not overshoot, to z 970 where the ground ends (the far landscape beyond is #708's). `STREET_STUB_PLAN` is the village street's last 60 m at (1292, 718): #688 replaces it with the street. The road network's `junction` is `{ end: 'start', onto: 'aRoad' }`, and `bellmouth`, `edgeBreaks`, `layBys` and `camber` are new road fields drawn by `roadMesh.js` (a flared mouth with a kerb each side, a painted give-way triangle, `crownAt`).
- **No invisible walls.** The lane's walls went with #568 (`laneBarriers` is empty on the new farm); the end walls are not there either. Nothing stops a vehicle at the road's ends: `tests/aRoad.test.js` walks every road's middle line to its end and finds no hedge or box across it.
- **Her land cut back (#569).** `NEIGHBOUR_LAND_PLAN`: the north edge is z -18 (the lane runs along z -31 and bends down), the lane hedge starts at z 8 and the north hedge at x 950 where the bend is past, the east hedge is at x 1282 (the A-road's own edge is at 1287 at the nearest), and the cottages' plot (plan 912 to 960, 405 to 450) is not hedged across. A test checks that no hedge stretch is within a metre of any road's tarmac.
- **Traffic.** `laneRoutes.js` (pure) lays waypoints every 10 m along the lane (30 m cut the bend's corner by 5 m), with the A-road's leg in or out: a pass comes in from the A-road's north or south end, round the bend and out of the lane's south end, or the other way, and a visitor does the same (`places.route.in`/`out`); the girls' third way home (`via` 'aRoad', `aRoadAmy` and `aRoadJess` of what is left after Back Lane) runs north up the lane and out onto the A-road to where it leaves the map. The A-road's own vehicles are `LaneDrive`s with nothing to watch, dealt every 5 to 20 s (`aRoadEvery` 12 gives 0.4 to 1.6 times that: 4.8 to 19.2 s), 22 to 27 m/s, from `A_ROAD_KINDS`: the girls' Finch and Griffin, the Crag as a van, and a box lorry (`lorryModel.js`, new, drawn only).
- **Being turned round.** `turnRound.js` (pure): lines laid on the network from `layout.turnRound` (each junction's give-way line at 3 m from its end, and the lane's southern line at plan z 955, `wide` across the whole ground so there is no way round it), `crossed`, `putBack` (25 m back up the road on its left as you face home, or back along your own way across open ground), and a `TurnRound` state: out over 0.6 s, a 0.25 s hold at black where you are moved once, in over 0.6 s. A step over 30 m in a frame is a teleport, not a crossing; being on the A-road at all (from open ground, by no line) turns you round too. `TurnRoundHost` (inside `TrafficHost`) reads where you are (your vehicle's if you are on one, in any seat), uses the HUD's fade, moves the vehicle with `placeAt` (stopped, with anyone aboard) or you with `moveTo`, and the girl nearest says one of four lines (`turnRound`, `turnRoundSouth` for the lane's south) or, alone, the toast. Tuning group 'Turn round'; console `lane.turnRound` and `lane.turnYouRound`.
- **The look.** Junction furniture from `roadSigns.js` (a give-way triangle sign, fingerpost arms with the places on both faces) and the new `roadFurniture.js` (grit bin, grit heap, postbox, bin stand, bus stop flag). The pair of cottages are the village's own (`buildCottage` with a new `hollow` option, so they are panels round a shell, not a solid block), turned to face the lane. Trees no longer scatter on a road or its verge, or in the cottages' plot.
- **Measured.** The frame budget's movers (every pooled traffic body, as if all in view) went from 5,275 to 5,406 calls with the A-road's four extra bodies (one paint each for the Finch and Griffin, two for the lorry), under its 5,700; the five spots are on today's farm and did not move. A junction budget spot was not added.
- **After the recheck (#1022).** The give-way lines, painted and turn-round, were 3 m from the A-road's middle, inside its tarmac, so only the "on the A-road at all" catch fired and it put you back along your own way: they are now `GIVE_WAY_AT` 5.2 m, and the catch puts you back up the nearest junction's road. Towing: a quad on the tow rope is left behind when you are put back (#1023).
- **Past the map's edge, and the verges (#1025, Jamie: "as recommended on both").** The A-road runs 150 m past the plan's ends (1,342 m of tarmac), so what leaves by it is gone 190 m from a junction and not 41, and the "on the A-road" catch counts its verge (half width plus `verge`, by the nearest road) so you cannot walk it to the map's edge unturned. The road, its hedges and verge plants beyond the ground are drawn by the same builders; #708 puts the far landscape round them.

## Nether Thornby across the lane, behind ?layout=2 (#688)

- **Decided with Jamie (19 November 2026).** All eleven cottages the plan's map draws (six north, five south) are placed now, with today's `buildCottage`; the new house designs and front gardens stay with #819 (#543). The issue also takes Change 10 of the review (#386, the garage canopy and forecourt: they are rebuilt on the lane anyway). The village's sound and its night lighting are filed on their own. The brook is carried east from a new lane bridge in this issue.
- **Placed from the plan** (`VILLAGE_PLAN`, `villageLayout`, `layoutNew.js`): one anchor at plan (1000, 710), every building centred on its footprint on the map at the size it was built at (pub 17 by 11 inside the plan's 22 by 12, shop 11 by 9 inside 14 by 10, garage 16 by 8.4 inside 22 by 9, so the "about" in the issue's numbers), facing the street (yaw 0 north of it, Math.PI south of it). `village`, `anchors.village` and the street are `REPLACED`; the church's plot, the hall and the playing field are `clear` rectangles a test keeps empty (#715, #716).
- **A street that goes through.** `layout.village.end`, the walls, the gate and the road beyond it are gone on the new farm (`S.closed` false, `S.walls` empty): the village code draws a road that runs east from the lane's east edge (`S.eastward`: the mouth clamps from the other side with `clampMin`, the give-way line is on the south half, the signs and the village sign turn the other way, the car park's apron takes the north edge) as far as the A-road's stub, which is the street's last 60 m as a road of the network (`streetEnd`, drawn by `roadMesh`). It is a single smooth street of 325 m (two roads of the network joined end to end), with a lamp every 15 m and a 20 mph repeater every 80 m.
- **The garage on the lane** faces south (yaw 0): its MOT bay, the bay's world place, the parts rack's and the clipboard's stand were all worked out for a north-facing garage and now read its yaw (`MOT_BAY`, `village.js`). Its forecourt is its own rectangle reaching the lane's east edge (its mouth), pumps at plan x 922, 930 and 938, z 606; the price board stands by the lane face-on to its traffic. **The land falls east 6% along the 46 m forecourt**, so a flat pad left a 1.1 m step in front of the doors and 1.7 m under the lane at the mouth: only the pumps' island and the bay are levelled (to the ground at the island's middle and the garage's own floor), and the slab follows the land.
- **The ground is the land's own** (`flat: false`): no plane along the street, since it runs 325 m across a fall; each building, the car park, the island and the bay are levelled as before.
- **The bridge** (`brook.bridge`, `buildLaneBridge`, `brookProps.js`): the brook runs on under the lane (the culvert's span, no channel across the tarmac) and east on past the north of the village to a spring 30 m short of the A-road's verge, not on under it (the plan draws it on to the map's edge; one culvert, at the lane). The bridge is a stone parapet each side of the road with end piers and copings (solid), and an arched headwall at each end of the span. The brook's waypoints follow the plan's curve through (905, 653) and (1000, 674).
- **The village sign is at the junction** (the layout line), not the bridge's end as the review suggests: the bridge is 60 m north of it, and the sign is read by what turns in.
- **Measured** (`tools/budget.mjs` on `?layout=2`, built from main and from this branch): the village spot 2,087 calls and 3.10M triangles on main, 2,135 and 3.43M here, under its 2,150 and 3.77M. A first try at 2,151 had every lamp post a group of its own: all lamps and road signs are now one group (`ctx.furniture`) that merges to a mesh or two a material. The other spots on layout 2 are within a few calls of main (spawn 989 to 994, yard 2,358 to 2,378).
- **Looked at** with a camera in the running game on `?layout=2`: the street from the junction, the pub and its beer garden, the car park, the shop front, the cottages, the garage from four sides, the bridge from both ends. Not looked at: the village at dusk, the brook east of the village from the A-road side, a vehicle on the bridge.

## After the recheck of the village move (#1031)

- **The canopy's lights were inside its band.** The navy band is a closed box, so the lit panels and the pump lights centred inside it were never seen (a ray up from the forecourt hit navy, not glow); they hang under its bottom face now.
- **The forecourt's markings** are worked out in `village.js` (`forecourtMarkings`, `motBayRect`): the far line and sides of each bay and the drain are on the side away from the garage, so none lies in the MOT bay on either layout (on today's farm, where the garage faces north, the first version put a drain on the bay's back line).
- **The new farm's mud grid** was one rectangle over the village's whole 325 by 226 m area (294,000 cells, 0.2 ms a frame): it is now a grid on the street's bounds, one on the car park and one on the forecourt (about 25,000 cells).
- **The bridge** is merged to a mesh a material (it was 28) and named in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`.

## The Moor beyond Back Lane (#713)

- **What is on it** (`MOOR_FEATURES_PLAN`, `moor.js`, plan metres; `layout.moor` in game metres): the ridge wall (1.35 m, 0.6 m thick, wandering from x -327 to -337, 880 m), the ladder stile at its vertex (-334, 612) where `RESERVED_PLAN`'s footpath ends, the trig pillar (-330, 480, 7 m inside the wall) with the cairn 26 m south-east of it, the spring on the stream's own line at (-200, 571) (the issue says about (-200, 560); the stream wanders 2.5 m about z 570 and the spring is where it is), nine flushes, two peat banks, four sheep tracks from the moor gate and the lane up to the ridge, 26 outcrops and 60 boulders dealt on a fixed seed (the Moor is the same every game: it is not the farm's character).
- **The ground** runs out to x -345 (`GROUND_PLAN`, was -70): 1,695 by 1,040 m, about 1.76 M grid samples. The land's lie was already there (`LANDFORM_PLAN`, #813); `reliefAt` adds outcrops and the peat cut on top, and a test holds the whole common, 30 m clear of the lane's own cuttings, under 38 degrees. A peat face is 0.9 m over 2.4 m. The stream's `west.minX` (`BACK_LANE_PLAN.ford.west`) is now -200, so the hill's channel and ribbon run to the spring, and a test holds the channel cut east of it and gone west of it.
- **Back Lane's cattle grids** are the lane's own points nearest the plan's (60, 34) and (-30, 840): the north one is 16 m from it, the south one is 25 m before the hairpin at plan (-14, 818), not on the bend, where a grid would be on a corner. The common's side (west, side -1) is unhedged between them (`moorStretch`, a gap in `laneGaps`, trees and verge follow it); the farm's side keeps its hedge, and a bridle gate stands in the hedge beside each grid, 3.2 m outside the stretch. The rattle of the drive's grid (`createCattleGrid`, vibration) is not carried to these: that is #821's ("sound").
- **The wall is whole.** A ladder stile climbs over a wall, so the wall has no gap and every ray across it hits a collider (a test casts them the whole length, at three heights). Climbing is crossing the turn-round line 1.2 m short of the wall at the stile, 6 m long, with a short line from each end to the wall so sliding along the wall's face to the ladder crosses one (#1035) (`TURN_ROUND_PLAN`'s `moor-stile`, three `free` lines: `turnRound.js` puts you back along your way, or 25 m east from the debug button); the capsule's centre stops 0.6 m from the wall's middle, so it always reaches the line first (a Rapier test walks it).
- **The moor gate** is a gateway of `GATEWAYS_PLAN` (`id: 'moor'`, 4.9 m, the nearest point of the outline to `RESERVED_PLAN.moorGate`) with the bypass gate's steel leaves turned to the wall's line, shut, and a gate in the lane's hedge across from it.
- **West of the lane only** (#1035): outcrops, boulders and every plant are placed with `onCommon` (`moor.js`, west of `laneEdgeAt` less the verge), the same edge the ground's painting uses; the first version scattered them over the farm's verge as well.
- **The plants** are tiled instances with the draw distance (`roughDistance`, 75 m): 44,000 heather clumps (an 80-face dome, its normals leaning up so it reads as one soft clump), 6,500 bracken and 6,500 tussocks with the cotton grass's heads over half of them. Their colour is the material's, set from the farm's date (`moorCover.js`, `props.moor.setDate`): heather brown, purple from the middle of July to the end of September; bracken green from May, rust from October; cotton grass white through June, gone by August. Where they grow is `moor.js` (`heatherAt`, `brackenAt`, `wetAt`), which the ground's painting (`moorPaint.js`, west of the lane only) reads too, so the paint and the plants agree.
- **Looked at** on `?layout=2` in two rounds: the stile and wall from the east, the trig pillar and wall from the ridge, a cattle grid on the lane, the cairn, a flush with the spring, a peat bank and an outcrop. Round one's heather read as angular tents (one jitter a corner of a non-indexed dome cracked it open); a shared jitter per place and upward normals fixed it.

## The Moor on the frame budget (#713, measured after the recheck)

- `tools/budget.mjs` on `?layout=2` (seed 3, sunny, 15.5), built from the commit before the Moor (75de4750) and after it. The budgets are today's farm's, so every layout-2 spot but the yard and movers reads over them already; the change is what matters: spawn +5 calls (+0.06M triangles), yard -11, village +90, wood -3, hilltop +195 (-0.13M), hairpins +404 calls (-0.04M), movers the same. The hairpins spot looks west across the common's open side and the hilltop spot up the hill: the heather, bracken, tussock and rock tiles (each a draw call per 30 m tile, within `roughDistance` 75 m) and the wall's tiles are what they added. No load time or frame time was measured on layout 2: `tools/perf.mjs` has no layout option.
- The cairn (about 60 stone meshes) and the spring ring (13) are not merged; they are near-only (the `moor` set piece), seen from none of the spots.

## Room for an articulated lorry (#816)

- **What.** A 16.5 m lorry (`LORRY` in `src/world/lorryPath.js`: a tractor with the pin on its rear axle and a 13.6 m trailer, 2.55 m wide) is driven in a model through the farmstead (`lorryJourneys`) and swept against everything that stands there: `tests/lorryPath.test.js` fails on any building, kept-clear place, the garden, a car or a trailer within 0.3 m. A reversing leg is swept as the drive away from where it ends, which is the same journey and is stable; each leg ends where the next begins and the test checks they meet.
- **The numbers** (`LORRY_PLAN`, plan metres). The turning circle is 25 m across at (478, 355.4): its north side is the drive's line, and a lorry on full lock sweeps 12.5 m at its outer front corner on a rear-axle circle of 10.4 m, so the middle is 10.4 m south of the line. It is kept clear as the square round it (`keptClear`, 'lorry turning circle'). A lorry coming into the turn swings its tail out to 16.75 m from the middle; nothing stands there either. The sheep bay is at the pens' north face, x 460, the tail at z 383 facing north; the grain bay at the store's west face, z 367, facing west. The ramp and the door are #695's and #699's to place on those faces.
- **The drive** was 6 m wide, over the 4 m asked, and gets a bellmouth of 12 m radius at the lane (`DRIVE_PLAN.bellmouth`, `fillets` on its road, `roads.js`): hard standing painted and gripped as tarmac between each edge and an arc (`filletDistance`, `filletOutline`).
