Turned in on a rear-axle radius of 11 m, the lorry's wheels stay within 1.5 m of the tarmac (1.2 m measured); a rear-axle radius of 18 m leaves them 3 m out.
- **Moved: the tipping trailer**, from plan (562, 366), where the grain store's bay swept over it (the test shows the hit), to (588, 394) south of the store. Nothing else needed moving: the sprayer, harrows and trailer are clear.
- **Kept clear** are also the scrap corner behind the machinery shed (plan 412.5 to 424 by 325 to 371, from the rail Home Ground's seats are on: the plan's 412 put two seats in it) and the last 11 kV pole's spot by the farmhouse (566, 274).
- **Left:** the lorry itself and its journeys (#707, #699, #122); where it would stand to be loaded is the bays above. The lorry's exit turn onto the lane is the entry turned the other way and is not swept again.

## The yard dressed: concrete, furniture, gates, the stackyard and the yard's sound (#817, part a)

- **What.** On `?layout=2` the yard's concrete is painted with tarred expansion joints (a dark core with a pale lip), patched and cracked bays, a central drain to a gully grate by the barn doors, darker tyre paths with polished wheel lines, mud fans at the ways in, a scraped muck line at the barn's wall foot and puddles (`yardGround.js`, replacing `paintSlab`). The stackyard is hardcore, painted in 0.4 m cells so it reads as crushed stone. Places are `FARMSTEAD_PLAN.yardKit` in plan metres, taken to game metres by `yardKitLayout` into `layout.farmstead.yard`.
- **Standing water** is a gloss disc over each low spot, shown by `standingWater(wet)` from the ground's wetness (none under 0.15, a full sheet from 0.65) and hidden altogether when dry. It is only a look: no grip and no drowning, unlike the track's puddles.
- **The kit** (`yardKit.js`, merged by material: 1 to 14 meshes a group): the hose reel, brush, shovel, bucket, baler twine on a nail and a leant pallet on the barn's **east end wall** (the door wall is covered by the doors' leaves, found on the first look), built in their own frame and turned onto the wall; five pallets and six tyres at the west corner, the gully's grate, nettle cards by `tiledInstances` along the wall feet, at the heaps, the gully and the gateposts; two steel gates, hung open on stone posts at the Home Ground rail and timber at the south hedge (tests tie each to a real gateway); the stackyard's square bales stacked in bond in the Dutch barn's west bay (clear of its stanchions and its round bales), two round bales on the hardcore, loose straw and a bale spike. The Dutch barn already had its round-bale stack.
- **Gates and marks (Jamie, 20 November, after the recheck).** Three gates, one at each hedge or rail gateway of the farmstead (Home Ground, the south field, Top Field); the drive's 8 m mouth has none, so the lorry (#816) has the whole width. The painted drain, tyre paths and mud stop short of the kept-clear rectangles (the drain at plan z 385, the second path at 380, the third path at x 446), and the mud at Home Ground's gate waits for #617 and #712; a test holds it.
- **Sound.** `yardHeard` (`yardBed.js`) is 1 on the slab, fading to 0 over 14 m beyond it, scaled by how far outdoors you are, so it is silent in the barn and on today's farm. `EngineAudio.setYard` plays loose tin (the tin-rain ticks slowed and put through a band that opens with the wind) and a drip every 0.8 to 3.2 s (faster in rain), at `yardVolume` (0.07; range 0 to 0.5). It rides on `setRain`'s call, as `game.js` is at its line ceiling.
- **The wrapped bales** behind the barn are not hard to reach on this layout: a test proves a 7 m approach from the west, a metre wider than the stack either side, clear of walls, the orchard, the garden and the kept-clear places.
- **The paddock** was already gone with Home Ground's rails (#682): `tests/farmstead.test.js` holds it.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: the barn wall's gear, the heaps, a gate at each post, the stackyard's bales and spike.
- **Not in this part:** the bays, bowsers, lights, vane (part b) and the pit (part c).

## The yard's lights, the vane, the barn's trim, the bays and the bowsers (#817, part b)

- **Floodlights (#171).** Two black halogen floodlights (finned back, hood, glass front, a round PIR sensor under, a bracket on the wall or arm) in `yardLights.js`: one on the barn's door gable beside the hay door, one on a creosoted timber pole 6.2 m high behind the two tanks (a concrete collar, a steel arm and brace, a junction box, conduit up it). Each has a `SpotLight` made once at world build and put in the scene, not in the far-detail group, which hides, so a light never goes missing and recompiles every shader. `yardLightLevel(darkness, powered)` is 0 below darkness 0.3 and 1 from 0.55 (a smoothstep), and 0 with the generator off: they run off it like the barn's bulb, so on a farm that never starts it they never light. That is Jamie's to change (a mains light that needs no generator is one line). Two more spot lights are always in the scene on the new farm; measured with `tools/perf.mjs --query layout=2` (2 runs each, this Mac's GPU), with the lights and without them (the builder returning null): the frame time was the same within the noise at every spot (spawn 11.3 against 10.6 ms, yard 15.6 against 15.4, village 16.6 against 16.5, wood 14.2 against 15.6, hairpins 15.8 against 16.7, hilltop 13.7 against 14.9), so they stay out of the vehicle lights' pool. Both runs are over the 8 ms budget everywhere, as before. Cone 0.75 rad, 120 candela, the pole's aimed 5 m out so the tanks beside it are lit too.
- **The vane (#169).** An iron cockerel and a tail-finned arrow on a spindle, a gilt ball and collar, fixed N, S, E and W arms with letters, a bracket on the ridge cap, 1.7 m in from the door gable. `vaneHeading` brings the arrow round the short way to face into the wind (`wind.heading` plus a half turn) at a rate that rises with the wind, and flutters it in a gust. `weatherHost.updateWind` turns it (and sets the lights), so `game.js` did not grow.
- **Barn trim (`barnTrim.js`).** Fascia and barge boards, half-round gutters on both outer eaves with brackets and stop ends, a swan-necked downpipe on clips to a shoe at each span's west end, the valley gutter's end stops and a drop, a timber rain hood and cover battens over the second door, a concrete pad and two knee braces at each divider post. Merged by material, no colliders.
- **Bays (`bayKit.js`, #817).** The painted yellow rectangles are gone: a precast wheel stop across the back (72% of the bay's width, 10 cm), an L of hazard-striped kerb at each corner (7 cm, so a quad rides over it, none has a collider), an oil stain decal and a galvanised drip tray with a skin of oil. A test keeps the stain and tray out of the big bay's pit. The line-up sleepers gained pegging spikes and tufts of weed at their back edge and ends.
- **Bowsers (#614).** `FuelTank` stands on a concrete slab (2.7 by 1.7 m, 10 cm proud, 30 cm sunk; no collider on the slab, the kerbs or the wheel stops, so wheels pass through them) set by `bowserStand`: the top is the highest ground under it plus the rise, the bottom the lowest less the sink, so a slope buries the high side and never leaves a gap (a test on the new farm's real ground). The tank has domed ends, weld beads, foot plates, a filler cap and a painted texture, its label is lit like the rest, and the hose runs from the valve to the nozzle hung in a holster on a steel post. Nothing else changed in how it is used.
- **Pens.** The hens' run and the pig's fence (`farmAnimalsView.js`) had flat-colour rails: now textured, capped posts with the rails on their outer face.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: the vane, the tanks, the pole, the bays, the roofs, the annex door and the yard at 21:00 with the generator running (one round, then a second for the kerbs and the pole's aim).
- **Not in this part:** the pit (part c).

## The inspection pit as a real hole (#817, part c)

- **Settled with Jamie.** The pit looks like a trench but is crossed at floor level (he answered "As you recommend" when the options were put to him). The ground is one 1 m heightfield, drawn and physical, which Rapier 0.21 cannot punch a hole in, and the player and the girls stand on `heightAt`; making it walkable means lowering the collider, a pit-aware height for the girls and a way past the player's clamp (`player.js`), which break the rule that `heightAt` and the collider agree. That is a separate issue if he ever wants to climb down. #329's stuck-or-tip part stays on #329.
- **What is cut.** The barn's slab is an extrusion with the opening cut out (`barnBuild.js`, same concrete scale on every piece), and `pitPlug`, a slab-thick piece of the same concrete outside the merged barn group, fills it while the pit isn't bought. The drawn ground gets a `pitHole` rectangle (`terrain.js`, a fragment `discard`, the same pattern as `quarryRect`); `ShedView.cutPit` shows the plug and sets the rectangle (`pitHoleRect`, world axes however the barn is turned). The physics ground is untouched.
- **The pit (`buildPit`).** 1.4 m deep, 3.8 by 0.9 m: limewashed block walls with grime and damp, a floor with a drain channel, a sump grate and oil, four steps down at the +x end with steel nosings and a grab rail, two caged lamps (emissive tubes, no light added, dark when the generator stops like the barn's bulbs: `ShedView.setLamps`), the steel kerb with hazard edging and a chequer-plate roll-up cover lying over the -x end with its drum standing beyond it. `tests/pitModel.test.js` casts rays down it for the depth, the steps and the cover, checks the slab's hole and plug, and runs the coplanar check.
- **Jobs.** The four pit jobs are done at the bench menu and need only the vehicle over the pit (`atPit`), so nothing asks you to stand in it.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2 --run "farm.pit on" --eye ... --look ...` (new `--run` option): from above the open end and from the south-west, one round.

## The barn as one building (#818)

- **Settled with Jamie** (20 November 2026, "All as recommended"): the barn fills the plan's 498 to 524 by 319 to 337 as one building, 26 m along its long south wall (the doors' wall, an eaves wall) by 18 m deep, with one pair of doors 6.4 m wide and 3.9 m high. The hayloft goes with the hay door, hide and seek in the hay, Jess's straw, the loft lines and the achievement. Fonts are two roles from one place. The weather is one place by the doors.
- **Shape.** `barnPlan.js` writes the new barn's numbers and everything inside it in the barn's own frame (+x to the doors, z across). `barnShape.wide` says the doors are in an eaves wall with the ridge running along it: `barnWide.js` draws walls as extruded outlines (so the boards run on across the doorway instead of starting again), steel portal frames every 3.25 m, tin on 18 m of span, gables with two windows and a louvre each, and the one pair of leaves; `barnTrim.js` `buildWideTrim` the fascia, gutters, a downpipe at each end of each eave and the barge boards. Today's two spans are untouched (`twoSpanShell`), so layout 1 and the smoke test are as they were.
- **Yard.** The barn grew over the yard's old frame, so the yard's anchor moved 7.2 m south to keep the doors 10 m from it, the petrol tank, the diesel bowser and what stands by them went to the barn's east end (`tanksMove`, off the way to the doors: the visitors' test), the generator to the west wall, the bell beside the doors, the wall gear, pallets and tyres to the new walls, the silage back to z 313.5.
- **Lamps.** Six bulbs hang from the purlins in two rows. They share three `PointLight`s (every light in the scene is paid for in every shader), and `game.js` gives each light `2 x BARN_BULB / lights`, so the lit barn is as bright overall as with two.
- **Weather, clock, calendar.** The thermometer, barometer and forecast slate are one narrower board on the doors' wall beside the doorway; the clock is its own `WallClock` high on the bench's wall; the calendar stays on the back wall with the boards.
- **Inside.** Bales are scattered (four, each turned its own way) by the back wall; a farm desk and stool (`workshop/deskModel.js`) by the boards; the pegboard is gone until there are real tools to hang on it (#517); the helmet hooks and the jumpers' rack on the south side wall, reached by a way in round the big bay. The computer for the classifieds is #806's.
- **Fonts.** `boardFont.js`: `board` (hand-written: job board, photo board, slate, calendar's month, notebook) and `label` (printed: ads, dials, tin and rack labels, charger), system stacks without Comic Sans, `--font-board` and `--font-label` for the stylesheets, a test that fails on a stray font in the barn's files. The rest of the game's text is #807's.
- **Not done here.** #465 (shadows from the lamps), #246 (the shelf and the bottles), #212 (the radio) and #503 (what each board is for) each have their own issue; the new barn leaves them their wall. The loft's code, voice lines, FAQ answers and achievement stay for today's barn and go when #692 retires layout 1; the FAQ is brought up to the new barn then.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: the four sides and the doors' wall close up, then three from inside, two rounds. The first showed the floodlight on the roof and the vane floating above it (both placed for the old gable), fixed in the second.

## The stone tracks, the boundary track and the headlands (#689)

- **Settled with Jamie** (20 November 2026, "1. A"): the boundary track goes round the Old Pit's field outside its fence (down x 700 in Top Field, through one gateway in the hedge to Lane Field, east at plan z 214 to rejoin the east side) rather than through it, because the pit keeps its two gates (#685): a gap in the fence would be a way in for the girls. The rest is the outline drawn in 14 m (`insetPolygon`), 3.4 km, with a detour to the green lane's ford instead of wading the stream where it meets the west side.
- **Built in `farmTracks.js`** (pure, plan metres) and `tracksLayout()` in `layoutNew.js`: `stoneNorth`, `stoneSouth` (surface `stone`) and `boundaryTrack` (a new surface `grass`) are roads of the network, painted into the ground (`drawTrack`, `planTrack` in `groundTexture.js`). Straight pieces are densified every 25 to 30 m because the Catmull-Rom curve bowed a 760 m side out by 7 m. The boundary track's 11 crossings are open gateways added to `GATEWAYS_PLAN` by `trackGateways`.
- **How they ride.** `fields.stoneAt` feeds the surface feel as gravel at `stoneLoose` 0.45 of the pit's looseness and exempts the ground from wear; `fields.laneAt` includes the grass track so it wears three times as fast and recovers a fifth as fast as the green lane does; the instanced grass skips both. Routes: `createFieldMap` takes `trackAt` and takes a quarter off a walk between two gateway points that runs along a track (worked out once per pair).
- **Headlands.** `layout.headlands` has a 6 m ring for each of the six arable fields; `fields.stubbleAt` is 0 on the headland and on tracks, so no stalks stand there and the ground is painted grass (`paintHeadland`). #697 reads `fields.headlandAt` when it grows a crop.
- **The farm bridge** (`farmBridge.js`, `FARM_BRIDGE_SIZE`): over the river's head at plan (424, 572), the track's crossing; the shut river gate at the line x 420 stands 4 m west of it. Walked over with a capsule in `tests/farmBridge.test.js`. The east brook, where the boundary track meets it at plan (871, 650), is the same 0.32 m-deep channel as the hill stream, so the track wades it; `brook.deepAt` is 0 there.
- **Home Ground and the sheep field** are the two fields no track enters: the first opens straight onto the yard, the second's gate is on the south track.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: the bridge's four sides and close-up, a length of the stone track and the grass track with a headland. The grass was growing through the stone track (fixed: the grass scatter skips tracks); the first bridge had a dirt disc for its worn bank that floated over the abutment (now painted into the ground).


## The track discount keeps to its points (#1058)

- The girls' discount along tracks (`trackCost`, `fieldMap.js`) was cached by node number, and the nodes are numbered over the gateways that are not shut, so a gate shutting made cached shares belong to other pairs: 8 of 400 probe routes differed from a fresh map. It is now kept by the two points themselves. The green lane's console value and panel buttons say they cover the boundary track too (the wear's lane includes it, #689); the stone track's FAQ line says what the grip is.

## The jobs on the new fields (#691)

- **Each job's field** is `JOB_FIELDS` in `src/world/jobFields.js`: muck on Long Piece, spraying on Lane Field, harrowing and silage on Low Ley, hay on Hay Meadow, logs at Hollow Wood, the hens and pig in the farmstead's orchard. Until the crop year decides (#697, #698) the muck, spraying and harrowing fields are the ones settled with Jamie on 13 November 2026.
- **The area is the polygon.** The jobs still read a rectangle (`layout.hayMeadow`, `sprayField`, `harrowField`); on layout 2 it is the field's bounds and carries `poly`, so a cell of a job's grid that is not in the field is not live (`liveCells`: index -1, left out of the coverage, the picture and the saved cells). Spraying uses the headland-free middle (6 m in, `HEADLAND_WIDTH`), hay and harrowing the whole field. The three keys come off `NOT_MOVED_YET` and onto `REPLACED`.
- **Settled with Jamie** (20 November 2026, "1. a"): the field is the place only, and a job is done after about today's area wherever on the field it is done. A share of the real fields was 4.5 km of boom (21 minutes at top speed, 19 tank fills) for spraying and 8.6 km (19 minutes) for harrowing, against a minute or two today, and a day from a morning start lasts about 40 minutes. `areaNeeded` is 680 m² sprayed and 350 m² harrowed, and a job is done at the less of that and `coverNeeded` of the field (`Spraying.needed`), so today's small patches keep their share and their length. Hay was already a count (60 cells cut, 8 bales), about 1% of the meadow.
- **Muck** counts only on the field the job names (`layout.jobFields.muck`), the toast says which. **Silage:** the wrapped stack moved out to Low Ley (plan (700, 545), 60 m in from the Brook Meadow gateway); the jerry can that stood by it is now "behind the barn". The chalkboard names each job's field by its dealt name (`jobsFor`, "Spray Lane Field"); the girls say none of it (`fieldPhrase`).
- **The hay's long grass** is capped at 60,000 tufts (`TUFTS_MAX`), each wider as it thins (not taller: #1073, the tallest blade was reaching 2.8 m), as the whole meadow (2 ha) would otherwise be 285,000. Looked at with `tools/shot.mjs --layout 2`: it still reads as a full meadow.
- **Not done here:** feeding the sheep in the sheep field. The feed job has only ever been the hens and the pig (their hunger, bucket and save are keyed to the two), a sheep stop is a new mechanic and the troughs are #710.

## Nether Thornby's approach and the village fixes (#819, with #536, #537, #539, #542 and #543)

- **Where.** `VILLAGE_PLAN.approach` in `layoutNew.js`, in plan metres: three houses at plan x 927, z 500, 526 and 552 facing west to the lane (`yaw` a quarter turn, so `Frame` takes a sine as well as a cosine and `solid` swaps the half sizes), the small farm's yard at (932 to 982, 442 to 478) with the farmhouse on its north side and the barn on its south, its gateway in the west wall at z 459.5 on `farmTrack` (a road of the network that joins the lane), and nine signs. They are `layout.village.approach`; the buildings are `S.laneBuildings` in `village.js` (kept apart from `S.buildings`, which the street's tests read) but count as built on and are levelled like the rest, with the yard its own pad.
- **The neighbour's hedge.** Her lane hedge (x 912) is open from z 405 to 555 so the approach's frontage is not a second hedge behind the houses' own, and the second of #687's two field gateways moved from z 505 to 459.5, the farm's track mouth. Her south field is unchanged: the houses stand in its west edge.
- **30 and 20.** The plan says 30 signs, #537 says 20 inside and none on the lane, so the 30s stand on the three approaches and the 20s inside the street. Each post's back shows the national limit to the traffic leaving. `streetLimitSigns` and `approachSignSpecs` in `village.js` are pure so the tests read them.
- **Designs (#543).** `villageHouses.js`: `thatched` (a new `thatchTexture`, eyebrow dormers), `gabled` (the kit's new `crossGable`), `bungalow`, `terrace` (three doors) and `farmhouse`, with `buildCottage` still the plain pair of storeys. The street has all five; n7 and a bench fill the end opposite the pub. The street's old garden sheds and the pub's kitchen extension were solid blocks the bare-block test had on its known list; they are boards round a hollow now and the list is empty.
- **Fixes.** The village sign, kiosk, lamps, bench and signs are in `villageFurniture.js`; every part of each touches another (`tests/villageApproach.test.js` walks the contacts, and fails a lantern with no hanger). The picnic table has A-frame legs and shared bearers. The car park's bays and symbols are `carParkBays` and `carParkTexture`; its cars are dealt from the game's random stream each game. The first house's bicycle is gone, not mended.
- **Pavements (#536).** The street's pavements already stop at the car park's dropped kerb and keep off every building and its garden on the new farm's street (`tests/villageApproach.test.js`); the garage is on the lane here, so there is no forecourt on the street to run to.

## Riding the Moor: bogs, a summer flock, wind and birds, the grids (#821)

- **The flushes bog.** `moor.bogAt` is 0.9 of `wetAt` (full in a flush's middle, nothing past its wet edge) and `heights.bogAt` takes the larger of it and the layout's bogs, so `mudAt`, `world.bogAt` and `bog.js`'s grip and sinking read it with no new call site: a quad's grip there is 0.37 (`tests/moorBog.test.js`). A flush is 10 to 16 m across, so a quad that sinks is at most a few metres from firm ground; rocking and the winch work as on the farm's bogs (not tried in play).
- **Rocks are cut off with the plants.** The heather, bracken and tussocks already were; the rocks now register on `roughDistance` too (75 m), and `tests/moorCutoff.test.js` holds all four to it.
- **Both grids rattle.** `createLaneGrid` (`cattleGrid.js`) is a grid at an angle on a lane (`onGrid`, `barsAlong`), `world.cattleGrids` holds the drive's and the Moor's two, and `RideAudio` takes the grid nearest the quad (`world.gridNear`, 8 m) for the knocks and the vibration model. The deck is `MOOR_GRID_LENGTH` by the lane's width plus 0.5 m, as built.
- **The flock is a stand-in for #707** (Jamie, 22 November: "as recommended"). `moorFlock.js`: from May to September (farm months 4 to 8) a flock of its own, `moorCount` 12 sheep, grazes the common near the moor gate and may graze the lane's verge and its west half between the grids (`LANE_BAND` 9 m, `LANE_FLOCK_REACH` 1.5 m), out of flushes, outcrops, peat and the wall. None in winter; the hill's flock and the sheep field's are untouched; the moor gate stays shut. #707's `stockYear.js` replaces the rule (and #707 depends on #821, so it could not be left to it).
- **Sound** is `moorBed.js` and `moorAudio.js`, procedural: `moorHeard` is 1 on the common and fades over 40 m east of the lane; the ambient wind is up to `moorWind` (1.2) times louder again there, in a calm too; curlews (March to June) and skylarks (March to July) call by day through a pan and a low-pass, every 14 s and 9 s on average, `moorVolume` 0.05. It rides on `setRain`'s call, as the yard's does, because `game.js` is at its line ceiling.
- **The girls** (`moorLines.js`, `moorSense.js`): on the back on the common above `voiceMoorSpeed` (6 m/s) Jess wants to open it up and Amy asks for care (`moorOpen`); a flush 5 to 13 m ahead on the heading (`moorBog`); sheep within 18 m ahead (`moorSheep`, on the lane too). Three lines each per key, merged last so no earlier bank moves. The moor gate is already one of the places they drive out to (#690); the trig pillar was not added, as nothing has tried a girl's quad on the ridge.
- **Budget spots** `moorRidge` (beside the trig pillar) and `moorMiddle` (the common's middle): measured when it was built (1,345 calls and 2.44M triangles, and 1,442 and 2.27M); re-measured with all the others in #692 (see "Opening the new farm (#692)").

## The far landscape (#708)

- **A ring from the skirt's edge out 3.5 km** (`farLand.js` pure, `farLandView.js` the meshes, called from `terrain.js` on the new farm only). The skirt (`createOuterRing`, 4 m cells, vertex colours, `outerMargin` 290 m beyond the ground) stays; the ring starts at its outer edge and uses the same height function there, so the two share heights at the join. Its cells start 24 m across and grow 1.15 times a step to 380 m, along the edge 40 m. About 20 thousand triangles.
- **Its land** is `landform` (#813) carried on, plus what the far country adds, reached over 260 m so there is no step: the far hillside west of the moor's plateau (60 m over 1.7 km), fields rising north (75 m over 2.9 km), the valley carrying on south (28 m), the far side of the valley east (62 m), and hills round the horizon topping out at about 2.8 km and dropping behind, so the rim at 3.5 km is a valley they hide. The valley (x about 450 to 1900) is flat. Upper Thornby (#686) stays where it is, on the plateau inside the skirt.
- **Painted in two parts**, so the near is not made of blocks: out to 900 m from the skirt at about 2.4 m a texel (2048 square), the rest over the whole at 4.5. They meet along shared vertices with no crack. The paint is the patchwork's own 85 m cells (the same warp and palette as the skirt, so a field carries on across the join), a hedge line between each pair of fields but some left out beyond 450 m so fields come in different sizes, dark blocks for woods (about 3 in 100), pale walls and a brown moor tone above 85 m up, haze (blue, up to 0.4) with distance.
- **Roads and river are ribbons, not paint**, so they run on through the skirt as well: the lane south on towards Haddon from where the ground ends, the A-road both ways, and the river down the valley, with a line of trees along it. Far farms (a house and a barn or two with grey roofs and a few trees, one at most in each 520 m square, never on a slope over 9 m in 45 or near a road or the river) are boxes with gabled roofs in one `farVersion.js` mesh. 5 draw calls and one mesh for the shapes.
- **The camera's far plane is 6000** instead of its own pass. A depth buffer's precision is set by the near plane (0.05), not the far, so a far plane that reaches the rim costs the near view nothing (z squared over near, times 2 to the 24th: the same to the centimetre at 100 m). The issue wanted an own pass for that reason; it would have needed three render sites changed for no gain.
- **Fog.** A stand-in until #820 makes the weather's fog a fraction of the view distance: on the new farm a clear or fair day's fog is 0.36 of what it was (`farFog`), so 1.5 km shows (5% of the sky's colour left there, from nothing), rain and mist are as they were, and the weathers blend smoothly between.
- **Left for #820:** the View distance setting, the budget spots and what shows at 3 km. The farmstead's barns are drawn in full at any distance (not in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`), so their roofs already hold to the view distance; no far swap was built for them.

## View distance (#820)

- **A setting, not a stand-in.** `viewDistance` in the Graphics params (`core/graphics.js`, 1500 m, `[300, 3000, 50]`): how far a clear day shows on the new farm. It is on the Tune tab under Graphics and `tune.graphics.viewDistance` in the console. The old map is unchanged, since a longer view there would show its edge.
- **Every weather is a fraction of it.** `farFog(density, view)` is the weather's old stand-in (0.36 of a clear or fair day's density, rain and mist as they were) times 1500 over the view. A fog's visible range goes as the inverse of its density, so each weather shows in proportion to the setting and keeps its murk relative to it: at 1500 m a clear day shows about 1.56 km (4% of the sky left), overcast about 530 m, rain under 250 m, mist under 70 m; at 3000 m each shows twice that. Weathers still blend by their old densities, so the changeover is as smooth as before.
- **Sky range.** The panel's `fogDensity` slider runs to 0.15, since mist at 300 m is 0.14.
- **Budget spots.** `viewHilltop` and `viewMoorRidge` look east and `viewBackLane` looks south from the lane's north end (`backLaneNorth`, plan 13, 100, on the lane: the hairpins' spot looks at a hedge). Each is counted at 1500 m and at 3000 m and the worse kept (`VIEW_SPOTS`, `VIEW_DISTANCES` in `tools/frameBudget.mjs`). They came out the same at both, as fog changes colour and not what is drawn (the camera's far plane is 6000 and the ring runs to 3.5 km, so nothing is cut at 3000): hilltop 1,203 calls and 2.00M triangles, ridge 1,761 and 2.69M, Back Lane 1,254 and 2.60M, budgets about 8% over. All three are new-farm-only until #692.
- **Looked at** from the hilltop, the ridge and Back Lane's north end at 3000 m on a fair day: no edge of the world shows. The quality presets (#700) and the settings screen (#701) set the value when they land.
- **A grey day closed in (#1097, Jamie: "as recommended").** The ramp that keeps rain and mist as they were started at overcast's own density, so a grey day got the clear day's thinning and showed 906 m, further than a clear day on today's map and not "a few hundred metres". It now runs from 0.004 to 0.0075 (`smooth(0.004, 0.0075, d)`): overcast shows about 530 m at 1500, and clear (1,557 m), fair (1,246 m), drizzle (199 m), rain (150 m) and mist (64 m) are as they were.

## Drag races on the drive (#616)

- **Decided.** On the new farm there is no gravel strip: the races run along the drive's tarmac. `layout.dragStrip` has the same fields as today's (`x`, `z`, `yaw`, `length` 56, `runOff` 8, `width` 10) plus `onDrive`, so `npc/drag.js` and `game.js` read it unchanged. The start line is at plan (632, 345), 14 m in from the yard's gateway, and the run goes east, away from the yard. Today's farm keeps its strip until #692 takes layout 1 away.
- **Fit.** The staging point 11 m behind the line (plan 621) is clear of the yard gateway's apron (2.5 m either side of 618) and the run-off ends at 696, short of the field gateways' aprons (697.5). The two lanes sit 2 m either side of the middle, inside the 6 m of tarmac. The pothole that lay on the run moved from plan x 668 to 716, and the two washes by the field gateways from 700 to 703.5, so the mud and straw stop short of the run-off.
- **Nothing is laid for it.** `gravelStrip(layout)` is today's strip or null; the gravel patch, the level in `terrainHeight.js`, the spawn spots, the cleared grass, the wear exemption and the map's gravel polygon all read that, so they leave the new farm alone. The map keeps its green S and blue F marks. The cones (#559) stand on the verges, `width / 2 + 0.4` (5.4 m) either side of the middle, 1.4 times size. There are no lamps: #559 made none.
- **Traffic.** While a race is being set up or run (`drag.active`), `TrafficHost.dragHold` stops visits starting (`ctx.holdVisits`, also the tuner's call) and puts two `hold` blockers on the drive, 14 m behind the line and 2 m past the run-off. A vehicle waits at a `hold` blocker for as long as it stands, where it edges past a person who is not moving after 40 s (`Drive.held`).
- **Names.** The girls' outing is "the drive" and her stranded spot is the drive's own "on the drive" there. `dragRoute` moved into `npc/drag.js` to be tested.

## Opening the new farm (#692)

- **Decided.** The farm on `docs/farm-plan.md` is the one everybody plays: `LAYOUT` is `layoutNew(...)`, with no `?layout=` switch, no `layout 1` and no "not saved" farm. `layout.js` keeps only the base numbers (`RAW`) that the plan is laid over, and `tests/helpers/todaysLayout.js` resolves them as the old farm for the tests that say how far something moved. `LAYOUT_VERSION` is 2: an old save opens on the new farm with its vehicles, money, the girls, records and the farm's calendar, its positions dropped and the toast from #663 (a test loads the layout-1 fixture).
- **Nothing is left at the old numbers.** `NOT_MOVED_YET` is empty (a test keeps it so) and `REMOVED` names what the plan has no use for: `fieldHalf`, `farm` and the trough. The stone shed stands at the Sheep Field's field-barn spot (plan 378, 451) with its seats, can and parking spot until #624 builds the barn there; the east fields' spawn spots went, the one by the shed came with it.
- **What the old tests were hiding.** About 90 tests read the old farm's numbers; those of pieces with no old counterpart now were deleted (the grass field's gates, the big gate, the two-span barn's loft, the old hill's banks, the east fields), the rest read the new layout. They turned up real faults on the new farm, fixed here: the compressor's hose (9 m) reached neither bay of the bigger barn (now 13 m), the shed catalogue lay on the back half of the bench and the money tin was against the spares rack, and the barn's props went at 25 m in a barn 31 m across (now 35). They also gave numbers to look at, filed as issues: the garden lawn takes about 26 minutes to mow (#1108), the sprayer stands 55 m from the hose (#1109), Low Ley has a brook corner and puddles in it (#1110), 11 pairs of names on the farm map run into each other (`KNOWN_OVERLAPS`, #1111) and the jump's run lies on a 6% slope (#1113).
- **Frame budgets.** Every spot was measured on the build (seed 3, sunny, 15.5) and held to about 8% over: spawn 718 calls and 1.29M triangles, yard 1,815 and 1.94M, village 2,143 and 2.49M, wood 1,486 and 3.46M, hilltop 1,370 and 2.03M, the hairpins 1,507 and 2.04M, the A-road junction (14 m up the lane) 1,173 and 2.13M, the pit's gate 1,634 and 2.03M. The two new spots are `junction` and `pitGate` in `layout.budgetSpots`. Against main on the old farm the five spots read: calls spawn 488 to 717, yard 1,972 to 1,815, village 1,818 to 2,147, wood 1,115 to 1,475, hilltop 1,355 to 1,367; triangles spawn 1.77M to 1.29M, yard 2.76M to 1.94M, village 1.71M to 2.46M, wood 2.64M to 3.40M, hilltop 4.42M to 1.99M.
- **Load time** measured on this Mac's GPU with `tools/perf.mjs`: 11.4 s to Ready, against 3.3 s for main on the old farm in the same session (the last check-up in docs/perf-checks.md recorded 2.49 s). 32.3 MB fetched either way. A frame is slower too: spawn 9.5 ms against 6.7, the yard 13.8 against 11.1, the village 15.8 against 10.7. Not fixed here: #1107.
- **Left for a tidy.** The old layout's fallbacks in `fields.js`, `hill.js`, `woods.js`, `quarry.js`, `terrainHeight.js`, `props.js` (`fieldHalf`, `farm`, the big gate, the grid gate, the two-span barn's trim and loft code) no longer run; they are listed in #1112 to take out. The trees and grass tufts round the yard keep their old half-side as `YARD_HALF` so they stand where they stood.


## Hedges you can't walk into (#829)

- Found on Jamie's walk of Home Ground: on foot you could walk through part of a hedge. Cause, measured from the real models' vertices: the collider was 0.9 m either side of the line, but a hedge block's leaves reached a median 1.29 m (up to 1.77 m) and a bush in it (30% of blocks, 2.3 to 3.9 m tall, no collider) 1.5 to 4.4 m, so you pushed 0.4 to 4 m into leaves before anything stopped you. Blocks were also laid to within 0.4 m of a gateway while the collider stopped at its edge, so leaves and a bush could hang over an open way.
- Agreed with Jamie (option b of three: a collider on every bush, this, or shrinking everything to the old collider): the collider is `HEDGE_SOLID_HALF` = 1.3 m either side of the line, and no leaf is let past it by more than a hand (`HEDGE_LEAF_SLACK`, 0.15 m) or into a gateway (`src/world/hedgeSolid.js`). The model's leaf reach is read from its vertices (`leafReach`), so a block's width is capped (`blockWidth`: at most 1.45 m of leaf reach less the jitter, divided by the model's reach), its jitter across the line is 0.4 m in all (was 0.6), a bush's height is brought down until it fits (`bushHeight`), and blocks and bushes keep a block's reach clear of a gap.
- The farm's own hedges are laid by `layHedge` (`src/world/hedgeLay.js`, pure: blocks, bushes and colliders as numbers) which `props.js` turns into instances; Back Lane's and the A-road's (`layHedges`) and the neighbour's (`neighbourLand.js`) keep their own loops with the same caps and the same solid half-width. The gateways are unchanged, so the girls' routes (`fields.route`) are too. The lanes' verges are 0.4 m narrower where a hedge's box now reaches further.
- `tests/hedgeColliders.test.js` lays every hedge of the farm with the real hedge and bush vertices and fails if a leaf is further out than the box and a hand, if a leaf is in a gateway, if there is a metre of hedge with no collider outside a gap, or a capsule crossing at any point meets a leaf before the box. With the cap off it fails.
- Checked: the unit tests; none of the seven farmstead `tp` places is within 2.2 m of a hedge.

## The Fern Trail's marker (#846)

- Found on Jamie's walk of Hollow Wood (#671): the green board with a white border on two posts, at the trail's start, was too loud for a wood. `buildSign` is now `buildTrailSign` (`src/world/trailProps.js`): one squared oak post, 0.26 by 0.14 m and 1.2 m above the ground, in grey grain with a green damp foot, a four-sided cap and a slight lean.
- The name is routed into the face (`routedPostTexture` in `neighbourTextures.js`: a dark cut with cream paint in it, no border), under the blue band (the way-marker posts' own `POST_BAND`, with the arrow disc on it), over a "Two-wheelers" plate 0.19 m long (`platedTexture`). `MARKER` holds the sizes, and `tests/trailMarkers.test.js` checks the lettering, band and plate sit inside the face, that no faces fight (`coplanarOverlaps`) and that the post is built in `buildWorldModels` as `trailSign`.
- It stands where the sign did (`woods.trail.entrance`, 3 to 4.5 m off the lead-in, so `tp me fern` and the map are unchanged) and faces the lead-in 5 m up towards the gate. Moss grows on its north side, and leaf litter and seven ferns ring the foot, kept off the face.
- Looked at with `tools/shot.mjs --name trailSign`, by day and in autumn, one round and a moss tweak. The post is about 20 small meshes in one group (one marker, so left unmerged); CI's smoke run measured the wood spot at 1,485 calls, level with the 1,486 before it. A recheck then fixed the cap (turned in its geometry, not its mesh), the collider's yaw and `tp me fern` (now 1.5 m in front of the post, `fernMarkerSpot`) (#1127).

## The wood's trees on the map (#847)

- The map drew the wood as a staggered lattice of dots every 6 m over the whole rectangle, so it showed a grid where the world has random oaks in glades. It now draws one dot for each oak the wood dealt.
- The oaks are dealt fresh on each load (`woodsPlants`, with the props' seeded random), so `buildTrees` keeps them on `fields.woods.oaks` (`{ x, z, s }`) and `farmMapFeatures` passes them as `woodsOaks`. The map is built after the world, so they are there; before the wood has dealt them the list is empty, not a lattice.
- A dot is 1.1 m times the oak's scale across in map metres. Nothing extra keeps the Run, the pond and the bogs clear: `treeOk` already does when the oaks are dealt, and the test checks it for the dealt ones.
- About 250 oaks on the new wood; drawn once into the fixed layer.

## The Wet Meadow's veteran oak (#881)

- The Wet Meadow's landmark (#815's list): a stag-headed pollard on the pond's west bank at plan (98, 291), `kind: 'pollardOak'` in `FIELD_LANDMARKS_PLAN`, built by `buildPollardOak` (`landmarkProps.js`) from `fieldOak` with a `pollard` option, so it shares the kit's bark, ivy, burr, hollow, worn earth and fallen limb. Behind `?layout=2` like the rest of the landmarks.
- Scale 1.28 of the field oak: about 18 m tall (the meadow's oaks are 1.1 to 1.7 times the model, so it stands about a quarter over them), a bole 1.4 m across. The top fifth of the crown is left bare: its tips are 3 m grey dead spikes and no leaf clump covers them, so the stag head shows from across the meadow.
- A limb 6.5 m long runs level east over the water at about 3 m, with a collar, a droop at its end, two twigs and leaf clumps. `group.userData.swing` is the point 80% of the way along it, where #147's rope swing hangs. The fallen limb lies on the land side.
- The bank under the limb is kept clear: the pond's reeds are left off a 4.5 m circle there (`hillProps.js`), and the meadow's own oak at offset (-10, -3) from the pond was dropped from `HILL_PLAN.meadow.trees`, as the veteran stands where it did. Trunk collider as the other field oaks (`trunk`), name in `SET_PIECE_NAMES`, `tp me to pollardoak`.
- Looked at with `tools/shot.mjs` from the south-west: the dead spikes and the dome read from 45 m. The tool hides the body of a tree with a group box this small, so it was shot with `--keep` and a placed eye.

## Her upland farmhouse (#910)

- The box of four-square stone with a grid of sashes became `buildFarmhouse` (`neighbourFarmProps.js`), a Pennine house of the 1700s that no house in the village resembles. The main range is 15 by 8.4 m, walls 5 m, ridge 7.9 m (34.6 degrees), the front facing the yard. The layout's `house` gained `wing`, `leanTo` and `garden` (and `door.x` 11 from the middle of the range, `backDoor`, `garden.gate`) on both layouts, so `neighbourFarmShape` gives three footprints and `builtAt`, `covered`, `sightBuildings` and the map follow them; the wing is the low later part at the west end (ridge 5.9 m, newer paler stone) and the lean-to is a scullery at the back whose roof is the main south slope carried on down to 2.5 m (a catslide), cut as three slabs so only the scullery's width is long.
- Walls are one plane each, seen from outside (`wallFace`), because a solid 5 by 8 by 15 m box fails the bare-block test and its hidden faces counted in the surface the triangle floor is judged by. Everything on a wall (`onWall`) runs a few mm into it by its own amount, and the stood-on-ground parts a few mm into the ground, so the many backs and bottoms never share a plane (`tests/farmhouse.test.js` runs `coplanarOverlaps` over the house). Each window picks the lit or the dark glass at random.
- New textures in `neighbourTextures.js`: `oldStoneTexture`, `newStoneTexture`, `dressedStoneTexture` (lintels, mullions, hoods, copings, kneelers, flags), `stoneSlateTexture`, `limewashTexture` (a few big peeled patches, four metres a tile, no repeating foot band: a band showed as stripes), `castIronTexture`, `lawnTexture`, `flowersTexture` and `logStackTexture`. The bench, butt and bins moved from the yard builder into the house's, as the butt now stands under the east downpipe's foot.
- Walked in `tests/farmhouseEntry.test.js` (a capsule from the yard through the garden's gate to the front step, round the back to the scullery door, and stopped by the garden wall elsewhere). The gate is hung open: it was a fixed leaf swung back, no gate to open, as it is the garden's, not a barrier.
- The yard's random stream took 21,188 draws on today's farm (9,873 before): the new textures draw more. The far version gains the wing and the lean-to as two more low boxes (156 triangles, was 124).
- Looked at with `tools/shot.mjs` (placed eyes, as the group's box frames the whole yard): the front with its door and garden, the east gable, the back and the west end. The limewash's first pass read as leopard spots (sixteen small patches, a damp band repeating every two metres), so it is four patches low, two at the eaves and one, with no band.

## Her yard dressed as a working dairy yard (#911)

- **What.** `NEIGHBOUR_PLAN.furniture` (`layoutNew.js`) lists 25 pieces as offsets from her anchor: along the barn's south wall the feed passage (a concrete silage trough, a lick tub on a pallet, a galvanised water trough with its ball cock, a hose on its hook, a yard tap); a round-bale feeder and two wrapped bales torn at the top; the tools, barrow and a scraper blade by the barn door; pallets, an IBC on a stand, lime, sacks and tyres on the strip west of the barn, with a hen coop and run; a cat on the gateway's wall, a churn stand and a name board ("Low Hollins Dairy Farm") by the gate; boots on the scullery's back step and a kennel, its chain and a bowl by her front door; the muck heap with its plank ramp at the south wall; a yard light on a pole and one on the barn's gable. `neighbourYard.js` (pure) gives each kind its footprint (`YARD_KINDS`) and the marks; `neighbourYardProps.js` draws them; `neighbourFarmShape` carries them as `furniture` and `createNeighbourFarm`'s `builtAt` counts them.
- **Kept clear.** `tests/neighbourYard.test.js` searches for a 3 m wide way (the tanker's width) from the gateway to the barn door, the collecting yard's gap and the tank room's door, with every piece as an obstacle, and a person's width to her front door. The pieces hug walls (barn south wall, the strip west of the barn, the gateway's wall) for that reason.
- **Set dressing, not animals.** The hens, the cat and the dog's kennel are static models. `Cat`, `Dog` and `FarmAnimals` are written for our own yard's places and tuning, so a second set would have meant new state machines; none was made.
- **The marks are one decal.** `yardMarks` (pure, from `shape` and a stream) deals wheel tracks from the gateway to the barn door and the tank strip, hoof prints from it to the collecting yard's gap, oil under each vehicle on the concrete (the UTV stands on grass, so it has none), a puddle in the low corner by the gateway side, straw by the barn door and its foot, the loader's scrape along the collecting yard, silage juice under the trough and the drip under the tap. They are painted at 14 px a metre into one transparent plane 6 mm over the slab (`buildYardMarks`), not a mesh a mark. Stains are blobs from a seed, never circles.
- **Lamps.** The yard light's and the gable light's lenses share one emissive material that `setNight` scales (2 at full dark); no light is added to the pool. Looked at with `tools/shot.mjs` at 22:00: both glow.
- **Cost.** The furniture is merged by material with the house and the barn: 24 materials, 12 of them the dairy's own (already merged), so 12 new draw calls and the decal, about 26 with shadows, and about 9,900 triangles. `perf.mjs`' five spots don't reach her yard (they are unchanged within noise); this is counted from the build, not measured at her gate. Her furniture deals from a stream of its own (`createRandom(911)`), so the numbers the rest of the farm takes from `random` don't move (`tests/neighbourMove.test.js`, #969).
- **Held to the standard.** The coplanar test passes over the lot (a dozen overlaps found and moved: trough floors, the IBC's cage bars, bale feeder bars, the lime bag's spill); `yardFurniture` is built in `tests/helpers/worldModels.js` for the bare-block test with nothing added to a `KNOWN_` list; `buildYardMarks` is in `NOT_MODELS` (a painted plane). Two rounds with `tools/shot.mjs --eye`.

## Concrete that looks poured and worn (#912)

- **What.** `concretePaint.js` paints concrete in metres on a tile of `metres` square: noise-edged stains with halos (oil, diesel, a dragged smear), damp lines running to a low corner with a green-black slime stain, curved tread marks, parallel blade scrapes, a swept pale patch, hairline cracks from joint to joint, one patched paler square, grit and joints every 4 m. Nothing is a circle or an oval (a test reads the source and the recorded calls for `ellipse`, `arc` and radial gradients). A mark near an edge is drawn again at the wrapped place, so the tile joins itself.
- **No repeat in sight.** The village and the other props keep `concreteTexture(random)`, a 4 m tile (same API, new look). The neighbour's slab is laid in 16 m cells, each taking one of four different 1024 px tiles by `slabTileAt(i, j)` (no two neighbours alike across or down), merged to four meshes. 64 px a metre; the 4 m tile is 128.
- **Placed wear.** A tile cannot know the gateway, so the polished path from the gate to the barn door and the exposed-aggregate rim are painted into the marks' decal (`paintSlabWear`, 14 px a metre) under the marks.
- **Random stream.** The ramp's and the dairy's concrete draws from its own stream and burns 390 numbers from the farm's, as the old texture took them, so the trees dealt after it stand where they did (#969).
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs` from the gate and the barn door, two rounds: the first had every damp line running to the low corner (90 of them, a dark fan) and halos in hard bands; the count is now about 8 and the halos fainter.

## The concrete's faults from the recheck (#1146)

- **A repeating tile carries only what could be anywhere.** The village's 4 m tile (squeezed to 2.4 m on the village farm's apron) had a patched square, a slime stain, a tread, a smear and damp lines, so they showed about 300 times. It now has the mottling, small stains, grit, cracks and joints. The one-offs are on the 16 m slab tiles only.
- **The drain is placed.** The slime and the damp lines run to the slab's low corner by the puddle, painted by `paintSlabWear` into the marks' decal, not once per 16 m tile.
- **Seams.** A stain's copies share one outline (`raggedFactors` once per stain, reach widened for the rough edge). A slab tile no longer wraps: it is cut at its edge, which is a joint, and joints are half on each side of an edge so they join full width. A test finds the yard's four 16 m meshes with four different maps.
- Corrects the #912 section: its tile "joins itself" only for the 4 m one.

## Her farm quad and tipping trailer (#925)

- **What.** `NEIGHBOUR_PLAN.parked` (`layoutNew.js`) gains a `quad` along the collecting yard's fence, north of the gap the herd comes in by, and a `tipTrailer` beside the barn door, its drawbar to the south. A dairy farm has a quad for the herd and a trailer for feed and bedding; they are scenery with a collider each, like the Tor, UTV and tractor, and lending (#315) will make the quad and the UTV borrowable and nothing else.
- **Built from the farm's own models.** `trafficView.js` `MODELS` gets `quad` (the Farm 300's body, `neighbourVehicles.js` `buildFarmQuad`: chalked green, a plastic crate with bungees on the rear rack, a tartan blanket draped over the seat, dried mud in the footwells) and `tipTrailer` (`buildTipTrailerModel({ farm })`, `tipTrailerFarm.js`: its own rusted galvanised materials, straw with silage stain, a tarp and twine over the front, one drop side let down flat on its chains (the west one, away from the yard; the collider is 0.95 half-width for it) and the other's hinges and catches, the jockey wheel wound down through `addJockeyWheel`'s new `lift`, cracked rear lenses). The player's trailer is untouched: every farm part is behind the `farm` option and its materials are clones.
- **Dealt from the farm seed.** `dealNeighbourVehicles(layout, farmSeed)` adds a `look` to each (how chalked the green, the crate and tarp colours, which of four blankets, how much mud, how rusty, how full of straw) from its own stream, `farmSeed ^ 0x925f`, so no other stream moves; game.js applies it beside `namedLayout`. The look is not kept: it is a pure function of the seed, so it is the same on every load.
- **The trailer's collider is not centred on its origin.** The model's origin is under the axle, 0.38 m behind the middle of the drawbar and tub, so `PARKED_SIZES.tipTrailer` has `cz` and the layout's `x`, `z` are the vehicle's middle: the collider and the footprint are centred there and the model is placed `cz` back along its heading.
- **No oil under the trailer.** `yardMarks` stains the concrete under each engine; a trailer has none.
- **Kept clear.** Both stand outside the 3 m ways to the barn door and the gap (`tests/neighbourYard.test.js` still passes unchanged); `tests/neighbourFarm.test.js` checks nothing overlaps and the colliders are the sizes, `tests/neighbourVehicles.test.js` that each box holds its model.
- **Cost.** Merged by material like the three before them (`mergeParkedVehicle`): the quad is 41 meshes and 60k triangles, the trailer 31 meshes and 14k, against the Tor's 53, the UTV's 59 and the tractor's 31, so each is under any one of the three. None of the frame budget's spots is in her yard, so `node tools/perf.mjs` against main moved no spot's draw calls beyond the noise (the yard 1,234 and 1,234, the village 2,198 and 2,199) and the smoke test's spots are the same within the vehicles' distance trim.
- **Looked at** with `tools/shot.mjs --at` on each, two rounds: the first had the blanket a flat plank over the tank end of the seat and the crate hanging off the rack's back edge; the blanket is now a draped sheet and the crate sits on the rack.

## The neighbour's trailer, fixed after the recheck (#1149)

- The let-down side rests propped 0.45 rad above level, on the mudguard's arc, not flat: the guard stands 0.09 m above the tub floor and flat it cut through the guard and the tyre. It hinges on its knuckles; the let-down side has no catches.
- `addMudguard` copies the material's map as well as its colour, so her guards are the rusted steel and not plain white (the player's have no map, so are unchanged).
- The coplanar test builds her trailer with the let-down side (and the straw heap is narrower than the straw so their faces share no plane). A test builds the yard with a recording `fixedBox` and checks each collider's centre and that the model is inside it, which the earlier shape-against-sizes case could not fail.


## The hill's edge no longer drops 15 m to Back Lane (#945)

- **What was wrong.** The hill's profile is 15.7 m above the land's lie at the pasture's hedges. Today's ground rises 22 m beyond its hedges and holds that edge up; the new farm's landform has no such rise, so the 40 m fade ended as a bank: 33 to 47 degrees between the south hedge and Back Lane (z 487 to 525, x about -470 to -110) and 26 to 33 beyond the east hedge, with Back Lane laid level across the middle of the south one. The issue was written when the hill was still at today's numbers; #684 moved it and the bank moved with it.
- **The fix.** `hill.weight(x, z, fade)` and `westCut(x, z, fade)` take the fade length (40 m by default, so today's layout and every other reader are unchanged) and `natural()` asks for `HILL_FADE_ON_LANDFORM`, 160 m, when the layout has a landform. The stream's west cut takes the same length, or the channel there is cut twice over (the moor's steepness test caught it).
- **Numbers.** Steepest metre on the ground, hedge to Back Lane, at x -460, -380, -273 and -200: 27, 26, 33 and 26 degrees at 90 m, 16, 15, 23 and 15 at 130, 11, 8, 17 and 7 at 180; 160 m, as built after #1172 (blended in over 30 m past the hedges), gives 17, 20, 14 and 20, all under the cap. The cap is 21 degrees, today's hill fade at its worst measured on `resolveLayout(RAW_LAYOUT)`.
- **Test.** `tests/landform.test.js` checks the natural ground over the whole 5 m grid on both axes (the old test compared the layout with itself) and the finished ground along the south and east edges. Left out because they are cut on purpose: the pit, the wood's and the hill's own ground inside their hedges, and everything west of x -480 (the hill's west edge, the stream to Back Lane's ford, the moor).

## The long hill fade is for the south and east only (#1172)

- **What was wrong.** #945 widened the hill's fade on every side. On the west the hill's banks run along z, so they carried on 160 m out at partial weight: the ground at x -540 to -520, z 262 to 330 climbed about 25 degrees along z and Back Lane's centreline beside the west hedge up to 32 (19.4 at 40 m). The whole-ground test skipped `x < -480` as "shaped on purpose" and hid it; the recheck found it.
- **The fix.** `hill.weight(x, z, fade)` blends the long fade in over 30 m past the pasture's south (`maxZ`) and east (`maxX`) hedges, fades it out again over 30 m west of the west hedge (Back Lane's south-west hairpin cuts 1.8 m into a bank otherwise, which `tests/backLane.test.js` refuses) and keeps 40 m everywhere else; `westCut` follows it. `createTerrainHeight` takes `{ hillFade }` so a test can build the same farm at 40 m and compare.
- **Tests.** West of the pasture, over its whole length, no metre is steeper than it is at 40 m; Back Lane's centreline at x -510 from z 207 to 335 is at most 21 degrees (19.4, the same as at 40 m). Both fail with the long fade restored. The whole-ground test now leaves out west of the pasture by that name, not by a guess. The west edge itself is steep at 40 m too (22 to 35 degrees from the hedge out); that is old and not this fix's.

## The meadow's and the wood's ponds sit level on the lie (#946)

- **What was wrong.** A pond's water is a flat sheet at the ground's height at its middle, but its bowl was cut relative to the local ground, and the new farm's lie falls 3.7 degrees (about 12 degrees at these ponds, with the meadow's and the wood's own profile). The downhill shore dropped away under the sheet and the uphill one stood over it. Probed at the rim before the change: water above the ground by up to 0.74 m at the meadow pond (-408, -73) and 0.45 m at the wood's (-437, -242); on the uphill side the ground stood over the water by about as much.
- **The fix.** `heightAt` in `terrainHeight.js` levels the ground under each pond to the lie at its middle (the hut's terrace does the same), over the pond's radius and eased back to the lie by 2.5 radii (`PONDS_EASE_TO`), then the bowl is cut as before. Only on the new farm (`landform`); the pre-cut level is found once, lazily, from `natural`.
- **Numbers.** Rim water minus ground is now 0.03 m all round at both ponds (the rim is taken at 0.97 of the wet radius, so it stands 3 cm under the water).
- **Tests.** `tests/landform.test.js`: the rim is within 5 cm of the ground at 48 bearings, no ground stands over wet points and none lies under the water on the dry side, for both ponds. All four fail with the levelling removed. The game has only layout 2 now, so there is no layout 1 run; the issue's coordinates (-66 110, -42 -150) were the old frame, the ponds are at the ones above.

## The ponds' shoulder made longer (#1184)

- **What the recheck found.** The shoulder in #946 eased back over 1.7 radii, but the ground there falls about 12 degrees, so levelling moved it by up to 1.5 m: the meadow's steepest ground between 1 and 2.6 radii went from 12.3 to 24.7 degrees, and the Wet Meadow's pollard oak (1.28 radii out on the uphill bank) dropped 1.18 m, leaving its foot flare 0.10 m clear of the ground.
- **The fix.** `PONDS_EASE_TO` is 2.5: steepest 15.7 degrees at the meadow, and the oak's foot is buried again. 2.2 gave 18.4. The wood's steep banks at 1.8 to 2.6 radii (33 to 37 degrees) were there before #946.
- **Tests.** The meadow's shoulder is under 20 degrees, and the oak's foot ring falls under its 0.25 m sink; both fail at 1.7.

## One test for things in the wrong place (#1187)

- **The list.** `farmPlacements` (`src/world/farmPlacement.js`, pure) returns 12,191 things on the new farm, each with its kind, middle, footprint and whether it is solid, read from what the builders read: `layHedge` for the farm's hedges block by block, and the same for Back Lane's and the A-road's (`layLaneHedge`, pulled out of backLaneProps.js), the oaks and far clumps (`farmTreeSpots`, `distantTreeSpots`, pulled out of props.js), the shape modules and the layout's places, with each builder's own random stream (`propStreams.js`). A test holds `PLACEMENT_SOURCES` to the builders `buildProps` runs. What builders work out only inside their drawing code is not listed yet, named builder by builder in `PLACEMENT_SOURCES.partly` and `notYet` (the yard's kit, the garden wall, the village's furniture, the Moor's stile and pillar, the pit's plant, the lanes' hedgerow trees and a few more).
- **The rules,** each `(thing, ctx) => why or null`, asked together by `placementBreaks`: nothing solid and no hedge leaves in a gateway's opening (the gap and 1.3 m either side of the line; Back Lane's gaps too); nothing in the stream, the river or a pond; nothing solid on a road's carriageway (the tarmac and the tracks); what belongs to a field or plot inside it; nothing on a footpath's line, a crossing, a trough's or a pole's place, the moor gate or a kept-clear rectangle but what it is kept for. A touch of 5 cm is allowed.
- **Found, the first run:** gateway 39, water 5, track 7, plot 0, reserved 2.
- **Fixed here.** Gateway 37: Back Lane's hedge at its gates, ford and bridle gates, 21 leaf blocks past a run's end (now left out, so #1122 is closed) and 16 colliders reaching 0.2 m into the gap (they now end at the run's ends). Water 1, track 5, by moving, in plan metres: Back Lane's point (10, 560) to (11, 545), as the road looped back on itself just before the ford and its hedge's colliders lay across it (the cuttings move 7 m back along with it, and every road is now tested never to double back); the lane's grit bin (905, -2) to (906, -5), as it stood 0.4 m on Back Lane's tarmac at its mouth; Hollow Wood's branch (108, 60) to (110, 56), off the boundary track; the boundary track's corner (196, 502) to (197, 501), so its edge clears the Far Field gateway's hedge; the brook alders from 0.08 to 0.05 along their curve, so the first, at (671, 607), no longer stands in the river.
- **Filed,** in `KNOWN` by number: #1216 (gateway 2, water 4: hedge corners at the ford and where the brook leaves the farm), #1217 (track 1, reserved 2: 48 of the old farm's 55 oaks still stand across the farmstead) and #1082 (track 1, the lane gate across the small farm's track).
- **Time:** the whole farm is listed and checked in about 0.6 s, the file in under 1 s on its own.
